```
1→"""Scoring primitives for holistic review batch merges."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.scoring_constants import CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS
     9→
    10→_IMPACT_SCOPE_WEIGHTS = {
    11→    "local": 1.0,
    12→    "module": 1.3,
    13→    "subsystem": 1.6,
    14→    "codebase": 2.0,
    15→}
    16→_FIX_SCOPE_WEIGHTS = {
    17→    "single_edit": 1.0,
    18→    "multi_file_refactor": 1.3,
    19→    "architectural_change": 1.7,
    20→}
    21→
    22→# Blending ratio between weighted mean and per-batch floor score.
    23→_WEIGHTED_MEAN_BLEND = 0.7
    24→_FLOOR_BLEND_WEIGHT = 0.3
    25→
    26→# Maximum total penalty that issues can impose on a dimension score.
    27→_MAX_ISSUE_PENALTY = 24.0
    28→
    29→# Per-unit penalty from cumulative issue severity.
    30→_PRESSURE_PENALTY_MULTIPLIER = 2.2
    31→
    32→# Extra penalty per additional issue beyond the first.
    33→_EXTRA_ISSUE_PENALTY = 0.8
    34→
    35→# Issues-based score cap parameters.
    36→_CAP_FLOOR = 60.0
    37→_CAP_CEILING = 90.0
    38→_CAP_PRESSURE_MULTIPLIER = 3.5
    39→
    40→
    41→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    42→class ScoreInputs:
    43→    """Normalized inputs for a single dimension merge computation."""
    44→
    45→    weighted_mean: float
    46→    floor: float
    47→    issue_pressure: float
    48→    issue_count: int
    49→
    50→
    51→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    52→class ScoreBreakdown:
    53→    """Named intermediate values for one merged dimension score."""
    54→
    55→    weighted_mean: float
    56→    floor: float
    57→    floor_aware: float
    58→    issue_penalty: float
    59→    issue_cap: float | None
    60→    final_score: float
    61→
    62→
    63→class DimensionMergeScorer:
    64→    """Compute pressure-adjusted merged scores for holistic review dimensions."""
    65→
    66→    def issue_severity(
    67→        self,
    68→        issue: dict[str, Any],
    69→        *,
    70→        note: dict[str, Any] | None,
    71→    ) -> float:
    72→        """Compute per-issue severity used for score-pressure adjustments."""
    73→        note_ref = note if isinstance(note, dict) else {}
    74→        confidence = str(
    75→            issue.get("confidence", note_ref.get("confidence", "medium"))
    76→        ).strip().lower()
    77→        impact_scope = str(
    78→            issue.get("impact_scope", note_ref.get("impact_scope", "local"))
    79→        ).strip().lower()
    80→        fix_scope = str(
    81→            issue.get("fix_scope", note_ref.get("fix_scope", "single_edit"))
    82→        ).strip().lower()
    83→
    84→        confidence_weight = CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(confidence, 1.0)
    85→        impact_weight = _IMPACT_SCOPE_WEIGHTS.get(impact_scope, 1.0)
    86→        fix_weight = _FIX_SCOPE_WEIGHTS.get(fix_scope, 1.0)
    87→        return confidence_weight * impact_weight * fix_weight
    88→
    89→    def issue_pressure_by_dimension(
    90→        self,
    91→        issues: list[dict[str, Any]],
    92→        *,
    93→        dimension_notes: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
    94→    ) -> tuple[dict[str, float], dict[str, int]]:
    95→        """Summarize how strongly issues should pull dimension scores down."""
    96→        pressure_by_dim: dict[str, float] = {}
    97→        count_by_dim: dict[str, int] = {}
    98→        for issue in issues:
    99→            dim = str(issue.get("dimension", "")).strip()
   100→            if not dim:
   101→                continue
   102→            note = dimension_notes.get(dim)
   103→            pressure_by_dim[dim] = pressure_by_dim.get(dim, 0.0) + self.issue_severity(
   104→                issue,
   105→                note=note if isinstance(note, dict) else None,
   106→            )
   107→            count_by_dim[dim] = count_by_dim.get(dim, 0) + 1
   108→        return pressure_by_dim, count_by_dim
   109→
   110→    def score_dimension(self, inputs: ScoreInputs) -> ScoreBreakdown:
   111→        """Compute one merged score with explicit intermediate values."""
   112→        floor_aware = (
   113→            _WEIGHTED_MEAN_BLEND * inputs.weighted_mean
   114→            + _FLOOR_BLEND_WEIGHT * inputs.floor
   115→        )
   116→        issue_penalty = min(
   117→            _MAX_ISSUE_PENALTY,
   118→            (inputs.issue_pressure * _PRESSURE_PENALTY_MULTIPLIER)
   119→            + (max(inputs.issue_count - 1, 0) * _EXTRA_ISSUE_PENALTY),
   120→        )
   121→        issue_adjusted = floor_aware - issue_penalty
   122→
   123→        issue_cap: float | None = None
   124→        if inputs.issue_count > 0:
   125→            cap_penalty = (
   126→                (inputs.issue_pressure * _CAP_PRESSURE_MULTIPLIER)
   127→                + (max(inputs.issue_count - 1, 0) * _EXTRA_ISSUE_PENALTY)
   128→            )
   129→            issue_cap = max(
   130→                _CAP_FLOOR,
   131→                _CAP_CEILING - cap_penalty,
   132→            )
   133→            issue_adjusted = min(issue_adjusted, issue_cap)
   134→
   135→        final_score = round(max(0.0, min(100.0, issue_adjusted)), 1)
   136→        return ScoreBreakdown(
   137→            weighted_mean=inputs.weighted_mean,
   138→            floor=inputs.floor,
   139→            floor_aware=floor_aware,
   140→            issue_penalty=issue_penalty,
   141→            issue_cap=issue_cap,
   142→            final_score=final_score,
   143→        )
   144→
   145→    def merge_scores(
   146→        self,
   147→        score_buckets: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]],
   148→        score_raw_by_dim: dict[str, list[float]],
   149→        issue_pressure_by_dim: dict[str, float],
   150→        issue_count_by_dim: dict[str, int],
   151→    ) -> dict[str, float]:
   152→        """Compute pressure-adjusted weighted mean for each dimension."""
   153→        merged: dict[str, float] = {}
   154→        for key, weighted_scores in sorted(score_buckets.items()):
   155→            if not weighted_scores:
   156→                continue
   157→            numerator = sum(score * weight for score, weight in weighted_scores)
   158→            denominator = sum(weight for _, weight in weighted_scores)
   159→            weighted_mean = numerator / max(denominator, 1.0)
   160→            floor = min(score_raw_by_dim.get(key, [weighted_mean]))
   161→            breakdown = self.score_dimension(
   162→                ScoreInputs(
   163→                    weighted_mean=weighted_mean,
   164→                    floor=floor,
   165→                    issue_pressure=issue_pressure_by_dim.get(key, 0.0),
   166→                    issue_count=issue_count_by_dim.get(key, 0),
   167→                )
   168→            )
   169→            merged[key] = breakdown.final_score
   170→        return merged
   171→
   172→
   173→__all__ = ["DimensionMergeScorer", "ScoreBreakdown", "ScoreInputs"]
   174→
```

```
1→"""State suppression accounting helpers.
     2→
     3→Scoring recomputation lives in :mod:`desloppify.engine._scoring.state_integration`.
     4→"""
     5→
     6→from __future__ import annotations
     7→
     8→__all__ = [
     9→    "suppression_metrics",
    10→]
    11→
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    13→
    14→_SUPPRESSION_FIELDS = frozenset({"ignored", "raw_issues", "suppressed_pct", "ignore_patterns"})
    15→
    16→
    17→def _has_suppression_fields(finding: dict) -> bool:
    18→    """Check if a scan-history entry has any suppression-related fields."""
    19→    return isinstance(finding, dict) and bool(_SUPPRESSION_FIELDS & finding.keys())
    20→
    21→
    22→def _clamped_pct(value: float) -> float:
    23→    """Clamp a percentage to [0, 100] and round to one decimal."""
    24→    return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, value)), 1)
    25→
    26→
    27→def _last_suppressed_pct(finding: dict) -> float:
    28→    """Extract the last suppressed percentage from a scan-history entry."""
    29→    if "suppressed_pct" in finding:
    30→        return _clamped_pct(float(finding.get("suppressed_pct") or 0.0))
    31→    ignored = int(finding.get("ignored", 0) or 0)
    32→    raw = int(finding.get("raw_issues", 0) or 0)
    33→    return _clamped_pct(ignored / raw * 100) if raw else 0.0
    34→
    35→
    36→def _empty_suppression_metrics() -> dict[str, int | float]:
    37→    return {
    38→        "last_ignored": 0,
    39→        "last_raw_issues": 0,
    40→        "last_suppressed_pct": 0.0,
    41→        "last_ignore_patterns": 0,
    42→        "recent_scans": 0,
    43→        "recent_ignored": 0,
    44→        "recent_raw_issues": 0,
    45→        "recent_suppressed_pct": 0.0,
    46→    }
    47→
    48→
    49→def suppression_metrics(state: StateModel, *, window: int = 5) -> dict[str, int | float]:
    50→    """Summarize ignore suppression from recent scan history."""
    51→    history = state.get("scan_history", [])
    52→    if not history:
    53→        return _empty_suppression_metrics()
    54→
    55→    scans_with_suppression = [
    56→        entry for entry in history if _has_suppression_fields(entry)
    57→    ]
    58→    if not scans_with_suppression:
    59→        return _empty_suppression_metrics()
    60→
    61→    recent = scans_with_suppression[-max(1, window) :]
    62→    last = recent[-1]
    63→
    64→    recent_ignored = sum(int(entry.get("ignored", 0) or 0) for entry in recent)
    65→    recent_raw = sum(int(entry.get("raw_issues", 0) or 0) for entry in recent)
    66→    recent_pct = _clamped_pct(recent_ignored / recent_raw * 100) if recent_raw else 0.0
    67→
    68→    last_ignored = int(last.get("ignored", 0) or 0)
    69→    last_raw = int(last.get("raw_issues", 0) or 0)
    70→
    71→    return {
    72→        "last_ignored": last_ignored,
    73→        "last_raw_issues": last_raw,
    74→        "last_suppressed_pct": _last_suppressed_pct(last),
    75→        "last_ignore_patterns": int(last.get("ignore_patterns", 0) or 0),
    76→        "recent_scans": len(recent),
    77→        "recent_ignored": recent_ignored,
    78→        "recent_raw_issues": recent_raw,
    79→        "recent_suppressed_pct": recent_pct,
    80→    }
    81→
```

> DEVELOPER

Research the Go unused-import detector in desloppify. Look for:
1. The Go language plugin directory structure
2. The unused-import detector for Go
3. How it determines if an import is "used" - does it scan for the package name in the source?
4. Specifically, does it handle interface method signatures, generic type parameters, and struct field types?

The bug is that imports used only in type declarations (interface signatures, struct fields, generic params) are falsely flagged as unused.

Search in desloppify/languages/ for go-related code. Be very thorough.

```
1→"""Shared subjective follow-up rendering helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Protocol
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     8→
     9→
    10→class SubjectiveFollowup(Protocol):
    11→    """Contract for objects passed to print_subjective_followup."""
    12→
    13→    low_assessed: object  # falsy when no dimensions are below threshold
    14→    threshold_label: str
    15→    rendered: str
    16→    command: str
    17→    integrity_lines: list[tuple[str, str]]
    18→
    19→
    20→def print_subjective_followup(
    21→    followup: SubjectiveFollowup,
    22→    *,
    23→    leading_newline: bool = False,
    24→    objective_backlog: int = 0,
    25→) -> bool:
    26→    """Render common subjective quality/integrity guidance lines.
    27→
    28→    When *objective_backlog* > 0, quality nudges are suppressed entirely — the
    29→    queue already surfaces subjective work when it becomes actionable.
    30→    Integrity warnings are always shown (anti-gaming safeguard).
    31→
    32→    Returns True when any line is rendered.
    33→    """
    34→    printed = False
    35→    prefix = "\n" if leading_newline else ""
    36→    if followup.low_assessed and objective_backlog <= 0:
    37→        print(
    38→            colorize(
    39→                f"{prefix}  Subjective quality (<{followup.threshold_label}%): "
    40→                f"{followup.rendered}",
    41→                "cyan",
    42→            )
    43→        )
    44→        print(
    45→            colorize(
    46→                f"  Next command to improve subjective scores: {followup.command}",
    47→                "dim",
    48→            )
    49→        )
    50→        printed = True
    51→
    52→    if followup.integrity_lines:
    53→        if printed:
    54→            print()
    55→        for style, message in followup.integrity_lines:
    56→            print(colorize(f"  {message}", style))
    57→        printed = True
    58→    return printed
    59→
    60→
    61→__all__ = ["print_subjective_followup"]
    62→
```

```
1→"""Merge-support scoring and issue-key helpers for batch review results."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.intelligence.review.feedback_contract import (
     6→    LEGACY_REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY,
     7→    REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY,
     8→)
     9→from desloppify.intelligence.review.issue_merge import (
    10→    normalize_word_set,
    11→)
    12→
    13→from .core_models import (
    14→    BatchDimensionNotePayload,
    15→    BatchIssuePayload,
    16→    BatchResultPayload,
    17→)
    18→from .scoring import DimensionMergeScorer
    19→
    20→_DIMENSION_SCORER = DimensionMergeScorer()
    21→
    22→
    23→def assessment_weight(
    24→    *,
    25→    dimension: str,
    26→    issues: list[BatchIssuePayload],
    27→    dimension_notes: dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload],
    28→) -> float:
    29→    """Evidence-weighted assessment score weight with a neutral floor.
    30→
    31→    Weighting is evidence-based and score-independent: the raw score does not
    32→    influence how much weight a batch contributes during merge.
    33→    """
    34→    note = dimension_notes.get(dimension, {})
    35→    note_evidence = len(note.get("evidence", [])) if isinstance(note, dict) else 0
    36→    issue_count = sum(
    37→        1 for issue in issues if issue["dimension"].strip() == dimension
    38→    )
    39→    return float(1 + note_evidence + issue_count)
    40→
    41→
    42→def _issue_pressure_by_dimension(
    43→    issues: list[BatchIssuePayload],
    44→    *,
    45→    dimension_notes: dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload],
    46→) -> tuple[dict[str, float], dict[str, int]]:
    47→    """Summarize how strongly issues should pull dimension scores down."""
    48→    return _DIMENSION_SCORER.issue_pressure_by_dimension(
    49→        issues,
    50→        dimension_notes=dimension_notes,
    51→    )
    52→
    53→
    54→def _accumulate_batch_scores(
    55→    result: BatchResultPayload,
    56→    *,
    57→    score_buckets: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]],
    58→    score_raw_by_dim: dict[str, list[float]],
    59→    merged_dimension_notes: dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload],
    60→    abstraction_axis_scores: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]],
    61→    abstraction_sub_axes: tuple[str, ...],
    62→) -> None:
    63→    """Accumulate assessment scores, dimension notes, and sub-axis data from one batch."""
    64→    result_issues = result["issues"]
    65→    result_notes = result["dimension_notes"]
    66→    for key, score in result["assessments"].items():
    67→        if isinstance(score, bool):
    68→            continue
    69→        score_value = float(score)
    70→        weight = assessment_weight(
    71→            dimension=key,
    72→            issues=result_issues,
    73→            dimension_notes=result_notes,
    74→        )
    75→        score_buckets.setdefault(key, []).append((score_value, weight))
    76→        score_raw_by_dim.setdefault(key, []).append(score_value)
    77→
    78→        note = result_notes.get(key)
    79→        existing = merged_dimension_notes.get(key)
    80→        existing_evidence = (
    81→            len(existing.get("evidence", [])) if isinstance(existing, dict) else -1
    82→        )
    83→        current_evidence = (
    84→            len(note.get("evidence", [])) if isinstance(note, dict) else -1
    85→        )
    86→        if current_evidence > existing_evidence and note is not None:
    87→            merged_dimension_notes[key] = note
    88→
    89→        if key == "abstraction_fitness" and isinstance(note, dict):
    90→            sub_axes = note.get("sub_axes")
    91→            if isinstance(sub_axes, dict):
    92→                for axis in abstraction_sub_axes:
    93→                    axis_score = sub_axes.get(axis)
    94→                    if isinstance(axis_score, bool) or not isinstance(
    95→                        axis_score, int | float
    96→                    ):
    97→                        continue
    98→                    abstraction_axis_scores[axis].append(
    99→                        (float(axis_score), weight)
   100→                    )
   101→
   102→
   103→def _issue_identity_key(issue: BatchIssuePayload) -> str:
   104→    """Build a stable concept key; prefer dimension+identifier when available."""
   105→    dim = issue["dimension"].strip()
   106→    ident = issue["identifier"].strip()
   107→    if ident:
   108→        return f"{dim}::{ident}"
   109→    summary = issue["summary"].strip()
   110→    summary_terms = sorted(normalize_word_set(summary))
   111→    if summary_terms:
   112→        return f"{dim}::summary::{','.join(summary_terms[:8])}"
   113→    return f"{dim}::{summary}"
   114→
   115→
   116→def _accumulate_batch_quality(
   117→    result: BatchResultPayload,
   118→    *,
   119→    coverage_values: list[float],
   120→    evidence_density_values: list[float],
   121→) -> float:
   122→    """Accumulate quality metrics from one batch. Returns high-score-missing-issues delta."""
   123→    quality: object = result["quality"]
   124→    if not isinstance(quality, dict):
   125→        return 0.0
   126→    coverage = quality.get("dimension_coverage")
   127→    density = quality.get("evidence_density")
   128→    missing_issue_note = quality.get(REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY)
   129→    if not isinstance(missing_issue_note, int | float):
   130→        missing_issue_note = quality.get(
   131→            LEGACY_REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY
   132→        )
   133→    if isinstance(coverage, int | float):
   134→        coverage_values.append(float(coverage))
   135→    if isinstance(density, int | float):
   136→        evidence_density_values.append(float(density))
   137→    return (
   138→        float(missing_issue_note)
   139→        if isinstance(missing_issue_note, int | float)
   140→        else 0.0
   141→    )
   142→
   143→
   144→def _compute_merged_assessments(
   145→    score_buckets: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]],
   146→    score_raw_by_dim: dict[str, list[float]],
   147→    issue_pressure_by_dim: dict[str, float],
   148→    issue_count_by_dim: dict[str, int],
   149→) -> dict[str, float]:
   150→    """Compute pressure-adjusted weighted mean for each dimension."""
   151→    return _DIMENSION_SCORER.merge_scores(
   152→        score_buckets,
   153→        score_raw_by_dim,
   154→        issue_pressure_by_dim,
   155→        issue_count_by_dim,
   156→    )
   157→
   158→
   159→def _compute_abstraction_components(
   160→    merged_assessments: dict[str, float],
   161→    abstraction_axis_scores: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]],
   162→    *,
   163→    abstraction_sub_axes: tuple[str, ...],
   164→    abstraction_component_names: dict[str, str],
   165→) -> dict[str, float] | None:
   166→    """Compute weighted abstraction sub-axis component scores."""
   167→    abstraction_score = merged_assessments.get("abstraction_fitness")
   168→    if abstraction_score is None:
   169→        return None
   170→
   171→    component_scores: dict[str, float] = {}
   172→    for axis in abstraction_sub_axes:
   173→        weighted = abstraction_axis_scores.get(axis, [])
   174→        if not weighted:
   175→            continue
   176→        numerator = sum(score * weight for score, weight in weighted)
   177→        denominator = sum(weight for _, weight in weighted)
   178→        if denominator <= 0:
   179→            continue
   180→        component_scores[abstraction_component_names[axis]] = round(
   181→            max(0.0, min(100.0, numerator / denominator)),
   182→            1,
   183→        )
   184→    return component_scores if component_scores else None
   185→
   186→
   187→__all__ = [
   188→    "assessment_weight",
   189→    "_accumulate_batch_quality",
   190→    "_accumulate_batch_scores",
   191→    "_compute_abstraction_components",
   192→    "_compute_merged_assessments",
   193→    "_issue_identity_key",
   194→    "_issue_pressure_by_dimension",
   195→]
   196→
```

```
1→"""Merge and dedupe logic for holistic review batch outputs."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import cast
     6→
     7→from desloppify.intelligence.review.feedback_contract import (
     8→    REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY,
     9→)
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.review.issue_merge import (
    11→    merge_list_fields,
    12→    normalize_word_set,
    13→    pick_longer_text,
    14→    track_merged_from,
    15→)
    16→
    17→from .core_merge_support import (
    18→    _accumulate_batch_quality,
    19→    _accumulate_batch_scores,
    20→    _compute_abstraction_components,
    21→    _compute_merged_assessments,
    22→    _issue_identity_key,
    23→    _issue_pressure_by_dimension,
    24→    assessment_weight,
    25→)
    26→from .core_models import (
    27→    BatchDimensionJudgmentPayload,
    28→    BatchDimensionNotePayload,
    29→    BatchIssuePayload,
    30→    BatchResultPayload,
    31→)
    32→
    33→
    34→def _merge_issue_payload(
    35→    existing: BatchIssuePayload,
    36→    incoming: BatchIssuePayload,
    37→) -> None:
    38→    merge_list_fields(existing, incoming, ("related_files", "evidence"))
    39→    pick_longer_text(existing, incoming, "summary")
    40→    pick_longer_text(existing, incoming, "suggestion")
    41→    track_merged_from(existing, str(incoming.get("identifier", "")).strip())
    42→
    43→
    44→def _should_merge_issues(
    45→    existing: BatchIssuePayload,
    46→    incoming: BatchIssuePayload,
    47→) -> bool:
    48→    existing_summary = normalize_word_set(str(existing.get("summary", "")))
    49→    incoming_summary = normalize_word_set(str(incoming.get("summary", "")))
    50→    summary_similarity_signal = False
    51→    if existing_summary and incoming_summary:
    52→        overlap = len(existing_summary & incoming_summary)
    53→        union = len(existing_summary | incoming_summary)
    54→        summary_similarity_signal = bool(union and overlap / union >= 0.45)
    55→
    56→    existing_files = set(existing.get("related_files", []))
    57→    incoming_files = set(incoming.get("related_files", []))
    58→    file_overlap_signal = bool(existing_files and incoming_files and (existing_files & incoming_files))
    59→
    60→    existing_identifier = str(existing.get("identifier", "")).strip()
    61→    incoming_identifier = str(incoming.get("identifier", "")).strip()
    62→    identifier_signal = bool(
    63→        existing_identifier and incoming_identifier and existing_identifier == incoming_identifier
    64→    )
    65→
    66→    corroborating_signals = (
    67→        int(summary_similarity_signal)
    68→        + int(file_overlap_signal)
    69→        + int(identifier_signal)
    70→    )
    71→    if identifier_signal and (summary_similarity_signal or file_overlap_signal):
    72→        return True
    73→    return corroborating_signals >= 2
    74→
    75→
    76→def _append_batch_issues(
    77→    result: BatchResultPayload,
    78→    issues: list[BatchIssuePayload],
    79→) -> None:
    80→    for issue in result.get("issues", []):
    81→        if isinstance(issue, dict):
    82→            issues.append(cast(BatchIssuePayload, issue))
    83→
    84→
    85→def _merge_issue_group(group: list[BatchIssuePayload]) -> list[BatchIssuePayload]:
    86→    """Merge one dedupe-key group using transitive connected components."""
    87→    if len(group) <= 1:
    88→        return list(group)
    89→
    90→    visited: set[int] = set()
    91→    components: list[list[int]] = []
    92→
    93→    for start in range(len(group)):
    94→        if start in visited:
    95→            continue
    96→        stack = [start]
    97→        component: list[int] = []
    98→        visited.add(start)
    99→        while stack:
   100→            node = stack.pop()
   101→            component.append(node)
   102→            source = group[node]
   103→            for probe in range(len(group)):
   104→                if probe in visited:
   105→                    continue
   106→                target = group[probe]
   107→                if _should_merge_issues(source, target) or _should_merge_issues(
   108→                    target, source
   109→                ):
   110→                    visited.add(probe)
   111→                    stack.append(probe)
   112→        components.append(sorted(component))
   113→
   114→    merged_components: list[BatchIssuePayload] = []
   115→    for indexes in sorted(components, key=lambda ids: ids[0]):
   116→        base = group[indexes[0]]
   117→        for idx in indexes[1:]:
   118→            _merge_issue_payload(base, group[idx])
   119→        merged_components.append(base)
   120→    return merged_components
   121→
   122→
   123→def _merge_issues_transitively(
   124→    issues: list[BatchIssuePayload],
   125→) -> list[BatchIssuePayload]:
   126→    grouped: dict[str, list[BatchIssuePayload]] = {}
   127→    for issue in issues:
   128→        grouped.setdefault(_issue_identity_key(issue), []).append(issue)
   129→
   130→    merged: list[BatchIssuePayload] = []
   131→    for group in grouped.values():
   132→        merged.extend(_merge_issue_group(group))
   133→    return merged
   134→
   135→
   136→def merge_batch_results(
   137→    batch_results: list[BatchResultPayload],
   138→    *,
   139→    abstraction_sub_axes: tuple[str, ...],
   140→    abstraction_component_names: dict[str, str],
   141→) -> dict[str, object]:
   142→    """Deterministically merge assessments/issues across batch outputs."""
   143→    score_buckets: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]] = {}
   144→    score_raw_by_dim: dict[str, list[float]] = {}
   145→    all_issues: list[BatchIssuePayload] = []
   146→    merged_dimension_notes: dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload] = {}
   147→    merged_dimension_judgment: dict[str, BatchDimensionJudgmentPayload] = {}
   148→    coverage_values: list[float] = []
   149→    evidence_density_values: list[float] = []
   150→    high_score_missing_issue_note_total = 0.0
   151→    abstraction_axis_scores: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]] = {
   152→        axis: [] for axis in abstraction_sub_axes
   153→    }
   154→
   155→    for result in batch_results:
   156→        _accumulate_batch_scores(
   157→            result,
   158→            score_buckets=score_buckets,
   159→            score_raw_by_dim=score_raw_by_dim,
   160→            merged_dimension_notes=merged_dimension_notes,
   161→            abstraction_axis_scores=abstraction_axis_scores,
   162→            abstraction_sub_axes=abstraction_sub_axes,
   163→        )
   164→        _append_batch_issues(result, all_issues)
   165→        high_score_missing_issue_note_total += _accumulate_batch_quality(
   166→            result,
   167→            coverage_values=coverage_values,
   168→            evidence_density_values=evidence_density_values,
   169→        )
   170→        # Collect dimension_judgment — each batch covers one dimension, no conflicts
   171→        for dim_key, judgment in result.get("dimension_judgment", {}).items():
   172→            if isinstance(judgment, dict) and dim_key not in merged_dimension_judgment:
   173→                merged_dimension_judgment[dim_key] = cast(BatchDimensionJudgmentPayload, judgment)
   174→
   175→    merged_issues = _merge_issues_transitively(all_issues)
   176→    issue_pressure_by_dim, issue_count_by_dim = _issue_pressure_by_dimension(
   177→        merged_issues,
   178→        dimension_notes=merged_dimension_notes,
   179→    )
   180→
   181→    merged_assessments = _compute_merged_assessments(
   182→        score_buckets, score_raw_by_dim, issue_pressure_by_dim, issue_count_by_dim
   183→    )
   184→
   185→    merged_assessment_payload: dict[str, float | dict[str, object]] = {
   186→        key: value for key, value in merged_assessments.items()
   187→    }
   188→    component_scores = _compute_abstraction_components(
   189→        merged_assessments,
   190→        abstraction_axis_scores,
   191→        abstraction_sub_axes=abstraction_sub_axes,
   192→        abstraction_component_names=abstraction_component_names,
   193→    )
   194→    if component_scores is not None:
   195→        merged_assessment_payload["abstraction_fitness"] = {
   196→            "score": merged_assessments["abstraction_fitness"],
   197→            "components": list(component_scores),
   198→            "component_scores": component_scores,
   199→        }
   200→
   201→    return {
   202→        "assessments": merged_assessment_payload,
   203→        "dimension_notes": merged_dimension_notes,
   204→        "dimension_judgment": merged_dimension_judgment,
   205→        "issues": merged_issues,
   206→        "review_quality": {
   207→            "batch_count": len(batch_results),
   208→            "dimension_coverage": round(
   209→                sum(coverage_values) / max(len(coverage_values), 1),
   210→                3,
   211→            ),
   212→            "evidence_density": round(
   213→                sum(evidence_density_values) / max(len(evidence_density_values), 1),
   214→                3,
   215→            ),
   216→            REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY: int(
   217→                high_score_missing_issue_note_total
   218→            ),
   219→            "issue_pressure": round(sum(issue_pressure_by_dim.values()), 3),
   220→            "dimensions_with_issues": len(issue_count_by_dim),
   221→        },
   222→    }
   223→
   224→
   225→__all__ = ["assessment_weight", "merge_batch_results"]
   226→
```

```
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
    14→from desloppify.app.skill_docs import check_skill_version
    15→from desloppify.base.config import target_strict_score_from_config
    16→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS, CommandError
    17→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    18→from desloppify.base.tooling import check_config_staleness
    19→from desloppify.engine.plan_state import load_plan
    20→from desloppify.intelligence.narrative.core import NarrativeContext, compute_narrative
    21→
    22→from .concerns_view import _show_concerns
    23→from .dimension_views import (
    24→    _load_dimension_issues,
    25→    _render_clean_mechanical_dimension,
    26→    _render_no_matches,
    27→    _render_subjective_dimension,
    28→    _render_subjective_views_guide,
    29→)
    30→from .payload import ShowPayloadMeta, build_show_payload
    31→from .render import (
    32→    render_issues,
    33→    show_agent_plan,
    34→    show_subjective_followup,
    35→    write_show_output_file,
    36→)
    37→from .scope import load_matches, resolve_entity, resolve_noise, resolve_show_scope
    38→
    39→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    40→
    41→
    42→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    43→class ShowOptions:
    44→    """All user-facing show command options extracted once from argparse."""
    45→
    46→    pattern_raw: str = ""
    47→    show_code: bool = False
    48→    chronic: bool = False
    49→    no_budget: bool = False
    50→    output_file: str | None = None
    51→    top: int = 20
    52→
    53→    @classmethod
    54→    def from_args(cls, args: argparse.Namespace) -> ShowOptions:
    55→        return cls(
    56→            pattern_raw=str(getattr(args, "pattern", "") or ""),
    57→            show_code=bool(getattr(args, "code", False)),
    58→            chronic=bool(getattr(args, "chronic", False)),
    59→            no_budget=bool(getattr(args, "no_budget", False)),
    60→            output_file=getattr(args, "output", None),
    61→            top=int(getattr(args, "top", 20) or 20),
    62→        )
    63→
    64→
    65→def _handle_special_entity_views(
    66→    *,
    67→    entity,
    68→    state: dict,
    69→    config: dict,
    70→    lang_name: str | None,
    71→    pattern_raw: str,
    72→) -> bool:
    73→    if entity.kind == "special_view" and entity.pattern.strip().lower() == "concerns":
    74→        _show_concerns(state, lang_name)
    75→        return True
    76→    if entity.kind == "dimension" and entity.is_subjective:
    77→        _render_subjective_dimension(state, config, entity, pattern_raw)
    78→        return True
    79→    return False
    80→
    81→
    82→def _load_entity_matches(
    83→    *,
    84→    state: dict,
    85→    entity,
    86→    pattern: str,
    87→    status_filter: str,
    88→    scope: str | None,
    89→    chronic: bool,
    90→) -> tuple[str, list[dict]] | None:
    91→    if entity.kind != "dimension" or entity.is_subjective:
    92→        return pattern, load_matches(
    93→            state, scope=scope, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=chronic
    94→        )
    95→    matches = _load_dimension_issues(state, entity, status_filter)
    96→    if not matches:
    97→        _render_clean_mechanical_dimension(state, entity)
    98→        return None
    99→    return entity.display_name, matches
   100→
   101→
   102→def _active_plan_or_none() -> dict | None:
   103→    try:
   104→        plan = load_plan()
   105→    except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
   106→        logger.debug("Show command skipped active-plan lookup.", exc_info=exc)
   107→        return None
   108→    if plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("clusters"):
   109→        return plan
   110→    return None
   111→
   112→
   113→def cmd_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   114→    """Show all issues for a file, directory, detector, or pattern."""
   115→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   116→    state = runtime.state
   117→    config = runtime.config
   118→
   119→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   120→        return
   121→
   122→    skill_warning = check_skill_version()
   123→    if skill_warning:
   124→        print(colorize(f"  {skill_warning}", "yellow"))
   125→    config_warning = check_config_staleness(config)
   126→    if config_warning:
   127→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
   128→
   129→    print_triage_guardrail_info(state=state)
   130→
   131→    opts = ShowOptions.from_args(args)
   132→
   133→    ok, pattern, status_filter, scope = resolve_show_scope(args)
   134→    if not ok or pattern is None:
   135→        return
   136→
   137→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   138→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   139→    narrative = compute_narrative(
   140→        state,
   141→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="show"),
   142→    )
   143→
   144→    entity = resolve_entity(pattern, state)
   145→    if _handle_special_entity_views(
   146→        entity=entity,
   147→        state=state,
   148→        config=config,
   149→        lang_name=lang_name,
   150→        pattern_raw=opts.pattern_raw,
   151→    ):
   152→        return
   153→
   154→    match_result = _load_entity_matches(
   155→        state=state,
   156→        entity=entity,
   157→        pattern=pattern,
   158→        status_filter=status_filter,
   159→        scope=scope,
   160→        chronic=opts.chronic,
   161→    )
   162→    if match_result is None:
   163→        return
   164→    pattern, matches = match_result
   165→
   166→    if not matches:
   167→        _render_no_matches(entity, pattern, status_filter, narrative, state, config)
   168→        return
   169→
   170→    (
   171→        surfaced_matches,
   172→        hidden_by_detector,
   173→        noise_budget,
   174→        global_noise_budget,
   175→        budget_warning,
   176→    ) = resolve_noise(
   177→        config,
   178→        matches,
   179→        no_budget=opts.no_budget,
   180→    )
   181→    hidden_total = sum(hidden_by_detector.values())
   182→
   183→    payload = build_show_payload(
   184→        surfaced_matches,
   185→        pattern,
   186→        status_filter,
   187→        ShowPayloadMeta(
   188→            total_matches=len(matches),
   189→            hidden_by_detector=hidden_by_detector,
   190→            noise_budget=noise_budget,
   191→            global_noise_budget=global_noise_budget,
   192→        ),
   193→    )
   194→    write_query({"command": "show", **payload, "narrative": narrative})
   195→
   196→    if opts.output_file:
   197→        if write_show_output_file(opts.output_file, payload, len(surfaced_matches)):
   198→            return
   199→        raise CommandError("Failed to write output file")
   200→
   201→    render_issues(
   202→        surfaced_matches,
   203→        pattern=pattern,
   204→        status_filter=status_filter,
   205→        show_code=opts.show_code,
   206→        top=opts.top,
   207→        hidden_by_detector=hidden_by_detector,
   208→        hidden_total=hidden_total,
   209→        noise_budget=noise_budget,
   210→        global_noise_budget=global_noise_budget,
   211→        budget_warning=budget_warning,
   212→    )
   213→    plan_active = _active_plan_or_none()
   214→    show_agent_plan(narrative, surfaced_matches, plan=plan_active)
   215→    show_subjective_followup(
   216→        state,
   217→        target_strict_score_from_config(config),
   218→    )
   219→
   220→    _render_subjective_views_guide(entity)
   221→
   222→
   223→__all__ = ["cmd_show"]
   224→
```

```
1→"""Rendering and output helpers for show command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→from collections import defaultdict
     7→
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.rendering import (
     9→    print_agent_plan as render_plan_with_living_plan,
    10→)
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.rendering import (
    12→    print_ranked_actions,
    13→)
    14→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.subjective import print_subjective_followup
    15→from desloppify.app.commands.scan.reporting import (
    16→    dimensions as reporting_dimensions_mod,
    17→)
    18→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
    19→from desloppify.base.enums import canonical_issue_status
    20→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import print_write_error
    21→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    22→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import read_code_snippet
    23→from desloppify.engine.planning import CONFIDENCE_ORDER
    24→
    25→from .formatting import format_detail
    26→
    27→
    28→def write_show_output_file(output_file: str, payload: dict, surfaced_count: int) -> bool:
    29→    """Write serialized show payload to file."""
    30→    try:
    31→        safe_write_text(output_file, json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
    32→        print(colorize(f"Wrote {surfaced_count} issues to {output_file}", "green"))
    33→    except OSError as exc:
    34→        payload["output_error"] = str(exc)
    35→        print_write_error(output_file, exc, label="show output")
    36→        return False
    37→    return True
    38→
    39→
    40→def group_matches_by_file(matches: list[dict]) -> list[tuple[str, list]]:
    41→    """Group issues by file and sort by descending count."""
    42→    by_file: dict[str, list] = defaultdict(list)
    43→    for issue in matches:
    44→        by_file[issue["file"]].append(issue)
    45→    return sorted(by_file.items(), key=lambda item: -len(item[1]))
    46→
    47→
    48→def _print_single_issue(issue: dict, *, show_code: bool) -> None:
    49→    """Render a single issue to terminal."""
    50→    normalized_status = canonical_issue_status(issue.get("status"))
    51→    status_icon = {
    52→        "open": "○",
    53→        "fixed": "✓",
    54→        "wontfix": "—",
    55→        "false_positive": "✗",
    56→        "auto_resolved": "◌",
    57→    }.get(normalized_status, "?")
    58→    zone = issue.get("zone", "production")
    59→    zone_tag = colorize(f" [{zone}]", "dim") if zone != "production" else ""
    60→    print(
    61→        f"    {status_icon} T{issue['tier']} [{issue['confidence']}] {issue['summary']}{zone_tag}"
    62→    )
    63→
    64→    detail_parts = format_detail(issue.get("detail", {}))
    65→    if detail_parts:
    66→        print(colorize(f"      {' · '.join(detail_parts)}", "dim"))
    67→    if show_code:
    68→        detail_raw = issue.get("detail", {})
    69→        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}
    70→        target_line = (
    71→            detail.get("line") or (detail.get("lines", [None]) or [None])[0]
    72→        )
    73→        if target_line and issue["file"] not in (".", ""):
    74→            snippet = read_code_snippet(issue["file"], target_line)
    75→            if snippet:
    76→                print(snippet)
    77→    if issue.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2:
    78→        print(
    79→            colorize(
    80→                f"      ⟳ reopened {issue['reopen_count']} times — fix properly or wontfix",
    81→                "red",
    82→            )
    83→        )
    84→    if issue.get("note"):
    85→        print(colorize(f"      note: {issue['note']}", "dim"))
    86→    print(colorize(f"      {issue['id']}", "dim"))
    87→
    88→
    89→def render_issues(
    90→    matches: list[dict],
    91→    *,
    92→    pattern: str,
    93→    status_filter: str,
    94→    show_code: bool,
    95→    top: int,
    96→    hidden_by_detector: dict[str, int],
    97→    hidden_total: int,
    98→    noise_budget: int,
    99→    global_noise_budget: int,
   100→    budget_warning: str | None,
   101→) -> None:
   102→    """Render grouped issues and rollup summary to terminal."""
   103→    sorted_files = group_matches_by_file(matches)
   104→    print(
   105→        colorize(
   106→            f"\n  {len(matches)} {status_filter} issues matching '{pattern}'\n",
   107→            "bold",
   108→        )
   109→    )
   110→    if budget_warning:
   111→        print(colorize(f"  {budget_warning}\n", "yellow"))
   112→    if hidden_total:
   113→        global_label = (
   114→            f", {global_noise_budget} global" if global_noise_budget > 0 else ""
   115→        )
   116→        hidden_parts = ", ".join(
   117→            f"{det}: +{count}" for det, count in hidden_by_detector.items()
   118→        )
   119→        print(
   120→            colorize(
   121→                (
   122→                    "  Noise budget: "
   123→                    f"{noise_budget}/detector{global_label} "
   124→                    f"({hidden_total} hidden: {hidden_parts})\n"
   125→                ),
   126→                "dim",
   127→            )
   128→        )
   129→
   130→    shown_files = sorted_files[:top]
   131→    remaining_files = sorted_files[top:]
   132→    remaining_issues = sum(len(files) for _, files in remaining_files)
   133→
   134→    for filepath, issues in shown_files:
   135→        issues.sort(
   136→            key=lambda issue: (
   137→                issue["tier"],
   138→                CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(issue["confidence"], 9),
   139→            )
   140→        )
   141→        display_path = "Codebase-wide" if filepath == "." else filepath
   142→        print(
   143→            colorize(f"  {display_path}", "cyan")
   144→            + colorize(f"  ({len(issues)} issues)", "dim")
   145→        )
   146→
   147→        for issue in issues:
   148→            _print_single_issue(issue, show_code=show_code)
   149→        print()
   150→
   151→    if remaining_issues:
   152→        print(
   153→            colorize(
   154→                (
   155→                    f"  ... and {len(remaining_files)} more files "
   156→                    f"({remaining_issues} issues). "
   157→                    f"Use --top {top + 20} to see more.\n"
   158→                ),
   159→                "dim",
   160→            )
   161→        )
   162→
   163→    by_detector: dict[str, int] = defaultdict(int)
   164→    by_tier: dict[int, int] = defaultdict(int)
   165→    for issue in matches:
   166→        by_detector[issue["detector"]] += 1
   167→        by_tier[issue["tier"]] += 1
   168→
   169→    print(colorize("  Summary:", "bold"))
   170→    print(
   171→        colorize(
   172→            (
   173→                "    By tier:     "
   174→                + ", ".join(
   175→                    f"T{tier}:{count}" for tier, count in sorted(by_tier.items())
   176→                )
   177→            ),
   178→            "dim",
   179→        )
   180→    )
   181→    print(
   182→        colorize(
   183→            (
   184→                "    By detector: "
   185→                + ", ".join(
   186→                    f"{detector}:{count}"
   187→                    for detector, count in sorted(
   188→                        by_detector.items(), key=lambda item: -item[1]
   189→                    )
   190→                )
   191→            ),
   192→            "dim",
   193→        )
   194→    )
   195→    if hidden_total:
   196→        print(
   197→            colorize(
   198→                (
   199→                    "    Hidden:      "
   200→                    + ", ".join(
   201→                        f"{detector}:+{count}"
   202→                        for detector, count in hidden_by_detector.items()
   203→                    )
   204→                ),
   205→                "dim",
   206→            )
   207→        )
   208→    print()
   209→
   210→
   211→def show_agent_plan(
   212→    narrative: dict, matches: list[dict], *, plan: dict | None = None
   213→) -> None:
   214→    """Render a compact plan from current issues and narrative actions.
   215→
   216→    When a living *plan* is active, renders plan focus/progress instead.
   217→    """
   218→    if plan and (plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("clusters")):
   219→        render_plan_with_living_plan(
   220→            [],
   221→            plan=plan,
   222→            header="  AGENT PLAN (use `desloppify next` to see your next task):",
   223→        )
   224→        return
   225→
   226→    actions = narrative.get("actions", [])
   227→    if not actions and not matches:
   228→        return
   229→
   230→    print(
   231→        colorize(
   232→            "  AGENT PLAN (use `desloppify next --count 20` to inspect more items):",
   233→            "yellow",
   234→        )
   235→    )
   236→    if actions:
   237→        top = actions[0]
   238→        print(
   239→            colorize(
   240→                f"  Agent focus: `{top['command']}` — {top['description']}",
   241→                "cyan",
   242→            )
   243→        )
   244→    elif matches:
   245→        first = matches[0]
   246→        print(
   247→            colorize(
   248→                "  Agent focus: `desloppify next --count 20` — "
   249→                f"inspect and resolve `{first.get('id', '')}`",
   250→                "cyan",
   251→            )
   252→        )
   253→
   254→    if print_ranked_actions(actions):
   255→        print()
   256→
   257→
   258→def show_subjective_followup(
   259→    state: dict, target_strict_score: float, *, objective_backlog: int = 0,
   260→) -> None:
   261→    """Show subjective follow-up guidance for the current state."""
   262→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {}) or {}
   263→    if not dim_scores:
   264→        return
   265→
   266→    subjective = reporting_dimensions_mod.scorecard_subjective_entries(
   267→        state,
   268→        dim_scores=dim_scores,
   269→    )
   270→    if not subjective:
   271→        return
   272→
   273→    followup = reporting_dimensions_mod.build_subjective_followup(
   274→        state,
   275→        subjective,
   276→        threshold=target_strict_score,
   277→        max_quality_items=3,
   278→        max_integrity_items=5,
   279→    )
   280→    if print_subjective_followup(followup, objective_backlog=objective_backlog):
   281→        print()
   282→
   283→
   284→__all__ = [
   285→    "group_matches_by_file",
   286→    "render_issues",
   287→    "show_agent_plan",
   288→    "show_subjective_followup",
   289→    "write_show_output_file",
   290→]
```

```
1→"""Dimension and empty-result rendering helpers for show command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.query import write_query
     6→from desloppify.base.config import target_strict_score_from_config
     7→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     8→
     9→from .render import show_subjective_followup
    10→from .scope import _detector_names_hint, _lookup_dimension_score, load_matches
    11→
    12→
    13→def _print_dimension_score(dim_data: dict, display_name: str) -> None:
    14→    """Print the health/strict score line for a dimension if available."""
    15→    score_val = dim_data.get("score") if isinstance(dim_data, dict) else None
    16→    strict_val = (
    17→        dim_data.get("strict", score_val) if isinstance(dim_data, dict) else None
    18→    )
    19→    if score_val is not None:
    20→        print(
    21→            colorize(
    22→                f"  {display_name}: {score_val:.1f}% health (strict: {strict_val:.1f}%)",
    23→                "bold",
    24→            )
    25→        )
    26→
    27→
    28→def _render_judgment(state: dict, dimension_key: str) -> None:
    29→    """Print judgment narrative (strengths, issue_character, score_rationale) if available."""
    30→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments", {})
    31→    assessment = assessments.get(dimension_key, {})
    32→    if not isinstance(assessment, dict):
    33→        return
    34→    judgment = assessment.get("judgment")
    35→    if not isinstance(judgment, dict):
    36→        return
    37→
    38→    score_rationale = judgment.get("score_rationale", "")
    39→    if score_rationale:
    40→        print(colorize(f"  Score rationale: {score_rationale}", "dim"))
    41→
    42→    strengths = judgment.get("strengths", [])
    43→    if isinstance(strengths, list) and strengths:
    44→        print(colorize("  Strengths:", "dim"))
    45→        for s in strengths:
    46→            print(colorize(f"    - {s}", "dim"))
    47→
    48→    issue_character = judgment.get("issue_character", "")
    49→    if issue_character:
    50→        print(colorize(f"  Issue character: {issue_character}", "dim"))
    51→
    52→
    53→def _render_subjective_dimension(
    54→    state: dict,
    55→    config: dict,
    56→    entity,
    57→    pattern_raw: str,
    58→) -> None:
    59→    """Show score + subjective explanation for a subjective dimension."""
    60→    lowered = pattern_raw.strip().lower().replace(" ", "_") if pattern_raw else ""
    61→    dim_data, display_name = _lookup_dimension_score(state, entity.display_name)
    62→    _print_dimension_score(dim_data, display_name)
    63→    _render_judgment(state, lowered)
    64→    print(
    65→        colorize(
    66→            f"  '{pattern_raw.strip()}' is a subjective dimension "
    67→            "— its score comes from design reviews, not code issues.",
    68→            "yellow",
    69→        )
    70→    )
    71→    dim_reviews = [
    72→        issue
    73→        for issue in (state.get("issues") or {}).values()
    74→        if issue.get("detector") == "review"
    75→        and issue.get("status") == "open"
    76→        and lowered
    77→        in str(issue.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).lower().replace(" ", "_")
    78→    ]
    79→    if dim_reviews:
    80→        print(
    81→            colorize(
    82→                f"  {len(dim_reviews)} open review issue(s). "
    83→                "Run `show review --status open`.",
    84→                "dim",
    85→            )
    86→        )
    87→    show_subjective_followup(
    88→        state,
    89→        target_strict_score_from_config(config),
    90→    )
    91→
    92→
    93→def _render_clean_mechanical_dimension(state: dict, entity) -> None:
    94→    """Show score + 'no open issues' for a mechanical dimension with zero issues."""
    95→    dim_data, display_name = _lookup_dimension_score(state, entity.display_name)
    96→    _print_dimension_score(dim_data, display_name)
    97→    det_list = ", ".join(entity.detectors) if entity.detectors else "none"
    98→    print(
    99→        colorize(
   100→            f"  No open issues for {entity.display_name}. Detectors: {det_list}",
   101→            "green",
   102→        )
   103→    )
   104→
   105→
   106→def _load_dimension_issues(
   107→    state: dict,
   108→    entity,
   109→    status_filter: str,
   110→) -> list[dict]:
   111→    """Load issues for all detectors in a mechanical dimension."""
   112→    all_matches: list[dict] = []
   113→    for detector in entity.detectors:
   114→        matches = load_matches(
   115→            state, scope=detector, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=False
   116→        )
   117→        all_matches.extend(matches)
   118→    seen: set[str] = set()
   119→    unique: list[dict] = []
   120→    for item in all_matches:
   121→        issue_id = item.get("id", "")
   122→        if issue_id not in seen:
   123→            seen.add(issue_id)
   124→            unique.append(item)
   125→    return unique
   126→
   127→
   128→def _render_no_matches(entity, pattern, status_filter, narrative, state, config):
   129→    """Handle no-issues case for normal and subjective views."""
   130→    print(colorize(f"No {status_filter} issues matching: {pattern}", "yellow"))
   131→    write_query(
   132→        {
   133→            "command": "show",
   134→            "query": pattern,
   135→            "status_filter": status_filter,
   136→            "total": 0,
   137→            "issues": [],
   138→            "narrative": narrative,
   139→        }
   140→    )
   141→    if entity.kind == "special_view":
   142→        show_subjective_followup(
   143→            state,
   144→            target_strict_score_from_config(config),
   145→        )
   146→    else:
   147→        hint = _detector_names_hint()
   148→        print(
   149→            colorize(
   150→                f"  Try: show <detector>, show <file>, or show subjective. "
   151→                f"Detectors: {hint}",
   152→                "dim",
   153→            )
   154→        )
   155→
   156→
   157→def _render_subjective_views_guide(entity) -> None:
   158→    """Print related subjective views after subjective output."""
   159→    if entity.kind == "special_view" and entity.pattern.strip().lower() in (
   160→        "subjective",
   161→        "subjective_review",
   162→    ):
   163→        print(colorize("  Related views:", "dim"))
   164→        print(colorize("    `show review --status open`            Per-file design review issues", "dim"))
   165→        print(colorize("    `show subjective_review --status open`  Files needing re-review", "dim"))
   166→
   167→
   168→__all__ = [
   169→    "_load_dimension_issues",
   170→    "_print_dimension_score",
   171→    "_render_clean_mechanical_dimension",
   172→    "_render_no_matches",
   173→    "_render_subjective_dimension",
   174→    "_render_subjective_views_guide",
   175→]
   176→
```

```
1→"""State-path and scan-gating helpers for command modules."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→import argparse
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import auto_detect_lang_name
     8→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     9→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    10→
    11→
    12→def _sole_existing_lang_state_file() -> Path | None:
    13→    """Return the only existing language-specific state file, if unambiguous."""
    14→    state_dir = get_project_root() / ".desloppify"
    15→    if not state_dir.exists():
    16→        return None
    17→    candidates = sorted(path for path in state_dir.glob("state-*.json") if path.is_file())
    18→    if len(candidates) == 1:
    19→        return candidates[0]
    20→    return None
    21→
    22→
    23→def _allow_lang_state_fallback(args: argparse.Namespace) -> bool:
    24→    """Whether command can safely fallback to the sole existing lang state file."""
    25→    # Scan should always honor detected/explicit language mapping to avoid cross-lang merges.
    26→    return getattr(args, "command", None) != "scan"
    27→
    28→
    29→def state_path(args: argparse.Namespace) -> Path | None:
    30→    """Get state file path from args, or None for default."""
    31→    path_arg = getattr(args, "state", None)
    32→    if path_arg:
    33→        return Path(path_arg)
    34→    lang_name = getattr(args, "lang", None)
    35→    if not lang_name:
    36→        lang_name = auto_detect_lang_name(args)
    37→    if lang_name:
    38→        resolved = get_project_root() / ".desloppify" / f"state-{lang_name}.json"
    39→        if resolved.exists() or not _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    40→            return resolved
    41→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    42→        if fallback is not None:
    43→            return fallback
    44→        return resolved
    45→
    46→    if _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    47→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    48→        if fallback is not None:
    49→            return fallback
    50→    return None
    51→
    52→
    53→def require_completed_scan(state: dict) -> bool:
    54→    """Return True when the state contains at least one completed scan."""
    55→    has_completed_scan = bool(state.get("last_scan"))
    56→    if not has_completed_scan:
    57→        print(colorize("No scans yet. Run: desloppify scan", "yellow"))
    58→    return has_completed_scan
    59→
```

```
1→"""Go function extraction: regex-based with brace-depth tracking.
     2→
     3→Originally contributed by tinker495 (KyuSeok Jung) in PR #128.
     4→"""
     5→
     6→from __future__ import annotations
     7→
     8→import hashlib
     9→import re
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→
    12→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import resolve_path
    13→
    14→from desloppify.base.discovery.source import find_source_files
    15→from desloppify.engine.detectors.base import FunctionInfo
    16→
    17→GO_FILE_EXCLUSIONS = ["vendor", "testdata", ".git", "node_modules"]
    18→
    19→_FUNC_DECL_RE = re.compile(
    20→    r"(?m)^func\s+"
    21→    r"(?:\(\s*\w+\s+\*?\w+(?:\[[\w,\s]+\])?\s*\)\s*)?"  # optional receiver
    22→    r"(\w+)"  # function name
    23→    r"(?:\[[\w,\s~|]+\])?"  # optional type params (generics)
    24→    r"\s*\("
    25→)
    26→
    27→
    28→def find_go_files(path: Path | str) -> list[str]:
    29→    """Find Go source files under path."""
    30→    return find_source_files(path, [".go"], exclusions=GO_FILE_EXCLUSIONS)
    31→
    32→
    33→def _find_matching_brace(content: str, open_pos: int) -> int | None:
    34→    """Find closing brace for a Go function body with string/comment awareness."""
    35→    depth = 0
    36→    i = open_pos
    37→    length = len(content)
    38→    while i < length:
    39→        ch = content[i]
    40→
    41→        # Backtick raw string — skip until closing backtick
    42→        if ch == '`':
    43→            i += 1
    44→            while i < length and content[i] != '`':
    45→                i += 1
    46→            i += 1
    47→            continue
    48→
    49→        # Block comment
    50→        if ch == '/' and i + 1 < length and content[i + 1] == '*':
    51→            i += 2
    52→            while i + 1 < length:
    53→                if content[i] == '*' and content[i + 1] == '/':
    54→                    i += 2
    55→                    break
    56→                i += 1
    57→            else:
    58→                i += 1
    59→            continue
    60→
    61→        # Line comment
    62→        if ch == '/' and i + 1 < length and content[i + 1] == '/':
    63→            while i < length and content[i] != '\n':
    64→                i += 1
    65→            continue
    66→
    67→        # Interpreted string literal
    68→        if ch == '"':
    69→            i += 1
    70→            while i < length:
    71→                c = content[i]
    72→                if c == '\\':
    73→                    i += 2
    74→                    continue
    75→                if c == '"':
    76→                    break
    77→                i += 1
    78→            i += 1
    79→            continue
    80→
    81→        # Rune literal
    82→        if ch == "'":
    83→            i += 1
    84→            while i < length:
    85→                c = content[i]
    86→                if c == '\\':
    87→                    i += 2
    88→                    continue
    89→                if c == "'":
    90→                    break
    91→                i += 1
    92→            i += 1
    93→            continue
    94→
    95→        if ch == '{':
    96→            depth += 1
    97→        elif ch == '}':
    98→            depth -= 1
    99→            if depth == 0:
   100→                return i
   101→
   102→        i += 1
   103→    return None
   104→
   105→
   106→def normalize_go_body(body: str) -> str:
   107→    """Strip comments, blank lines, and logging for duplicate detection."""
   108→    lines: list[str] = []
   109→    for raw_line in body.splitlines():
   110→        stripped = raw_line.strip()
   111→        if not stripped:
   112→            continue
   113→        # Skip line comments
   114→        if stripped.startswith("//"):
   115→            continue
   116→        # Skip common logging/debug statements
   117→        if re.match(r"^\s*(?:log\.\w+|fmt\.Print)", stripped):
   118→            continue
   119→        lines.append(stripped)
   120→    return "\n".join(lines)
   121→
   122→
   123→def _find_body_brace(content: str, pos: int) -> int | None:
   124→    """Find the body-opening '{' after a Go func signature, skipping return types.
   125→
   126→    Handles return types like ``map[string]struct{}`` that contain braces
   127→    before the actual function body.  The body-opening brace in Go is always
   128→    preceded by whitespace, while type-literal braces (``struct{}``,
   129→    ``interface{}``) are preceded by an identifier character.
   130→    """
   131→    paren_depth = 1  # we start inside the param list '('
   132→    i = pos
   133→    length = len(content)
   134→    while i < length:
   135→        ch = content[i]
   136→
   137→        # Skip string / rune / backtick / comment literals
   138→        if ch == '`':
   139→            i += 1
   140→            while i < length and content[i] != '`':
   141→                i += 1
   142→            i += 1
   143→            continue
   144→        if ch == '"':
   145→            i += 1
   146→            while i < length:
   147→                if content[i] == '\\':
   148→                    i += 2
   149→                    continue
   150→                if content[i] == '"':
   151→                    break
   152→                i += 1
   153→            i += 1
   154→            continue
   155→        if ch == '/' and i + 1 < length and content[i + 1] == '/':
   156→            while i < length and content[i] != '\n':
   157→                i += 1
   158→            continue
   159→        if ch == '/' and i + 1 < length and content[i + 1] == '*':
   160→            i += 2
   161→            while i + 1 < length:
   162→                if content[i] == '*' and content[i + 1] == '/':
   163→                    i += 2
   164→                    break
   165→                i += 1
   166→            else:
   167→                i += 1
   168→            continue
   169→
   170→        if ch == '(':
   171→            paren_depth += 1
   172→        elif ch == ')':
   173→            paren_depth -= 1
   174→        elif ch == '{' and paren_depth == 0:
   175→            # In Go, the body-opening '{' is always preceded by whitespace
   176→            # (or ')' / '}' at end of return type).  Type-literal braces
   177→            # like struct{} and interface{} are preceded by an identifier.
   178→            prev = content[i - 1] if i > 0 else ' '
   179→            if not prev.isalnum() and prev != '_':
   180→                return i
   181→            # Otherwise it's a type-literal brace — skip the balanced pair.
   182→        i += 1
   183→    return None
   184→
   185→
   186→def _extract_params(content: str, open_paren: int) -> list[str]:
   187→    """Extract parameter names from the first balanced (...) starting at open_paren."""
   188→    depth = 0
   189→    i = open_paren
   190→    length = len(content)
   191→    # Find closing paren of param list
   192→    while i < length:
   193→        ch = content[i]
   194→        if ch == '(':
   195→            depth += 1
   196→        elif ch == ')':
   197→            depth -= 1
   198→            if depth == 0:
   199→                break
   200→        i += 1
   201→    else:
   202→        return []
   203→
   204→    raw_params = content[open_paren + 1 : i]
   205→    params: list[str] = []
   206→    for chunk in raw_params.split(","):
   207→        token = chunk.strip()
   208→        if not token:
   209→            continue
   210→        parts = token.split()
   211→        if parts:
   212→            param_name = parts[0]
   213→            if re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z_]\w*", param_name):
   214→                params.append(param_name)
   215→    return params
   216→
   217→
   218→def extract_go_functions(filepath: str) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
   219→    """Extract Go functions/methods from one file."""
   220→    try:
   221→        content = Path(resolve_path(filepath)).read_text(errors="replace")
   222→    except OSError:
   223→        return []
   224→
   225→    functions: list[FunctionInfo] = []
   226→    for match in _FUNC_DECL_RE.finditer(content):
   227→        name = match.group(1)
   228→        start = match.start()
   229→        start_line = content.count("\n", 0, start) + 1
   230→
   231→        # The regex ends just after the opening '(' of the param list.
   232→        # Scan forward to find the body-opening '{' while respecting
   233→        # balanced parens/brackets in return types like map[K]struct{}.
   234→        brace_pos = _find_body_brace(content, match.end())
   235→        if brace_pos is None:
   236→            continue
   237→
   238→        end = _find_matching_brace(content, brace_pos)
   239→        if end is None:
   240→            continue
   241→
   242→        end_line = content.count("\n", 0, end) + 1
   243→        body = content[start : end + 1]
   244→
   245→        # Extract parameter names — the regex match ends at '(' so
   246→        # match.end() - 1 is the opening paren position.
   247→        params = _extract_params(content, match.end() - 1)
   248→
   249→        normalized = normalize_go_body(body)
   250→        body_hash = hashlib.md5(normalized.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:12]
   251→        functions.append(
   252→            FunctionInfo(
   253→                name=name,
   254→                file=resolve_path(filepath),
   255→                line=start_line,
   256→                end_line=end_line,
   257→                loc=max(1, end_line - start_line + 1),
   258→                body=body,
   259→                normalized=normalized,
   260→                body_hash=body_hash,
   261→                params=params,
   262→            )
   263→        )
   264→
   265→    return functions
   266→
   267→
   268→def extract_functions(path: Path) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
   269→    """Extract all Go functions below a directory path."""
   270→    functions: list[FunctionInfo] = []
   271→    for filepath in find_go_files(path):
   272→        functions.extend(extract_go_functions(filepath))
   273→    return functions
   274→
```

```
    47→class GoConfig(LangConfig):
    48→    """Go language configuration."""
    49→
    50→    def __init__(self):
    51→        super().__init__(
    52→            name="go",
    53→            extensions=[".go"],

    55→            default_src=".",

    57→            entry_patterns=GO_ENTRY_PATTERNS,

    67→                ),

    70→                ),
    71→                *all_treesitter_phases("go"),

    79→            detect_commands=get_detect_commands(),

    85→            default_scan_profile="full",
    86→            detect_markers=["go.mod"],
    87→            external_test_dirs=[],

    96→            extract_functions=extract_functions,
    97→            zone_rules=GO_ZONE_RULES,
    98→        )
    99→
   100→
   101→def register() -> None:
   102→    """Register Go language config + hooks through an explicit entrypoint."""
   103→    register_full_plugin(
   104→        "go",
   105→        GoConfig,
   106→        test_coverage=go_test_coverage_hooks,
   107→    )

   112→    register_lang_hooks("go", test_coverage=go_test_coverage_hooks)
   113→
   114→Config = GoConfig
```

```
1→"""State persistence and migration routines."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import shutil
     8→import sys
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→from typing import cast
    11→
    12→__all__ = [
    13→    "load_state",
    14→    "save_state",
    15→]
    16→
    17→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
    18→from desloppify.base.text_utils import is_numeric
    19→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    20→    CURRENT_VERSION,
    21→    StateModel,
    22→    empty_state,
    23→    ensure_state_defaults,
    24→    get_state_file,
    25→    json_default,
    26→    validate_state_invariants,
    27→)
    28→
    29→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    30→
    31→_STATE_FILE_SENTINEL = object()
    32→STATE_FILE = _STATE_FILE_SENTINEL
    33→
    34→
    35→from desloppify.engine._state import _recompute_stats
    36→
    37→
    38→def _default_state_file() -> Path:
    39→    """Resolve the default state path, honoring runtime context overrides.
    40→
    41→    If tests monkeypatch ``STATE_FILE`` in this module, use that override.
    42→    """
    43→    if STATE_FILE is not _STATE_FILE_SENTINEL:
    44→        return Path(STATE_FILE)
    45→    return get_state_file()
    46→
    47→
    48→def _load_json(path: Path) -> dict[str, object]:
    49→    data = json.loads(path.read_text())
    50→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    51→        raise ValueError("state file root must be a JSON object")
    52→    return data
    53→
    54→
    55→def _normalize_loaded_state(data: object) -> dict[str, object]:
    56→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    57→        raise ValueError("state file root must be a JSON object")
    58→    ensure_state_defaults(data)
    59→    normalized = cast(StateModel, data)
    60→    validate_state_invariants(normalized)
    61→    return normalized
    62→
    63→
    64→def load_state(path: Path | None = None) -> StateModel:
    65→    """Load state from disk, or return empty state on missing/corruption."""
    66→    state_path = path or _default_state_file()
    67→    if not state_path.exists():
    68→        return empty_state()
    69→
    70→    try:
    71→        data = _load_json(state_path)
    72→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError, ValueError) as ex:
    73→        backup = state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    74→        if backup.exists():
    75→            logger.warning(
    76→                "Primary state load failed for %s; attempting backup %s: %s",
    77→                state_path,
    78→                backup,
    79→                ex,
    80→            )
    81→            try:
    82→                backup_data = _load_json(backup)
    83→                logger.warning(
    84→                    "Recovered state from backup %s after primary load failure at %s",
    85→                    backup,
    86→                    state_path,
    87→                )
    88→                print(
    89→                    f"  ⚠ State file corrupted ({ex}), loaded from backup.",
    90→                    file=sys.stderr,
    91→                )
    92→                return _normalize_loaded_state(backup_data)
    93→            except (
    94→                json.JSONDecodeError,
    95→                UnicodeDecodeError,
    96→                OSError,
    97→                ValueError,
    98→                TypeError,
    99→                AttributeError,
   100→            ) as backup_ex:
   101→                logger.warning(
   102→                    "Backup state load failed from %s after corruption in %s: %s",
   103→                    backup,
   104→                    state_path,
   105→                    backup_ex,
   106→                )
   107→                logger.debug("Backup state load failed from %s: %s", backup, backup_ex)
   108→
   109→        logger.warning(
   110→            "State file load failed for %s and backup recovery was unavailable. "
   111→            "Falling back to empty state: %s",
   112→            state_path,
   113→            ex,
   114→        )
   115→        print(f"  ⚠ State file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
   116→        rename_failed = False
   117→        try:
   118→            state_path.rename(state_path.with_suffix(".json.corrupted"))
   119→        except OSError as rename_ex:
   120→            rename_failed = True
   121→            logger.debug(
   122→                "Failed to rename corrupted state file %s: %s", state_path, rename_ex
   123→            )
   124→        if rename_failed:
   125→            logger.debug(
   126→                "Corrupted state file retained at original path: %s", state_path
   127→            )
   128→        return empty_state()
   129→
   130→    version = data.get("version", 1)
   131→    if version > CURRENT_VERSION:
   132→        print(
   133→            "  ⚠ State file version "
   134→            f"{version} is newer than supported ({CURRENT_VERSION}). "
   135→            "Some features may not work correctly.",
   136→            file=sys.stderr,
   137→        )
   138→
   139→    try:
   140→        return _normalize_loaded_state(data)
   141→    except (ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as normalize_ex:
   142→        logger.warning(
   143→            "State invariants invalid for %s; falling back to empty state: %s",
   144→            state_path,
   145→            normalize_ex,
   146→        )
   147→        print(
   148→            f"  ⚠ State invariants invalid ({normalize_ex}). Starting fresh.",
   149→            file=sys.stderr,
   150→        )
   151→        return empty_state()
   152→
   153→
   154→def _coerce_integrity_target(value: object) -> float | None:
   155→    if not is_numeric(value):
   156→        return None
   157→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value)))
   158→
   159→
   160→def _resolve_integrity_target(
   161→    state: StateModel,
   162→    explicit_target: float | None,
   163→) -> float | None:
   164→    target = _coerce_integrity_target(explicit_target)
   165→    if target is not None:
   166→        return target
   167→
   168→    integrity = state.get("subjective_integrity")
   169→    if not isinstance(integrity, dict):
   170→        return None
   171→    return _coerce_integrity_target(integrity.get("target_score"))
   172→
   173→
   174→def save_state(
   175→    state: StateModel,
   176→    path: Path | None = None,
   177→    *,
   178→    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None,
   179→) -> None:
   180→    """Recompute stats/score and save to disk atomically."""
   181→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   182→    _recompute_stats(
   183→        state,
   184→        scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
   185→        subjective_integrity_target=_resolve_integrity_target(
   186→            state,
   187→            subjective_integrity_target,
   188→        ),
   189→    )
   190→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   191→
   192→    state_path = path or _default_state_file()
   193→    state_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   194→
   195→    content = json.dumps(state, indent=2, default=json_default) + "\n"
   196→
   197→    if state_path.exists():
   198→        backup = state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
   199→        try:
   200→            shutil.copy2(str(state_path), str(backup))
   201→        except OSError as backup_ex:
   202→            logger.debug(
   203→                "Failed to create state backup %s: %s",
   204→                state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak"),
   205→                backup_ex,
   206→            )
   207→
   208→    try:
   209→        safe_write_text(state_path, content)
   210→    except OSError as ex:
   211→        print(f"  Warning: Could not save state: {ex}", file=sys.stderr)
   212→        raise
   213→
```
