```
1→"""Assessment import policy and provenance helpers for review imports."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→from dataclasses import replace
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.intelligence.review.importing.contracts_models import (
    10→    AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
    11→    AssessmentProvenanceModel,
    12→)
    13→from desloppify.intelligence.review.importing.contracts_types import (
    14→    AssessmentImportPolicy,
    15→    ReviewImportPayload,
    16→)
    17→
    18→from ..runtime_paths import blind_packet_path, runtime_project_root
    19→
    20→[REDACTED]
    21→BLIND_PROVENANCE_KIND = "blind_review_batch_import"
    22→SUPPORTED_BLIND_REVIEW_RUNNERS = {"codex", "claude"}
    23→ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_RUNNERS = {"claude"}
    24→ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_REQUIRED_PHRASES = ("without awareness", "unbiased")
    25→ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_ATTEST_EXAMPLE = (
    26→    "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."
    27→)
    28→ASSESSMENT_MODE_LABELS = {
    29→    "none": "issues-only (no assessments in payload)",
    30→    "trusted_internal": "trusted internal (durable scores)",
    31→    "attested_external": "attested external (durable scores)",
    32→    "manual_override": "manual override (provisional scores)",
    33→    "issues_only": "issues-only (assessments skipped)",
    34→}
    35→
    36→
    37→def _default_blind_packet_path() -> Path:
    38→    return blind_packet_path()
    39→
    40→
    41→def _is_sha256_hex(raw: object) -> bool:
    42→    return (
    43→        isinstance(raw, str)
    44→        and len(raw) == 64
    45→        and all(ch in "0123456789abcdefABCDEF" for ch in raw)
    46→    )
    47→
    48→
    49→def _hash_file_sha256(path: Path) -> str | None:
    50→    try:
    51→        data = path.read_bytes()
    52→    except OSError:
    53→        return None
    54→    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()
    55→
    56→
    57→def _resolve_packet_path(raw_path: object) -> Path | None:
    58→    if not isinstance(raw_path, str):
    59→        return None
    60→    text = raw_path.strip()
    61→    if not text:
    62→        return None
    63→    path = Path(text)
    64→    return path if path.is_absolute() else runtime_project_root() / path
    65→
    66→
    67→def _assessment_provenance_status(
    68→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
    69→    *,
    70→    import_file: str,
    71→) -> AssessmentProvenanceModel:
    72→    """Evaluate whether assessments come from a trusted blind batch artifact."""
    73→    provenance = issues_data["provenance"]
    74→    if not provenance:
    75→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
    76→            trusted=False,
    77→            reason="missing provenance metadata",
    78→            import_file=import_file,
    79→        )
    80→
    81→    kind = str(provenance.get("kind", "")).strip()
    82→    if kind != BLIND_PROVENANCE_KIND:
    83→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
    84→            trusted=False,
    85→            reason=f"unsupported provenance kind: {kind or '<missing>'}",
    86→            import_file=import_file,
    87→        )
    88→
    89→    if provenance.get("blind") is not True:
    90→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
    91→            trusted=False,
    92→            reason="provenance is not marked blind=true",
    93→            import_file=import_file,
    94→        )
    95→
    96→    runner = str(provenance.get("runner", "")).strip().lower()
    97→    if runner not in SUPPORTED_BLIND_REVIEW_RUNNERS:
    98→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
    99→            trusted=False,
   100→            reason=f"unsupported runner in provenance: {runner or '<missing>'}",
   101→            import_file=import_file,
   102→        )
   103→
   104→    packet_hash = provenance.get("packet_sha256")
   105→    if not _is_sha256_hex(packet_hash):
   106→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   107→            trusted=False,
   108→            reason="missing or invalid packet_sha256 in provenance",
   109→            import_file=import_file,
   110→        )
   111→
   112→    packet_path = _resolve_packet_path(provenance.get("packet_path"))
   113→    if packet_path is None:
   114→        packet_path = _default_blind_packet_path()
   115→    if not packet_path.exists():
   116→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   117→            trusted=False,
   118→            reason=f"blind packet not found: {packet_path}",
   119→            import_file=import_file,
   120→        )
   121→    observed_hash = _hash_file_sha256(packet_path)
   122→    if observed_hash is None:
   123→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   124→            trusted=False,
   125→            reason=f"unable to hash blind packet: {packet_path}",
   126→            import_file=import_file,
   127→        )
   128→    if observed_hash != packet_hash:
   129→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   130→            trusted=False,
   131→            reason=(
   132→                "blind packet hash mismatch "
   133→                f"(expected {packet_hash[:12]}..., got {observed_hash[:12]}...)"
   134→            ),
   135→            import_file=import_file,
   136→        )
   137→
   138→    return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   139→        trusted=True,
   140→        reason="trusted blind subagent provenance",
   141→        runner=runner,
   142→        packet_path=str(packet_path),
   143→        packet_sha256=packet_hash,
   144→        import_file=import_file,
   145→    )
   146→
   147→
   148→def validate_attested_external_attestation(attest: str | None) -> str | None:
   149→    """Validate and normalize attestation text for attested external imports."""
   150→    if not isinstance(attest, str) or not attest.strip():
   151→        return None
   152→    text = attest.strip()
   153→    lowered = text.lower()
   154→    if all(phrase in lowered for phrase in ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_REQUIRED_PHRASES):
   155→        return text
   156→    return None
   157→
   158→
   159→def apply_assessment_import_policy(
   160→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   161→    *,
   162→    import_file: str,
   163→    attested_external: bool,
   164→    attested_attest: str | None,
   165→    manual_override: bool,
   166→    manual_attest: str | None,
   167→    trusted_assessment_source: bool,
   168→    trusted_assessment_label: str | None,
   169→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   170→    """Apply trust gating for assessment imports (issues import always allowed)."""
   171→    assessments = issues_data["assessments"]
   172→    has_assessments = bool(assessments)
   173→    assessment_count = len(assessments) if has_assessments else 0
   174→    provenance_status = _assessment_provenance_status(
   175→        issues_data, import_file=import_file
   176→    )
   177→    policy = AssessmentImportPolicyModel(
   178→        assessments_present=has_assessments,
   179→        assessment_count=int(assessment_count),
   180→        trusted=False,
   181→        mode="none",
   182→        reason="",
   183→        provenance=provenance_status,
   184→    )
   185→
   186→    if not has_assessments:
   187→        return _attach_assessment_policy(issues_data, policy), []
   188→
   189→    if trusted_assessment_source:
   190→        trusted_policy = replace(
   191→            policy,
   192→            mode="trusted_internal",
   193→            trusted=True,
   194→            reason=(trusted_assessment_label or "trusted internal run-batches import"),
   195→        )
   196→        return _attach_assessment_policy(issues_data, trusted_policy), []
   197→
   198→    if attested_external:
   199→        return _apply_attested_external_policy(
   200→            issues_data,
   201→            policy=policy,
   202→            provenance_status=provenance_status,
   203→            attested_attest=attested_attest,
   204→        )
   205→
   206→    if manual_override:
   207→        return _apply_manual_override_policy(
   208→            issues_data,
   209→            policy=policy,
   210→            manual_attest=manual_attest,
   211→        )
   212→
   213→    return _apply_issues_only_policy(
   214→        issues_data,
   215→        policy=policy,
   216→        provenance_status=provenance_status,
   217→    )
   218→
   219→
   220→def _attach_assessment_policy(
   221→    payload: ReviewImportPayload,
   222→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
   223→) -> ReviewImportPayload:
   224→    normalized = dict(payload)
   225→    normalized[ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY] = policy.to_dict()
   226→    return normalized
   227→
   228→
   229→def _apply_attested_external_policy(
   230→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   231→    *,
   232→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
   233→    provenance_status: AssessmentProvenanceModel,
   234→    attested_attest: str | None,
   235→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   236→    normalized_attest = validate_attested_external_attestation(attested_attest)
   237→    if normalized_attest is None:
   238→        return None, [
   239→            "--attested-external requires --attest containing both "
   240→            "'without awareness' and 'unbiased'"
   241→        ]
   242→    if provenance_status.trusted is not True:
   243→        return None, [
   244→            "--attested-external requires valid blind packet provenance "
   245→            f"(current status: {provenance_status.reason or 'untrusted provenance'})"
   246→        ]
   247→    runner = provenance_status.runner.strip().lower()
   248→    if runner not in ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_RUNNERS:
   249→        return None, [
   250→            "--attested-external currently supports runner='claude' provenance only"
   251→        ]
   252→    attested_policy = replace(
   253→        policy,
   254→        mode="attested_external",
   255→        trusted=True,
   256→        reason="attested external blind subagent provenance",
   257→        attest=normalized_attest,
   258→    )
   259→    return _attach_assessment_policy(issues_data, attested_policy), []
   260→
   261→
   262→def _apply_manual_override_policy(
   263→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   264→    *,
   265→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
   266→    manual_attest: str | None,
   267→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   268→    if not isinstance(manual_attest, str) or not manual_attest.strip():
   269→        return None, ["--manual-override requires --attest"]
   270→    override_policy = replace(
   271→        policy,
   272→        mode="manual_override",
   273→        reason="manual override attested by operator",
   274→        attest=manual_attest.strip(),
   275→    )
   276→    return _attach_assessment_policy(issues_data, override_policy), []
   277→
   278→
   279→def _issues_only_reason(
   280→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   281→    *,
   282→    provenance_status: AssessmentProvenanceModel,
   283→) -> str:
   284→    if not issues_data["provenance"]:
   285→        return "missing trusted run-batches source; imported issues only"
   286→    provenance_reason = provenance_status.reason.strip()
   287→    if provenance_status.trusted is True:
   288→        return (
   289→            "external imports cannot self-attest trust even when provenance appears valid; "
   290→            "run review --run-batches to apply assessments automatically"
   291→        )
   292→    if provenance_reason:
   293→        return (
   294→            "external imports cannot self-attest trust "
   295→            f"({provenance_reason}); run review --run-batches to apply assessments automatically"
   296→        )
   297→    return (
   298→        "external imports cannot self-attest trust; "
   299→        "run review --run-batches to apply assessments automatically"
   300→    )
   301→
   302→
   303→def _apply_issues_only_policy(
   304→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   305→    *,
   306→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
   307→    provenance_status: AssessmentProvenanceModel,
   308→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload, list[str]]:
   309→    issues_only_policy = replace(
   310→        policy,
   311→        mode="issues_only",
   312→        reason=_issues_only_reason(issues_data, provenance_status=provenance_status),
   313→    )
   314→    payload = dict(issues_data)
   315→    payload["assessments"] = {}
   316→    payload[ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY] = issues_only_policy.to_dict()
   317→    return payload, []
   318→
   319→
   320→def assessment_policy_from_payload(payload: ReviewImportPayload) -> AssessmentImportPolicy:
   321→    """Return parsed assessment policy metadata from a loaded import payload."""
   322→    policy = payload[ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY]
   323→    if isinstance(policy, dict):
   324→        return policy
   325→    return AssessmentImportPolicyModel().to_dict()
   326→
   327→
   328→def assessment_policy_model_from_payload(
   329→    payload: ReviewImportPayload,
   330→) -> AssessmentImportPolicyModel:
   331→    """Return typed assessment policy metadata from a loaded import payload."""
   332→    return AssessmentImportPolicyModel.from_mapping(assessment_policy_from_payload(payload))
   333→
   334→
   335→def assessment_mode_label(policy: AssessmentImportPolicy) -> str:
   336→    """Return a user-facing label for the selected assessment import mode."""
   337→    mode = AssessmentImportPolicyModel.from_mapping(policy).mode.strip().lower()
   338→    return ASSESSMENT_MODE_LABELS.get(mode, f"unknown ({mode or 'none'})")
   339→
```

```
    90→def collect_reviewed_files_from_batches(
    91→    *,
    92→    batches: list[dict[str, object]],
    93→    selected_indexes: list[int],
    94→) -> list[str]:
    95→    """Collect normalized file paths reviewed in the selected batch set."""
    96→    reviewed: list[str] = []
    97→    seen: set[str] = set()
    98→    for idx in selected_indexes:
    99→        if idx < 0 or idx >= len(batches):
   100→            continue
   101→        batch = batches[idx]
   102→        files = batch.get("files_to_read", [])
   103→        if not isinstance(files, list):
   104→            continue
   105→        for raw in files:
   106→            if not isinstance(raw, str):
   107→                continue
   108→            path = raw.strip().strip(",'\"")
   109→            if not path or path in {".", ".."}:
   110→                continue
   111→            if path.endswith("/"):
   112→                continue
   113→            if path in seen:
   114→                continue
   115→            seen.add(path)
   116→            reviewed.append(path)
   117→    return reviewed
   118→
   119→
   120→def normalize_dimension_list(raw: object) -> list[str]:
   121→    """Normalize dimension collections to a stable, de-duplicated list."""
   122→    if not isinstance(raw, list):
   123→        return []
   124→    out: list[str] = []
   125→    seen: set[str] = set()
   126→    for item in raw:
   127→        if not isinstance(item, str):
   128→            continue
   129→        dim = item.strip()
   130→        if not dim or dim in seen:
   131→            continue
   132→        seen.add(dim)
   133→        out.append(dim)
   134→    return out
   135→
   136→
   137→def scored_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name: str) -> list[str]:
   138→    """Return default scored subjective dimensions for one language."""
   139→    try:
   140→        default_dims, _, _ = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name)
   141→    except (ValueError, RuntimeError):
   142→        return []
   143→    return normalize_dimension_list(default_dims)
   144→
   145→
   146→def missing_scored_dimensions(
   147→    *,
   148→    selected_dims: list[str],
   149→    scored_dims: list[str],
   150→) -> list[str]:
   151→    selected = set(selected_dims)
   152→    return [dim for dim in scored_dims if dim not in selected]
   153→
   154→
   155→def missing_dimensions_command(*, missing_dims: list[str], scan_path: str) -> str:
   156→    """Return rerun command for missing subjective dimensions."""
   157→    base = "desloppify review --prepare --scan-after-import"
   158→    if scan_path and scan_path != ".":
   159→        base += f" --path {shlex.quote(scan_path)}"
   160→    if missing_dims:
   161→        base += f" --dimensions {','.join(missing_dims)}"
   162→    return base
   163→
   164→
   165→def print_preflight_dimension_scope_notice(
   166→    *,
   167→    selected_dims: list[str],
   168→    scored_dims: list[str],
   169→    explicit_selection: bool,
   170→    scan_path: str,
   171→    colorize_fn,
   172→) -> None:
   173→    """Print trigger-time notice when run scope is a scored-dimension subset."""
   174→    if not scored_dims:
   175→        return
   176→    missing_dims = missing_scored_dimensions(

   179→    )
   180→    if not missing_dims:

   182→
   183→    covered_count = len([dim for dim in selected_dims if dim in set(scored_dims)])
   184→    scope_reason = (

   188→    )
   189→    tone = "yellow" if explicit_selection else "red"

   197→    )
   198→    preview = ", ".join(missing_dims[:5])
   199→    if len(missing_dims) > 5:
   200→        preview = f"{preview}, +{len(missing_dims) - 5} more"
   201→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Missing from this run: {preview}", "yellow"))
   202→    print(
   203→        colorize_fn(

   206→            "dim",
   207→        )
   208→    )
   209→
   210→
   211→def print_import_dimension_coverage_notice(

   237→    if len(missing_dims) > 5:

   239→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Still missing: {preview}", "yellow"))
   240→    print(
   241→        colorize_fn(

   244→            "dim",
   245→        )
   246→    )
   247→    return missing_dims
   248→
   249→
   250→def enforce_trusted_import_coverage_gate(
   251→    *,
   252→    missing_dims: list[str],
   253→    selected_dims: list[str],
   254→    allow_partial: bool,
   255→    scan_path: str,

   259→    if not selected_dims or not missing_dims:
```

```
1→"""Scope and queue helpers for show command selection."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     9→from desloppify.base import registry as registry_mod
    10→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    11→from desloppify.engine._scoring.policy.core import DIMENSIONS
    12→from desloppify.engine._scoring.subjective.core import DISPLAY_NAMES
    13→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    14→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.core import (
    15→    QueueBuildOptions,
    16→    build_work_queue,
    17→)
    18→
    19→
    20→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    21→class ResolvedEntity:
    22→    """Classifies what the user's show pattern refers to."""
    23→
    24→    kind: str  # "dimension", "special_view", "file_or_pattern"
    25→    pattern: str  # Original user pattern
    26→    display_name: str  # Human-readable label
    27→    detectors: tuple[str, ...] = ()  # Detector names (for dimension kind)
    28→    is_subjective: bool = False  # Whether this is a subjective dimension
    29→
    30→
    31→def _resolve_special_view(pattern: str, lowered: str) -> ResolvedEntity | None:
    32→    if lowered not in ("concerns", "subjective", "subjective_review"):
    33→        return None
    34→    return ResolvedEntity(
    35→        kind="special_view",
    36→        pattern=pattern,
    37→        display_name=pattern,
    38→    )
    39→
    40→
    41→def _resolve_mechanical_dimension(pattern: str, lowered: str) -> ResolvedEntity | None:
    42→    lookup = _build_dimension_lookup()
    43→    detectors = lookup.get(lowered) or lookup.get(pattern.lower())
    44→    if not detectors:
    45→        return None
    46→    dim_display = pattern
    47→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
    48→        dim_lowered = dim.name.lower().replace(" ", "_")
    49→        if dim.name.lower() == lowered or dim_lowered == lowered:
    50→            dim_display = dim.name
    51→            break
    52→    return ResolvedEntity(
    53→        kind="dimension",
    54→        pattern=pattern,
    55→        display_name=dim_display,
    56→        detectors=tuple(detectors),
    57→        is_subjective=False,
    58→    )
    59→
    60→
    61→def _resolve_subjective_dimension(
    62→    pattern: str,
    63→    lowered: str,
    64→    state: StateModel,
    65→) -> ResolvedEntity | None:
    66→    display_name = DISPLAY_NAMES.get(lowered)
    67→    if not display_name:
    68→        for key in state.get("dimension_scores") or {}:
    69→            if key.lower().replace(" ", "_") == lowered:
    70→                display_name = key
    71→                break
    72→    if not display_name:
    73→        return None
    74→    dim_data, display_name = _lookup_dimension_score(state, display_name)
    75→    is_subj = "subjective_assessment" in (
    76→        dim_data.get("detectors", {}) if isinstance(dim_data, dict) else {}
    77→    )
    78→    return ResolvedEntity(
    79→        kind="dimension",
    80→        pattern=pattern,
    81→        display_name=display_name,
    82→        detectors=(),
    83→        is_subjective=is_subj,
    84→    )
    85→
    86→
    87→def resolve_entity(pattern: str, state: StateModel) -> ResolvedEntity:
    88→    """Classify a user pattern as a dimension, special view, or passthrough.
    89→
    90→    Resolution priority:
    91→    1. Special views: "concerns", "subjective", "subjective_review"
    92→    2. Mechanical dimension name (from DIMENSIONS)
    93→    3. Subjective dimension name (from DISPLAY_NAMES / dimension_scores)
    94→    4. Everything else: file_or_pattern passthrough
    95→    """
    96→    lowered = pattern.strip().lower().replace(" ", "_")
    97→
    98→    # 1. Special views
    99→    special_view = _resolve_special_view(pattern, lowered)
   100→    if special_view is not None:
   101→        return special_view
   102→
   103→    # 2. Mechanical dimension (via DIMENSIONS list)
   104→    mechanical = _resolve_mechanical_dimension(pattern, lowered)
   105→    if mechanical is not None:
   106→        return mechanical
   107→
   108→    # 3. Subjective dimension (via DISPLAY_NAMES or dimension_scores)
   109→    subjective = _resolve_subjective_dimension(pattern, lowered, state)
   110→    if subjective is not None:
   111→        return subjective
   112→
   113→    # 4. Everything else
   114→    return ResolvedEntity(
   115→        kind="file_or_pattern",
   116→        pattern=pattern,
   117→        display_name=pattern,
   118→    )
   119→
   120→
   121→def _build_dimension_lookup() -> dict[str, list[str]]:
   122→    """Build a map from dimension name/key (lowered) to detector names."""
   123→    lookup: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   124→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   125→        detectors = list(dim.detectors) if hasattr(dim, "detectors") else []
   126→        lookup[dim.name.lower()] = detectors
   127→        # Also index by underscore key: "file_health" -> detectors
   128→        key = dim.name.lower().replace(" ", "_")
   129→        if key not in lookup:
   130→            lookup[key] = detectors
   131→    # Also add DISPLAY_NAMES reverse lookup (e.g. "abstraction_fit" -> "Abstraction Fit")
   132→    for key, display in DISPLAY_NAMES.items():
   133→        normalized_key = key.lower().replace(" ", "_")
   134→        normalized_display = display.lower()
   135→        # Find which dimension this belongs to via get_dimension_for_detector or direct name match
   136→        for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   137→            dim_lower = dim.name.lower()
   138→            if normalized_display == dim_lower or normalized_key == dim_lower.replace(" ", "_"):
   139→                detectors = list(dim.detectors) if hasattr(dim, "detectors") else []
   140→                if normalized_key not in lookup:
   141→                    lookup[normalized_key] = detectors
   142→                if normalized_display not in lookup:
   143→                    lookup[normalized_display] = detectors
   144→    return lookup
   145→
   146→
   147→def _lookup_dimension_score(
   148→    state: StateModel, display_name: str,
   149→) -> tuple[dict[str, Any], str]:
   150→    """Find dimension_scores entry with case-insensitive fallback.
   151→
   152→    Returns (dim_data_dict, resolved_display_name).
   153→    """
   154→    lowered = display_name.lower().replace(" ", "_")
   155→    dim_data = (state.get("dimension_scores") or {}).get(display_name, {})
   156→    if not dim_data:
   157→        for ds_key, ds_val in (state.get("dimension_scores") or {}).items():
   158→            if ds_key.lower().replace(" ", "_") == lowered:
   159→                dim_data = ds_val
   160→                display_name = ds_key
   161→                break
   162→    return dim_data, display_name
   163→
   164→
   165→def _detector_names_hint() -> str:
   166→    """Return a compact list of detector names for the help message."""
   167→    names = getattr(registry_mod, "DISPLAY_ORDER", [])
   168→    if names:
   169→        return ", ".join(names[:10]) + (", ..." if len(names) > 10 else "")
   170→    return "smells, structural, security, review, ..."
   171→
   172→
   173→def resolve_show_scope(args: object) -> tuple[bool, str | None, str, str | None]:
   174→    """Resolve scope/pattern/status for a show invocation."""
   175→    chronic = getattr(args, "chronic", False)
   176→    pattern = args.pattern
   177→    status_filter = "open" if chronic else getattr(args, "status", "open")
   178→    if chronic:
   179→        scope = pattern
   180→        pattern = pattern or "<chronic>"
   181→        return True, pattern, status_filter, scope
   182→    if not pattern:
   183→        print(
   184→            colorize(
   185→                "Pattern required (or use --chronic). Try: desloppify show --help",
   186→                "yellow",
   187→            )
   188→        )
   189→        return False, None, status_filter, ""
   190→    return True, pattern, status_filter, pattern
   191→
   192→
   193→def load_matches(
   194→    state: StateModel,
   195→    *,
   196→    scope: str | None,
   197→    status_filter: str,
   198→    chronic: bool,
   199→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   200→    """Load matching issues from the ranked queue."""
   201→    queue = build_work_queue(
   202→        state,
   203→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
   204→            count=None,
   205→            scope=scope,
   206→            status=status_filter,
   207→            include_subjective=False,
   208→            chronic=chronic,
   209→        ),
   210→    )
   211→    return [item for item in queue.get("items", []) if item.get("kind") == "issue"]
   212→
   213→
   214→def resolve_noise(
   215→    config: dict[str, Any],
   216→    matches: list[dict[str, Any]],
   217→    *,
   218→    no_budget: bool = False,
   219→) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, int], int, int, str | None]:
   220→    """Apply detector/global noise budget to show matches.
   221→
   222→    When *no_budget* is True, all matches are surfaced (nothing hidden).
   223→    """
   224→    if no_budget:
   225→        return (
   226→            matches,
   227→            {},
   228→            0,
   229→            0,
   230→            None,
   231→        )
   232→    noise_budget, global_noise_budget, budget_warning = (
   233→        state_mod.resolve_issue_noise_settings(config)
   234→    )
   235→    surfaced_matches, hidden_by_detector = state_mod.apply_issue_noise_budget(
   236→        matches,
   237→        budget=noise_budget,
   238→        global_budget=global_noise_budget,
   239→    )
   240→    return (
   241→        surfaced_matches,
   242→        hidden_by_detector,
   243→        noise_budget,
   244→        global_noise_budget,
   245→        budget_warning,
   246→    )
   247→
   248→
   249→__all__ = [
   250→    "ResolvedEntity",
   251→    "_detector_names_hint",
   252→    "_lookup_dimension_score",
   253→    "load_matches",
   254→    "resolve_entity",
   255→    "resolve_noise",
   256→    "resolve_show_scope",
   257→]
   258→
```

```
    28→def _primary_lang_from_issues(issues: dict) -> str | None:
    29→    counts: dict[str, int] = {}
    30→    for issue in issues.values():
    31→        if not isinstance(issue, dict):
    32→            continue
    33→        raw_lang = issue.get("lang")
    34→        if not isinstance(raw_lang, str) or not raw_lang.strip():
    35→            continue
    36→        key = raw_lang.strip().lower()
    37→        counts[key] = counts.get(key, 0) + 1
    38→    if not counts:

    40→    return max(counts, key=counts.get)

    57→        if isinstance(assessment, dict)

    59→    )

    61→        assessment.get("integrity_penalty")
    62→        if isinstance(assessment, dict)

    67→        and (
    68→            assessment.get("reset_by") == "scan_reset_subjective"
    69→            or assessment.get("source") == "scan_reset_subjective"

    73→    if reset_pending:

    75→        pass_rate = 0.0
    76→    elif integrity_penalty == "target_match_reset":

    78→        pass_rate = 0.0
    79→    elif has_assessment:

    81→        pass_rate = score / 100.0
    82→    else:

    84→        pass_rate = 0.0
```

```
    79→    right = normalize_word_set(b)
    80→    if not left or not right:
    81→        return 0.0
    82→    overlap = len(left & right)
    83→    union = len(left | right)
    84→    return float(overlap) / float(union) if union else 0.0

    92→    if not candidate or candidate in {"holistic", "changed", "stale", "unreviewed"}:
```

```
1→"""Import resolvers for compiled/backend-oriented languages."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→
     7→from .resolver_cache import read_go_module_path
     8→
     9→
    10→def resolve_go_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
    11→    """Resolve Go imports to local files."""
    12→    del source_file
    13→    if not import_text or "/" not in import_text:
    14→        return None
    15→
    16→    go_mod = os.path.join(scan_path, "go.mod")
    17→    module_path = read_go_module_path(go_mod)
    18→    if not module_path or not import_text.startswith(module_path):
    19→        return None
    20→
    21→    rel_path = import_text[len(module_path) :].lstrip("/")
    22→    candidate_dir = os.path.join(scan_path, rel_path)
    23→    if os.path.isdir(candidate_dir):
    24→        for filename in sorted(os.listdir(candidate_dir)):
    25→            if filename.endswith(".go") and not filename.endswith("_test.go"):
    26→                return os.path.join(candidate_dir, filename)
    27→    return None
    28→
    29→
    30→def resolve_rust_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
    31→    """Resolve Rust use declarations to local files."""
    32→    del source_file
    33→    if not import_text.startswith("crate::"):
    34→        return None
    35→
    36→    parts = import_text[len("crate::") :].split("::")
    37→    if not parts:
    38→        return None
    39→
    40→    src_dir = os.path.join(scan_path, "src")
    41→    if not os.path.isdir(src_dir):
    42→        src_dir = scan_path
    43→
    44→    path_parts = parts[:-1] if len(parts) > 1 else parts
    45→    candidate = os.path.join(src_dir, *path_parts) + ".rs"
    46→    if os.path.isfile(candidate):
    47→        return candidate
    48→
    49→    candidate = os.path.join(src_dir, *path_parts, "mod.rs")
    50→    if os.path.isfile(candidate):
    51→        return candidate
    52→
    53→    candidate = os.path.join(src_dir, *parts) + ".rs"
    54→    if os.path.isfile(candidate):
    55→        return candidate
    56→    return None
    57→
    58→
    59→def resolve_java_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
    60→    """Resolve Java imports to local files."""
    61→    del source_file
    62→    if import_text.endswith(".*"):
    63→        return None
    64→
    65→    parts = import_text.split(".")
    66→    if len(parts) < 2:
    67→        return None
    68→
    69→    rel_path = os.path.join(*parts[:-1], parts[-1] + ".java")
    70→    for src_root in ["src/main/java", "src", "app/src/main/java", "."]:
    71→        candidate = os.path.join(scan_path, src_root, rel_path)
    72→        if os.path.isfile(candidate):
    73→            return candidate
    74→    return None
    75→
    76→
    77→def resolve_kotlin_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
    78→    """Resolve Kotlin imports (same structure as Java)."""
    79→    del source_file
    80→    if import_text.endswith(".*"):
    81→        return None
    82→
    83→    parts = import_text.split(".")
    84→    if len(parts) < 2:
    85→        return None
    86→
    87→    for ext in (".kt", ".kts"):
    88→        rel_path = os.path.join(*parts[:-1], parts[-1] + ext)
    89→        for src_root in ["src/main/kotlin", "src/main/java", "src", "app/src/main/kotlin", "."]:
    90→            candidate = os.path.join(scan_path, src_root, rel_path)
    91→            if os.path.isfile(candidate):
    92→                return candidate
    93→    return None
    94→
    95→
    96→def resolve_cxx_include(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
    97→    """Resolve C/C++ quoted includes to local files."""
    98→    if not import_text:
    99→        return None
   100→
   101→    base = os.path.dirname(source_file)
   102→    candidate = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(base, import_text))
   103→    if os.path.isfile(candidate):
   104→        return candidate
   105→
   106→    for inc_dir in ["include", "src", "."]:
   107→        candidate = os.path.join(scan_path, inc_dir, import_text)
   108→        if os.path.isfile(candidate):
   109→            return candidate
   110→    return None
   111→
   112→
   113→def resolve_csharp_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
   114→    """Resolve C# using statements to local files."""
   115→    del source_file
   116→    parts = import_text.split(".")
   117→    if len(parts) < 2:
   118→        return None
   119→
   120→    filename = parts[-1] + ".cs"
   121→    for src_root in ["src", ".", "lib"]:
   122→        rel_path = os.path.join(*parts[:-1], filename)
   123→        candidate = os.path.join(scan_path, src_root, rel_path)
   124→        if os.path.isfile(candidate):
   125→            return candidate
   126→        candidate = os.path.join(scan_path, src_root, filename)
   127→        if os.path.isfile(candidate):
   128→            return candidate
   129→    return None
   130→
   131→
   132→def resolve_dart_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
   133→    """Resolve Dart imports to local files."""
   134→    if import_text.startswith("dart:"):
   135→        return None
   136→    if import_text.startswith("package:"):
   137→        parts = import_text[len("package:") :].split("/", 1)
   138→        if len(parts) < 2:
   139→            return None
   140→        candidate = os.path.join(scan_path, "lib", parts[1])
   141→        return candidate if os.path.isfile(candidate) else None
   142→
   143→    base = os.path.dirname(source_file)
   144→    candidate = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(base, import_text))
   145→    return candidate if os.path.isfile(candidate) else None
   146→
   147→
   148→def resolve_scala_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
   149→    """Resolve Scala imports to local files."""
   150→    del source_file
   151→    if import_text.endswith("._") or import_text.endswith(".{"):
   152→        return None
   153→
   154→    parts = import_text.split(".")
   155→    if len(parts) < 2:
   156→        return None
   157→
   158→    rel_path = os.path.join(*parts[:-1], parts[-1] + ".scala")
   159→    for src_root in ["src/main/scala", "src", "."]:
   160→        candidate = os.path.join(scan_path, src_root, rel_path)
   161→        if os.path.isfile(candidate):
   162→            return candidate
   163→    return None
   164→
   165→
   166→def resolve_swift_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
   167→    """Best-effort local Swift import resolution."""
   168→    text = import_text.strip()
   169→    if not text:
   170→        return None
   171→
   172→    module_path = text.replace(".", os.sep)
   173→    leaf = module_path.split(os.sep)[-1]
   174→    source_dir = os.path.dirname(source_file)
   175→    candidates = [
   176→        os.path.join(source_dir, module_path + ".swift"),
   177→        os.path.join(scan_path, module_path + ".swift"),
   178→        os.path.join(scan_path, "Sources", module_path + ".swift"),
   179→        os.path.join(scan_path, "Sources", module_path, f"{leaf}.swift"),
   180→        os.path.join(scan_path, module_path, f"{leaf}.swift"),
   181→    ]
   182→    seen: set[str] = set()
   183→    for candidate in candidates:
   184→        if candidate in seen:
   185→            continue
   186→        seen.add(candidate)
   187→        if os.path.isfile(candidate):
   188→            return candidate
   189→    return None
   190→
```

```
def resolve_go_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
    """Resolve Go imports to local files."""
    del source_file
    if not import_text or "/" not in import_text:
        return None

    go_mod = os.path.join(scan_path, "go.mod")
    module_path = read_go_module_path(go_mod)
    if not module_path or not import_text.startswith(module_path):
        return None

    rel_path = import_text[len(module_path) :].lstrip("/")
    candidate_dir = os.path.join(scan_path, rel_path)
    if os.path.isdir(candidate_dir):
        for filename in sorted(os.listdir(candidate_dir)):
            if filename.endswith(".go") and not filename.endswith("_test.go"):
                return os.path.join(candidate_dir, filename)
    return None


def resolve_rust_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
    """Resolve Rust use declarations to local files."""
    del source_file
    if not import_text.startswith("crate::"):
        return None

    parts = import_text[len("crate::") :].split("::")
    if not parts:
        return None

    src_dir = os.path.join(scan_path, "src")
    if not os.path.isdir(src_dir):
        src_dir = scan_path

    path_parts = parts[:-1] if len(parts) > 1 else parts
    candidate = os.path.join(src_dir, *path_parts) + ".rs"
    if os.path.isfile(candidate):
        return candidate

    candidate = os.path.join(src_dir, *path_parts, "mod.rs")
    if os.path.isfile(candidate):
        return candidate

    candidate = os.path.join(src_dir, *parts) + ".rs"
    if os.path.isfile(candidate):
        return candidate
    return None


def resolve_java_import(import_text: str, source_file: str, scan_path: str) -> str | None:
    """Resolve Java imports to local files."""
```

```
    22→def _load_json_payload_from_path(path: Path) -> dict:

    43→
    44→def _override_filename(filename: str) -> str:
    45→    """Convert ``foo.json`` to ``foo.override.json``."""
    46→    if not filename.endswith(".json"):

    56→def _validate_optional_string_list(value: object, *, context: str) -> list[str]:
    57→    """Validate an optional list of strings (empty allowed)."""
    58→    if value is None:
    59→        return []
    60→    if not isinstance(value, list):
    61→        raise ValueError(f"{context} must be a list of strings")
    62→    out: list[str] = []
    63→    for idx, item in enumerate(value):
    64→        if not isinstance(item, str) or not item.strip():

    69→
    70→def _apply_dimensions_override(
    71→    base_payload: dict,
    72→    override_payload: dict,
    73→    *,

    77→    """Apply a language override payload to a base dimensions payload."""

    90→    actual = set(override_payload)

    92→    if extra:

   108→    remove_dims = set(

   113→    )
   114→    if remove_dims:

   116→    out[dims_key] = dims
   117→
   118→    if "dimension_prompts" in override_payload:

   120→        if not isinstance(prompt_overrides, dict):
   121→            raise ValueError(f"{context}.dimension_prompts must be an object")
   122→        prompts = dict(out.get("dimension_prompts", {}))

   125→        out["dimension_prompts"] = prompts
   126→
   127→    remove_prompts = _validate_optional_string_list(
   128→        override_payload.get("dimension_prompts_remove"),

   130→    )
   131→    if remove_prompts:
   132→        prompts = dict(out.get("dimension_prompts", {}))
   133→        for dim_name in remove_prompts:

   136→
   137→    if "system_prompt" in override_payload:
   138→        out["system_prompt"] = override_payload["system_prompt"]
   139→    if "system_prompt_append" in override_payload:
   140→        suffix = override_payload["system_prompt_append"]
   141→        if not isinstance(suffix, str):

   143→        current = out.get("system_prompt", "")

   145→        out["system_prompt"] = f"{current}{sep}{suffix}"
   146→
   147→    return out

   149→
   150→def _load_payload_for_lang(
   151→    lang_name: str,
   152→    filename: str,
   153→    *,
   154→    dims_key: str,
   155→) -> tuple[dict, str]:
   156→    """Load payload for a language using shared-base + optional language overlay.
   157→
   158→    Resolution order:
   159→    1) Start from shared base payload in ``lang/framework/review_data``.
   160→    2) If language override payload exists, patch the selected base.
   161→    """
   162→    base_payload = _load_json_payload(filename)
   163→    context = filename
   164→
   165→    lang_override_path = _lang_payload_path(lang_name, _override_filename(filename))
   166→    if lang_override_path.is_file():
   167→        override_payload = _load_json_payload_from_path(lang_override_path)
   168→        base_payload = _apply_dimensions_override(
   169→            base_payload,
   170→            override_payload,
   171→            dims_key=dims_key,
   172→            context=str(lang_override_path),
   173→        )
   174→        context = f"{context} + {lang_override_path}"
   175→
   176→    return base_payload, context
   177→
   178→
   179→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   180→# Canonical loaders — use these for new code.
   181→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

   184→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)

   187→    payload = _load_json_payload(_DIMENSIONS_FILE)
   188→    dims, prompts, system_prompt = parse_dimensions_payload(
   189→        payload, context_prefix=_DIMENSIONS_FILE
   190→    )
   191→    return dims, prompts, system_prompt
   192→
   193→
   194→@lru_cache(maxsize=16)
   195→def load_dimensions_for_lang(
   196→    lang_name: str,
   197→) -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict[str, object]], str]:

   204→    dims, prompts, system_prompt = parse_dimensions_payload(

   206→    )
   207→    return dims, prompts, system_prompt
```

```
   467→def _validate_run_dir(run_dir: Path) -> tuple[dict, Path, str]:
   468→    """Validate run directory, load summary, and return (summary, blind_packet_path, immutable_packet_path).
   469→
   470→    Raises CommandError on any validation failure.
   471→    """
   472→    if not run_dir.is_dir():
   473→        raise CommandError(f"run directory not found: {run_dir}", exit_code=1)
   474→
   475→    summary_path = run_dir / "run_summary.json"
   476→    if not summary_path.exists():
   477→        raise CommandError(f"no run_summary.json in {run_dir}", exit_code=1)
   478→    try:
   479→        summary = json.loads(summary_path.read_text())
   480→    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
   481→        raise CommandError(f"Error reading run summary: {exc}", exit_code=1) from exc
   482→
   483→    successful = summary.get("successful_batches", [])
   484→    blind_packet_path = Path(str(summary.get("blind_packet", "")))
   485→    immutable_packet_path = str(summary.get("immutable_packet", ""))
   486→
   487→    if not successful:
   488→        raise CommandError("no successful batches in run summary.", exit_code=1)
   489→    if not blind_packet_path.exists():
   490→        raise PacketValidationError(f"blind packet not found: {blind_packet_path}", exit_code=1)
   491→
   492→    try:
   493→        packet = json.loads(Path(immutable_packet_path).read_text())
   494→    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
   495→        raise PacketValidationError(f"Error reading immutable packet: {exc}", exit_code=1) from exc
   496→
   497→    summary["_packet"] = packet
   498→    return summary, blind_packet_path, immutable_packet_path
   499→

   501→def do_import_run(
   502→    run_dir_path: str,
   503→    state: dict,

   512→) -> None:
   513→    """Re-import results from a completed run directory.
   514→
   515→    Replays the merge+provenance+import step that normally runs at the end of
   516→    ``--run-batches``.  Useful when the original pipeline was interrupted (e.g.
   517→    broken pipe from background execution) but all batch results completed.
   518→    """
   519→    run_dir = Path(run_dir_path)

   524→    successful = summary.get("successful_batches", [])

   527→

   533→        for idx in selected_indexes

   537→    if missing:
   538→        raise CommandError(f"missing result files for batches: {missing}", exit_code=1)
   539→
   540→    batch_results, failures = collect_batch_results(
   541→        selected_indexes=selected_indexes,

   543→        output_files=output_files,
```

Perfect! Now I have a thorough understanding. Let me create a comprehensive report of my findings:

## Comprehensive Research Report: Scoring Floor Logic in Desloppify

I've thoroughly analyzed the scoring floor logic in `desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/scoring.py` and related files. Here are my findings:

### 1. **Floor Calculation (Line 160)**

The floor is computed in the `merge_scores()` method:

```python
floor = min(score_raw_by_dim.get(key, [weighted_mean]))
```

**How it works:**
- `score_raw_by_dim` is a dict mapping dimension names to lists of raw individual assessment scores
- The floor takes the **minimum** of all individual file/batch scores for that dimension
- If no raw scores exist, it falls back to the weighted_mean itself
- This creates a hard floor that pulls down the final merged score to never exceed the lowest individual assessment

**The Gaming Problem:**
When merging two batches where one has a score of 45 (poor) and another has 92 (good):
- Without merging: floor = 45, weighted_mean ≈ 68, floor_aware = 0.7(68) + 0.3(45) = 61.1
- With strategic merging of the bad assessment into good files: the bad score could be eliminated from `score_raw_by_dim`, making floor = 92, eliminating the penalty entirely

### 2. **Blending Constants (Lines 23-24)**

```python
_WEIGHTED_MEAN_BLEND = 0.7      # 70% weight to the weighted mean
_FLOOR_BLEND_WEIGHT = 0.3       # 30% weight to the floor
```

**What they do:**
In the `score_dimension()` method (lines 112-115), the floor-aware score is computed as:
```python
floor_aware = (
    _WEIGHTED_MEAN_BLEND * inputs.weighted_mean
    + _FLOOR_BLEND_WEIGHT * inputs.floor
)
```

This blends the weighted average (70%) with the floor (30%), but since the floor is simply `min()`, a single low score dominates the calculation.

### 3. **score_raw_by_dim Accumulation (Lines 54-76 in core_merge_support.py)**

```python
def _accumulate_batch_scores(result, *, score_buckets, score_raw_by_dim, ...):
    for key, score in result["assessments"].items():
        score_buckets.setdefault(key, []).append((score_value, weight))
        score_raw_by_dim.setdefault(key, []).append(score_value)
```

**How it's populated:**
- Called once per batch result during merging
- Each dimension's assessment score is appended to `score_raw_by_dim[dimension]`
- This list is later used to calculate the floor via `min()`

### 4. **Full Score Computation Flow**

The complete flow in `score_dimension()` (lines 110-143):

```python
def score_dimension(self, inputs: ScoreInputs) -> ScoreBreakdown:
    # 1. Blend weighted mean and floor
    floor_aware = (
        _WEIGHTED_MEAN_BLEND * inputs.weighted_mean
        + _FLOOR_BLEND_WEIGHT * inputs.floor
    )
    
    # 2. Apply issue penalties (pressure-based)
    issue_penalty = min(
        _MAX_ISSUE_PENALTY,
        (inputs.issue_pressure * _PRESSURE_PENALTY_MULTIPLIER)
        + (max(inputs.issue_count - 1, 0) * _EXTRA_ISSUE_PENALTY),
    )
    issue_adjusted = floor_aware - issue_penalty
    
    # 3. Apply issue cap (prevents high scores with many issues)
    if inputs.issue_count > 0:
        cap_penalty = (
            (inputs.issue_pressure * _CAP_PRESSURE_MULTIPLIER)
            + (max(inputs.issue_count - 1, 0) * _EXTRA_ISSUE_PENALTY)
        )
        issue_cap = max(_CAP_FLOOR, _CAP_CEILING - cap_penalty)
        issue_adjusted = min(issue_adjusted, issue_cap)
    
    # 4. Clamp to [0, 100]
    final_score = round(max(0.0, min(100.0, issue_adjusted)), 1)
```

### 5. **Related Constants**

From lines 26-38:
```python
_MAX_ISSUE_PENALTY = 24.0              # Max total penalty
_PRESSURE_PENALTY_MULTIPLIER = 2.2     # Penalty per severity unit
_EXTRA_ISSUE_PENALTY = 0.8             # Extra penalty per additional issue
_CAP_FLOOR = 60.0                      # Minimum score when issues exist
_CAP_CEILING = 90.0                    # Max before penalty reduction
_CAP_PRESSURE_MULTIPLIER = 3.5         # Cap reduction per severity unit
```

### 6. **The Vulnerability**

The floor calculation is vulnerable because:
- **It's binary**: Either a score is included in the list or it isn't
- **Min aggregation**: A single outlier (intentionally or accidentally) crushes the entire dimension
- **No dampening**: Unlike the issue penalty system which has a max cap and multiplier, the floor has no scaling
- **Mergeability**: Batch deduplication logic could theoretically exclude bad assessments without semantic justification

### 7. **Proposed Fix: Percentile-Based Floor**

Instead of:
```python
floor = min(score_raw_by_dim.get(key, [weighted_mean]))
```

Use a percentile-based approach (bottom 10% of weight):
```python
def percentile_floor(scores: list[float], weights: list[float], percentile: float = 0.1) -> float:
    """Compute floor from weighted percentile (bottom N% of weight)."""
    if not scores:
        return scores[0] if scores else 50.0
    
    # Pair scores with their weights and sort ascending
    pairs = sorted(zip(scores, weights), key=lambda x: x[0])
    cumulative_weight = 0.0
    total_weight = sum(weights)
    threshold = total_weight * percentile
    
    # Find score where we've accumulated 10% of weight
    for score, weight in pairs:
        cumulative_weight += weight
        if cumulative_weight >= threshold:
            return score
    
    # Fallback: return lowest score if threshold not reached
    return pairs[0][0]
```

This approach:
- Uses weighted percentile (respects assessment weight from evidence/issue count)
- Ignores outliers below the 10th percentile
- Prevents single low scores from gaming merges
- Scales with portfolio size (bigger batches have more stable floors)

### Files to Review

**Primary files analyzed:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/scoring.py` (174 lines)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/core_merge_support.py` (196 lines)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/merge.py` (226 lines)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/base/scoring_constants.py` (19 lines)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/review/test_review_batch_core_direct.py` (472 lines)

The fix should:
1. Modify the floor calculation in `scoring.py:160` to use percentile-based logic
2. Update `merge_scores()` to pass both raw scores AND their corresponding weights
3. Add test coverage for percentile floor behavior with edge cases
4. Update documentation to explain the percentile floor rationale

```
    44→class ImportPayloadLoadError(ValueError):
    45→    """Raised when review import payload parsing/validation fails."""
    46→
    47→    def __init__(self, errors: list[str]) -> None:
    48→        cleaned = [str(error).strip() for error in errors if str(error).strip()]
    49→        self.errors = cleaned
    50→        message = "; ".join(cleaned) if cleaned else "import payload validation failed"
    51→        super().__init__(message)
    52→
    53→
    54→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    55→class ImportLoadConfig:
    56→    """Config bundle for import payload parsing/validation options."""
    57→
    58→    lang_name: str | None = None
    59→    allow_partial: bool = False
    60→    trusted_assessment_source: bool = False
    61→    trusted_assessment_label: str | None = None
    62→    attested_external: bool = False
    63→    manual_override: bool = False
    64→    manual_attest: str | None = None
    65→
    66→
    67→def _normalize_import_payload_shape(
    68→    payload: dict[str, Any],
    69→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
    70→    """Normalize payload into required-key contract with strict type checks."""
    71→    errors: list[str] = []
    72→    issues = payload.get("issues")
    73→    if not isinstance(issues, list):
    74→        errors.append("issues must be a JSON array")
    75→        issues = []
    76→
    77→    assessments = payload.get("assessments")
    78→    if assessments is None:
    79→        assessments = {}
    80→    elif not isinstance(assessments, dict):
    81→        errors.append("assessments must be an object when provided")
    82→        assessments = {}
    83→
    84→    reviewed_files = payload.get("reviewed_files")
    85→    normalized_reviewed_files: list[str] = []
    86→    if reviewed_files is None:
    87→        normalized_reviewed_files = []
    88→    elif isinstance(reviewed_files, list):
    89→        normalized_reviewed_files = [
    90→            str(item).strip()
    91→            for item in reviewed_files
    92→            if isinstance(item, str) and str(item).strip()
    93→        ]
    94→    else:
    95→        errors.append("reviewed_files must be an array when provided")
    96→
    97→    review_scope = payload.get("review_scope")
    98→    if review_scope is None:
    99→        review_scope = {}
   100→    elif not isinstance(review_scope, dict):
   101→        errors.append("review_scope must be an object when provided")
   102→        review_scope = {}
   103→
   104→    provenance = payload.get("provenance")
   105→    if provenance is None:
   106→        provenance = {}
   107→    elif not isinstance(provenance, dict):
   108→        errors.append("provenance must be an object when provided")
   109→        provenance = {}
   110→
   111→    dimension_notes = payload.get("dimension_notes")
   112→    if dimension_notes is None:
   113→        dimension_notes = {}
   114→    elif not isinstance(dimension_notes, dict):
   115→        errors.append("dimension_notes must be an object when provided")
   116→        dimension_notes = {}
   117→
   118→    policy = payload.get(ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY)
   119→    normalized_policy = (
   120→        policy if isinstance(policy, dict) else AssessmentImportPolicyModel().to_dict()
   121→    )
   122→    if errors:
   123→        return None, errors
   124→    return (
   125→        {
   126→            "issues": issues,
   127→            "assessments": assessments,
   128→            "reviewed_files": normalized_reviewed_files,
   129→            "review_scope": review_scope,
   130→            "provenance": provenance,
   131→            "dimension_notes": dimension_notes,
   132→            [REDACTED],
   133→        },
   134→        [],
   135→    )
   136→
   137→
   138→def _parse_and_validate_import(
   139→    import_file: str,
   140→    *,
   141→    config: ImportLoadConfig,
   142→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   143→    """Load, parse, and validate a review import file.
   144→
   145→    This helper performs filesystem I/O by reading ``import_file`` before
   146→    normalizing and validating the payload.
   147→
   148→    Returns ``(data, errors)`` where *data* is the normalized payload on
   149→    success, or ``None`` when errors prevent import.
   150→    """
```

```
# ── Unused imports tests ──────────────────────────────────────


class TestUnusedImports:
    def test_unused_import_detected(self, tmp_path):
        from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._specs_compiled import GO_SPEC
        from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._unused_imports import (
            detect_unused_imports,
        )

        code = """\
package main

import "fmt"
import "os"

func main() {
    fmt.Println("hello")
}
"""
        f = tmp_path / "main.go"
        f.write_text(code)

        entries = detect_unused_imports([str(f)], GO_SPEC)
        # "os" is imported but never used.
        names = [e["name"] for e in entries]
        assert "os" in names
        # "fmt" IS used.
        assert "fmt" not in names

    def test_no_unused_imports(self, tmp_path):
        from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._specs_compiled import GO_SPEC
        from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._unused_imports import (
            detect_unused_imports,
        )

        code = """\
package main

import "fmt"

func main() {
    fmt.Println("hello")
}
"""
        f = tmp_path / "main.go"
        f.write_text(code)

        entries = detect_unused_imports([str(f)], GO_SPEC)
        assert len(entries) == 0

--

        lang = get_lang("go")

    def test_go_has_unused_imports_phase(self):
        import desloppify.languages.go  # noqa: F401
        from desloppify.languages._framework.resolution import get_lang

        lang = get_lang("go")
        labels = [p.label for p in lang.phases]
        assert "Unused imports" in labels

    def test_bash_has_no_unused_imports(self):
        """Bash has import_query but it resolves source commands.
        Check unused imports phase IS present for bash."""
        import desloppify.languages.bash  # noqa: F401
        from desloppify.languages._framework.resolution import get_lang

        lang = get_lang("bash")
        labels = [p.label for p in lang.phases]
        # Bash has an import_query, so it should have unused imports.
        assert "Unused imports" in labels
```

```
    52→class ImportPayloadLoadError(ValueError):
    53→    """Raised when review import payload parsing/validation fails."""
    54→

    56→        cleaned = [str(error).strip() for error in errors if str(error).strip()]
    57→        self.errors = cleaned
    58→        message = "; ".join(cleaned) if cleaned else "import payload validation failed"

    76→    options: ImportParseOptions | None = None,
```

```
   682→# ── Signature variance tests ─────────────────────────────────
   683→

   685→class TestSignatureVariance:
   686→    def test_detects_variance(self, tmp_path):
   687→        from desloppify.engine.detectors.signature import detect_signature_variance
   688→        from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._extractors import (
   689→            ts_extract_functions,
   690→        )
   691→        from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._specs_compiled import GO_SPEC
   692→
   693→        # Create 3 files with same function name but different params.
   694→        for i in range(3):
   695→            params = ", ".join(f"p{j} int" for j in range(i + 1))
   696→            body_lines = "\n".join(f"    x{j} := {j}" for j in range(5))
   697→            code = f"package main\n\nfunc process({params}) int {{\n{body_lines}\n    return 0\n}}\n"
   698→            (tmp_path / f"file{i}.go").write_text(code)
   699→
   700→        file_list = [str(tmp_path / f"file{i}.go") for i in range(3)]
   701→        functions = ts_extract_functions(tmp_path, GO_SPEC, file_list)
   702→
   703→        entries, total = detect_signature_variance(functions, min_occurrences=3)
   704→        # 3 occurrences of "process" with different param counts.
   705→        assert any(e["name"] == "process" for e in entries)
   706→
   707→    def test_no_variance_when_identical(self, tmp_path):
   708→        from desloppify.engine.detectors.signature import detect_signature_variance
   709→        from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._extractors import (
   710→            ts_extract_functions,
   711→        )
   712→        from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._specs_compiled import GO_SPEC
   713→
   714→        # Create 3 files with identical function signatures.
   715→        for i in range(3):
```

```
   216→def _feedback_dimensions_from_issues(issues: object) -> set[str]:
   217→    """Return dimensions with explicit improvement guidance in issues payload."""
   218→    if not isinstance(issues, list):
   219→        return set()
   220→    dims: set[str] = set()
   221→    for entry in issues:
   222→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   223→            continue
   224→        dim = entry.get("dimension")
   225→        if not isinstance(dim, str) or not dim.strip():
   226→            continue
   227→        suggestion = entry.get("suggestion")
   228→        if isinstance(suggestion, str) and suggestion.strip():
   229→            dims.add(dim.strip())
   230→    return dims
   231→
   232→
   233→def _feedback_dimensions_from_dimension_notes(dimension_notes: object) -> set[str]:
   234→    """Return dimensions with concrete review evidence in dimension_notes payload."""
   235→    if not isinstance(dimension_notes, dict):
   236→        return set()
   237→    dims: set[str] = set()
   238→    for dim, note in dimension_notes.items():
   239→        if not isinstance(dim, str) or not dim.strip():
   240→            continue
   241→        if not isinstance(note, dict):
   242→            continue
   243→        if not _has_non_empty_strings(note.get("evidence")):
   244→            continue
   245→        dims.add(dim.strip())
   246→    return dims
   247→
   248→
   249→def _validate_assessment_feedback(
   250→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   251→) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
   252→    """Return dimensions missing required feedback and required low-score issues."""
   253→    assessments = issues_data["assessments"]
   254→    if not assessments:
   255→        return [], []
   256→
   257→    issue_dims = _feedback_dimensions_from_issues(issues_data["issues"])
   258→    feedback_dims = set(issue_dims)
   259→    feedback_dims.update(
   260→        _feedback_dimensions_from_dimension_notes(issues_data["dimension_notes"])
   261→    )
   262→    missing_feedback: list[str] = []
   263→    missing_low_score_issues: list[str] = []
   264→    for dim_name, payload in assessments.items():
   265→        if not isinstance(dim_name, str) or not dim_name.strip():
   266→            continue
   267→        score = coerce_assessment_score(payload)
   268→        if score is None:
   269→            continue
   270→        if score_requires_dimension_issue(score) and dim_name not in issue_dims:
   271→            missing_low_score_issues.append(f"{dim_name} ({score:.1f})")
   272→        if score_requires_explicit_feedback(score) and dim_name not in feedback_dims:
   273→            missing_feedback.append(f"{dim_name} ({score:.1f})")
   274→    return sorted(missing_feedback), sorted(missing_low_score_issues)
   275→

   277→def _load_import_json(import_file: str) -> tuple[object | None, list[str]]:
   278→    """Read import file and parse JSON payload."""
   279→    issues_path = Path(import_file)
   280→    if not issues_path.exists():
   281→        return None, [f"file not found: {import_file}"]
   282→    try:
   283→        return json.loads(issues_path.read_text()), []
   284→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
   285→        return None, [f"error reading issues: {exc}"]
   286→
   287→
   288→def _normalize_import_root_payload(
   289→    raw_payload: object,
   290→) -> tuple[ImportRootPayload | None, list[str]]:
   291→    """Normalize top-level payload shape before strict field validation."""
   292→    payload = {"issues": raw_payload} if isinstance(raw_payload, list) else raw_payload
   293→    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
   294→        return None, ["issues file must contain a JSON array or object"]
   295→
   296→    key_error = normalize_legacy_findings_alias(

   298→        missing_issues_error="issues object must contain an 'issues' key",
   299→    )
   300→    if key_error is not None:
   301→        return None, [key_error]
   302→    return payload, []
   303→
   304→
   305→def _validate_override_option_conflicts(
   306→    options: ImportParseOptions,
   307→    *,
   308→    override_enabled: bool,
   309→) -> list[str]:
   310→    """Validate mutually exclusive override/attestation option combinations."""
   311→    if options.attested_external and override_enabled:
   312→        return ["--attested-external cannot be combined with --manual-override"]
   313→    if options.attested_external and options.allow_partial:
   314→        return [
   315→            "--attested-external cannot be combined with --allow-partial; "
   316→            "attested score imports require fully valid issues payloads"
   317→        ]
   318→    if override_enabled and options.allow_partial:
   319→        return [
   320→            "--manual-override cannot be combined with --allow-partial; "
   321→            "manual score imports require fully valid issues payloads"
   322→        ]
   323→    return []
   324→
   325→
   326→def _validate_feedback_requirements(
   327→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   328→    *,
   329→    override_enabled: bool,
   330→    override_attest: str | None,
   331→) -> list[str]:
   332→    """Validate feedback and low-score issue requirements."""
   333→    missing_feedback, missing_low_score_issues = _validate_assessment_feedback(issues_data)
   334→    if missing_low_score_issues:
   335→        if override_enabled:
   336→            if not isinstance(override_attest, str) or not override_attest.strip():
   337→                return ["--manual-override requires --attest"]
   338→            return []
   339→        return [
   340→            f"assessments below {LOW_SCORE_ISSUE_THRESHOLD:.1f} must include at "
   341→            "least one issue for that same dimension with a concrete suggestion. "
   342→            f"Missing: {', '.join(missing_low_score_issues)}"
   343→        ]
   344→    if not missing_feedback:
   345→        return []
   346→    if override_enabled:
   347→        if not isinstance(override_attest, str) or not override_attest.strip():
   348→            return ["--manual-override requires --attest"]
   349→        return []
   350→    return [
   351→        f"assessments below {ASSESSMENT_FEEDBACK_THRESHOLD:.1f} must include explicit feedback "
   352→        "(issue with same dimension and non-empty suggestion, or "
   353→        "dimension_notes evidence for that dimension). "
   354→        f"Missing: {', '.join(missing_feedback)}"
   355→    ]
   356→
   357→
   358→def _validate_schema_requirements(
   359→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   360→    *,
   361→    lang_name: str | None,
   362→    allow_partial: bool,
   363→) -> list[str]:
   364→    """Validate holistic issue schema unless partial imports are enabled."""
   365→    schema_errors = _validate_holistic_issues_schema(issues_data, lang_name=lang_name)
   366→    if not schema_errors or allow_partial:
   367→        return []
   368→    visible_errors = schema_errors[:10]
   369→    remaining = len(schema_errors) - len(visible_errors)
   370→    errors = [
   371→        "issues schema validation failed for holistic import. "
   372→        "Fix payload or rerun with --allow-partial to continue."
   373→    ]
   374→    errors.extend(visible_errors)
   375→    if remaining > 0:
   376→        errors.append(f"... {remaining} additional schema error(s) omitted")
   377→    return errors
   378→
   379→
   380→def _parse_and_validate_import(
   381→    import_file: str,
   382→    *,
   383→    options: ImportParseOptions | None = None,
   384→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   385→    """Load, parse, and validate a review import file with filesystem I/O."""
   386→    resolved_options = _coerce_import_parse_options(options)
   387→
   388→    raw_payload, load_errors = _load_import_json(import_file)
   389→    if load_errors:
   390→        return None, load_errors
   391→    normalized_root, root_errors = _normalize_import_root_payload(raw_payload)
   392→    if root_errors:
   393→        return None, root_errors
   394→    if normalized_root is None:
   395→        return None, ["issues payload root normalization returned no data"]
   396→
   397→    normalized_issues_data, shape_errors = _normalize_import_payload_shape(normalized_root)
   398→    if shape_errors:
   399→        return None, shape_errors
```

```
    76→        # Formatter truncates to 80 chars

    81→            "fn_a": {"name": "foo", "line": 10},
    82→            "fn_b": {"name": "bar", "line": 20},
    83→        }
    84→        parts = format_detail(detail)
    85→        pair_part = [p for p in parts if "foo" in p and "bar" in p]
    86→        assert len(pair_part) == 1
    87→        assert "10" in pair_part[0]
    88→        assert "20" in pair_part[0]

    90→    def test_dupe_pair_missing_line(self):

    94→        }
    95→        parts = format_detail(detail)
    96→        pair_part = [p for p in parts if "foo" in p and "bar" in p]
    97→        assert len(pair_part) == 1

    99→    def test_patterns_used_formatter(self):
   100→        parts = format_detail({"patterns_used": ["singleton", "factory"]})
```

```
1→"""Compatibility entrypoint for the TypeScript unused-import fixer."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections import defaultdict
     6→
     7→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import FixResult
     8→
     9→from .fixer_io import apply_fixer
    10→from .import_rewrite import process_unused_import_lines
    11→
    12→
    13→def fix_unused_imports(entries: list[dict], *, dry_run: bool = False) -> FixResult:
    14→    """Remove unused imports from source files."""
    15→    import_entries = [entry for entry in entries if entry.get("category") == "imports"]
    16→
    17→    def transform(lines: list[str], file_entries: list[dict[str, object]]) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
    18→        unused_symbols = {str(entry["name"]) for entry in file_entries if "name" in entry}
    19→        unused_by_line: dict[int, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
    20→        for entry in file_entries:
    21→            line = entry.get("line")
    22→            name = entry.get("name")
    23→            if isinstance(line, int) and name is not None:
    24→                unused_by_line[line].append(str(name))
    25→
    26→        new_lines, removed_symbols = process_unused_import_lines(lines, unused_symbols, unused_by_line)
    27→        removed: list[str] = []
    28→        for entry in file_entries:
    29→            name = str(entry.get("name", ""))
    30→            if name in removed_symbols and name not in removed:
    31→                removed.append(name)
    32→        return new_lines, removed
    33→
    34→    return FixResult(entries=apply_fixer(import_entries, transform, dry_run=dry_run))
    35→
    36→
    37→__all__ = ["fix_unused_imports"]
    38→
```

```
1→"""Tree-sitter based unused import detection.
     2→
     3→Cross-references parsed import statements against file body to find
     4→imports whose names don't appear elsewhere in the file.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import logging
    10→import re
    11→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
    12→
    13→from .. import PARSE_INIT_ERRORS
    14→from ..imports.cache import _PARSE_CACHE
    15→from .extractors import _get_parser, _make_query, _node_text, _run_query, _unwrap_node
    16→
    17→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    18→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    19→
    20→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→
    23→def detect_unused_imports(
    24→    file_list: list[str],
    25→    spec: TreeSitterLangSpec,
    26→) -> list[dict]:
    27→    """Find imports whose names are not referenced elsewhere in the file.
    28→
    29→    Returns list of {file, line, name} entries.
    30→    """
    31→    if not spec.import_query:
    32→        return []
    33→
    34→    try:
    35→        parser, language = _get_parser(spec.grammar)
    36→    except PARSE_INIT_ERRORS as exc:
    37→        logger.debug("tree-sitter init failed: %s", exc)
    38→        return []
    39→
    40→    query = _make_query(language, spec.import_query)
    41→    entries: list[dict] = []
    42→
    43→    for filepath in file_list:
    44→        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
    45→        if cached is None:
    46→            continue
    47→        source, tree = cached
    48→        source_text = source.decode("utf-8", errors="replace")
    49→
    50→        matches = _run_query(query, tree.root_node)
    51→        if not matches:
    52→            continue
    53→
    54→        for _pattern_idx, captures in matches:
    55→            import_node = _unwrap_node(captures.get("import"))
    56→            path_node = _unwrap_node(captures.get("path"))
    57→            if not import_node or not path_node:
    58→                continue
    59→
    60→            raw_path = _node_text(path_node).strip("\"'`")
    61→            if not raw_path:
    62→                continue
    63→
    64→            # Check for alias (e.g. PHP ``use Foo as Bar``, Python ``import X as Y``).
    65→            # When an alias is present, search for the alias name instead.
    66→            alias_name = _extract_alias(import_node)
    67→
    68→            # Extract the imported name from the path.
    69→            name = alias_name or _extract_import_name(raw_path)
    70→            if not name:
    71→                continue
    72→
    73→            # Get the import statement's line range so we can exclude it
    74→            # from the search.
    75→            import_start = import_node.start_byte
    76→            import_end = import_node.end_byte
    77→
    78→            # Build text without the import statement itself.
    79→            rest = source_text[:import_start] + source_text[import_end:]
    80→
    81→            # Check if the name appears in the rest of the file.
    82→            if not re.search(r'\b' + re.escape(name) + r'\b', rest):
    83→                entries.append({
    84→                    "file": filepath,
    85→                    "line": import_node.start_point[0] + 1,
    86→                    "name": name,
    87→                })
    88→
    89→    return entries
    90→
    91→
    92→def _extract_alias(import_node) -> str | None:
    93→    """Extract alias name from ``use Foo as Bar`` or ``import X as Y``.
    94→
    95→    Walks children of the import node looking for an alias/name node
    96→    after an ``as`` keyword. Returns the alias text or None.
    97→    """
    98→    found_as = False
    99→    for child in _iter_children(import_node):
   100→        text = _node_text(child)
   101→        if text == "as":
   102→            found_as = True
   103→            continue
   104→        # The node immediately after "as" is the alias name.
   105→        if found_as and child.type in ("name", "identifier", "namespace_name"):
   106→            return _node_text(child)
   107→    return None
   108→
   109→
   110→def _iter_children(node):
   111→    """Recursively yield terminal-ish children relevant to alias extraction.
   112→
   113→    Only descends into namespace_use_clause / import_clause nodes (the
   114→    immediate import container) — avoids descending into unrelated subtrees.
   115→    """
   116→    for i in range(node.child_count):
   117→        child = node.children[i]
   118→        # Yield leaf-like nodes (keywords, identifiers).
   119→        if child.child_count == 0:
   120→            yield child
   121→        elif child.type in (
   122→            "namespace_use_clause", "import_clause",
   123→            "namespace_alias", "as_pattern",
   124→        ):
   125→            yield from _iter_children(child)
   126→
   127→
   128→def _extract_import_name(import_path: str) -> str:
   129→    """Extract the usable name from an import path.
   130→
   131→    Examples:
   132→        "fmt" -> "fmt"
   133→        "./utils" -> "utils"
   134→        "crate::module::Foo" -> "Foo"
   135→        "com.example.MyClass" -> "MyClass"
   136→        "MyApp::Model::User" -> "User"
   137→        "Data.List" -> "List"
   138→    """
   139→    # Strip common path separators and take the last segment.
   140→    for sep in ("::", ".", "/", "\\"):
   141→        if sep in import_path:
   142→            parts = import_path.split(sep)
   143→            # Filter out empty segments and take the last.
   144→            parts = [p for p in parts if p]
   145→            if parts:
   146→                name = parts[-1]
   147→                # Strip file extensions.
   148→                for ext in (".go", ".rs", ".rb", ".py", ".js", ".jsx", ".ts",
   149→                            ".tsx", ".java", ".kt", ".cs", ".fs", ".ml",
   150→                            ".ex", ".erl", ".hs", ".lua", ".zig", ".pm",
   151→                            ".sh", ".pl", ".scala", ".swift", ".php",
   152→                            ".dart", ".mjs", ".cjs"):
   153→                    if name.endswith(ext):
   154→                        name = name[:-len(ext)]
   155→                        break
   156→                return name
   157→
   158→    # No separator — the path itself is the name.
   159→    return import_path
   160→
   161→
   162→__all__ = ["detect_unused_imports"]
   163→
```
