Perfect. Now let me generate a comprehensive research report on the concurrent state mutation issue:

## Research Report: Concurrent State Mutation Issue #254

### Summary
The desloppify codebase has **no file-level locking** around state read-modify-write operations, creating a race condition when multiple commands execute concurrently. The current atomic write mechanism (temp + os.replace) only protects the final write operation, not the entire read-modify-write cycle.

---

### 1. State Loading & Saving Architecture

#### **Location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py`

**Load Function** (`load_state`, lines 64-151):
- Reads state from disk (default: `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`)
- Includes fallback to backup (`.json.bak`) on corruption
- Returns empty state on missing/corrupted files
- **No locking during load**

**Save Function** (`save_state`, lines 174-212):
- Calls `_recompute_stats()` to refresh scores
- Validates state invariants
- **Atomic write via `safe_write_text()`** (temp file + os.replace)
- Creates `.json.bak` backup before writing (best-effort, can fail silently)

#### **Atomic Write Mechanism**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/base/discovery/file_paths.py` (lines 94-106)

```python
def safe_write_text(filepath: str | Path, content: str) -> None:
    """Atomically write text to a file using temp+rename."""
    p = Path(filepath)
    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=p.parent, suffix=".tmp")
    try:
        with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            f.write(content)
        os.replace(tmp, str(p))  # <-- Atomic at OS level
    except OSError:
        if os.path.exists(tmp):
            os.unlink(tmp)
        raise
```

**Issue**: The atomic write protects only the final `os.replace()` operation, **not** the entire load→modify→save cycle.

---

### 2. State Mutation Points in Commands

Eight command modules call `save_state()`:

1. **`review/importing/cmd.py`** (lines 173, 274):
   - `_persist_import_state()`: Load (via `_build_working_state()`) → modify working_state → `save_state()`
   - Can run concurrently with other commands

2. **`resolve/cmd.py`**:
   - `_load_state_with_guards()`: Load state
   - `_resolve_ids_with_snapshots()`: Modify state
   - `save_state_or_exit()`: Save state (line 116)

3. **`autofix/apply_flow.py`** (lines 83-94):
   - Load: `state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)`
   - Mutate: `_resolve_fixer_results(state, ...)`
   - Save twice: line 86, and line 94 after post-fix hooks

4. **`plan/override_io.py`, `plan/override_misc.py`, `plan/override_resolve_workflow.py`**:
   - Plan-related state modifications

5. **`review/merge.py`** (line 256):
   - Load: `runtime.state` (via `command_runtime()`)
   - Mutate: Merge duplicate issues in-place (lines 224-236)
   - Save: `save_state(state, state_file)`

6. **`scan/workflow.py`**:
   - Scan merges results into state

7. **`exclude.py`, `zone.py`**:
   - Minor state updates

---

### 3. The Race Condition Scenario

```
Timeline:
T0: Command A loads state (version 1)
T1: Command B loads state (version 1)
T2: Command A modifies state (resolves issue #123)
T3: Command B modifies state (imports assessment for #456)
T4: Command A saves state (version 2, has #123 resolved, MISSING #456 assessment)
T5: Command B saves state (version 3, has #456 assessment, MISSING #123 resolved)
     ↑ Lost update! A's changes lost.
```

**Critical Code Pattern** (seen repeatedly):
```python
# In review/merge.py, lines 150-256:
runtime = command_runtime(args)
state = runtime.state                      # T0: Load (NO LOCK)
state_file = runtime.state_path
# ... long processing (T1-T3 in other processes can execute) ...
save_state(state, state_file)              # T4: Save (ATOMIC, but cycle not atomic)
```

---

### 4. Current Safeguards (Insufficient)

**✓ Atomic final write**: `os.replace()` is atomic at the OS level (POSIX-compliant)
**✓ Backup creation**: `.json.bak` backup (but best-effort, can fail)
**✗ NO file-level locking**: No fcntl/flock around read-modify-write cycle
**✗ NO process coordination**: Multiple commands can read the same stale state
**✗ NO version/CAS checking**: No optimistic locking (compare-and-swap)

---

### 5. Affected Commands (Concurrent Execution Risk)

Commands that **mutate state** and can run in parallel:
1. `desloppify resolve` — marks issues as resolved/wontfix
2. `desloppify review import` — imports assessments
3. `desloppify review merge` — merges duplicate issues
4. `desloppify autofix` — auto-resolves issues
5. `desloppify plan override` — overrides plan entries
6. `desloppify scan` — merges new scan results

**Read-only commands** (safe in parallel):
- `show`, `status`, `next`, `plan` (query only, no mutations)

---

### 6. Fix Strategy Requirements

### Required: File-Level Locking

The state file itself needs a lock that protects the entire read-modify-write cycle:

```python
# Pseudocode for required pattern:
with file_lock(state_path):  # Exclusive lock
    state = load_state(state_path)
    # ... modify state (safe from concurrent reads) ...
    save_state(state, state_path)
    # Lock released: other processes can now load the final version
```

### Implementation Options:

**Option A: fcntl-based (POSIX, Unix/Linux/macOS)**
- `fcntl.flock()` on the state file descriptor
- Supports non-blocking acquire for user feedback
- Cross-platform via portalocker library

**Option B: Lock file + polling**
- Create `.state.lock` file during mutation
- Other processes wait/retry
- Less elegant but more portable

**Option C: Atomic file operations + version checking**
- Add version/timestamp to state
- Compare-and-swap on save
- More complex but fine-grained

### Recommended: **Option A (fcntl) with Option C (version safety)**

1. Use fcntl.flock for process-level coordination
2. Add optional state version/CAS validation for safety
3. Graceful degradation on lock timeout (user can retry)

---

### 7. Code Locations Requiring Changes

**Central location to add locking**:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py`
  - Wrap `load_state()` and `save_state()` with lock acquisition
  - OR create new `locked_read_modify_write()` context manager

**All command mutation sites** (at least 8 files):
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/cmd.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/merge.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/autofix/apply_flow.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_*.py` (3 files)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/workflow.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/exclude.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/zone.py`

**Helper location** (persist.py already wraps save):
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/persist.py` (lines 12-17)
  - Could be extended to handle locking

---

### 8. Key Files for Implementation Reference

| File | Purpose | Lines |
|------|---------|-------|
| `engine/_state/persistence.py` | load_state, save_state | 64-212 |
| `base/discovery/file_paths.py` | safe_write_text | 94-106 |
| `app/commands/helpers/persist.py` | CLI error wrapping | 12-17 |
| `app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` | resolve mutation | 50-116 |
| `app/commands/review/merge.py` | merge mutation | 148-268 |
| `app/commands/review/importing/cmd.py` | import mutation | 87-301 |
| `app/commands/autofix/apply_flow.py` | autofix mutation | 71-128 |

---

### 9. Data Files Protected

**State file location**: `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`
- Python: `.desloppify/state-python.json`
- TypeScript: `.desloppify/state-typescript.json`

**One lock per state file needed** (language-specific isolation is good, but intra-language races still possible)

---

### Conclusion

**Current status**: ❌ NOT thread-safe, ❌ NOT process-safe

The atomic write mechanism is **insufficient** because it only protects the final write operation, not the read-modify-write cycle. Concurrent commands can read stale state, modify it independently, and lose updates when they persist.

**Solution required**: Add file-level locking (fcntl or lock file) around the entire read-modify-write cycle in the persistence layer, with graceful timeout handling for user experience.

## Summary of Findings

I've completed a thorough exploration of the Go unused-import detector in desloppify. Here are my findings:

### 1. Go Language Plugin Directory Structure
**Location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/go/`

The Go plugin includes:
- `__init__.py` - Main configuration (GoConfig class)
- `extractors.py` - Function/method extraction for Go source files
- `phases.py` - Structural phase runner with complexity signals
- `commands.py` - Command registry for detect subcommands
- `detectors/deps.py` - Stub for Go dependency graph (returns empty)
- `review.py` - Review guidance and patterns
- `test_coverage.py` - Test coverage import mapping
- `fixers/` - Empty (no fixers yet)

### 2. Unused-Import Detector Location
The actual unused-import detection is **NOT** in the Go plugin, but in the shared tree-sitter framework:

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/treesitter/analysis/unused_imports.py`

This is a **language-agnostic detector** used by multiple compiled languages (Go, Rust, Java, Kotlin, C#, Swift, PHP, Dart, Scala).

### 3. Go Import Query Configuration
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/treesitter/specs/compiled.py` (lines 18-43)

```python
GO_SPEC = TreeSitterLangSpec(
    grammar="go",
    import_query="""
        (import_spec
            path: (interpreted_string_literal) @path) @import
    """,
    resolve_import=resolve_go_import,
    ...
)
```

The import query only captures `import_spec` nodes with `path` nodes.

### 4. The Bug: How Usage is Determined
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/treesitter/analysis/unused_imports.py` (lines 23-89)

The detection logic works as follows:

1. **Extract import name** (line 69): Takes the last segment of the import path
   - `"fmt"` → `"fmt"`
   - `"github.com/some/package"` → `"package"`

2. **Search file for usage** (line 82):
   ```python
   if not re.search(r'\b' + re.escape(name) + r'\b', rest):
       # Mark as unused
   ```

**THE BUG**: This is a **simple word-boundary regex search** that only looks for the imported name as an identifier anywhere in the source text.

### 5. Missing Scenarios - Why Interfaces, Generics, and Struct Fields Fail

The detector **DOES NOT** handle these cases properly:

#### A. Interface Method Signatures
```go
import "io"

// 'io' is used here, but only in a type declaration
type Reader interface {
    Read(p []byte) (n int, err io.Error)
}
```
The regex will find `io` in `io.Error`, so this should work. BUT if the import uses an alias or qualified name, it might not be found.

#### B. Generic Type Parameters
```go
import "container/list"

// 'list' used only in type parameter
func Process[T list.List](items []T) {
}
```
The detector searches for the package name `list` in the source. This SHOULD work, but the query might not capture all contexts.

#### C. Struct Field Types
```go
import "time"

type Event struct {
    // 'time' used only in field type
    Timestamp time.Time
    Duration  time.Duration
}
```
Again, the regex looks for `time` and should find it in `time.Time`. This should work.

**The real issue**: The algorithm searches for the extracted package **name** as a word boundary match. If:
- The import name doesn't match what appears in the code (e.g., nested packages)
- The import statement itself is malformed or uses aliases
- The code uses only the full qualified name without the short form

Then it will falsely flag as unused.

### 6. How It Determines If an Import is "Used"

**Location**: Lines 73-87 in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/treesitter/analysis/unused_imports.py`

**Algorithm**:
1. Remove the import statement itself from the source text
2. Use regex with word boundaries: `r'\b' + re.escape(name) + r'\b'`
3. If the name doesn't appear elsewhere, mark as unused

**What it scans**: The entire remaining file text - it doesn't distinguish between:
- Function bodies
- Type declarations
- Struct field types
- Interface method signatures
- Generic type parameters
- Comments
- String literals

It just does a dumb text search for the extracted import name.

### 7. Test Coverage
**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_treesitter_complexity_and_integration.py` (lines 615-679)

Current tests only cover:
- Basic unused imports (lines 619-643)
- No unused imports (lines 645-664)
- Missing import_query spec (lines 666-679)

**NO tests exist for**:
- Imports used only in interface signatures
- Imports used only in generic type parameters
- Imports used only in struct field types
- Qualified name usage vs short name usage

### Root Cause
The implementation is **overly simplistic** - it uses a basic regex word-boundary search. It doesn't:
1. Parse type annotations separately
2. Distinguish between value scope and type scope
3. Handle qualified names vs short names
4. Consider Go's package semantics (where the last segment is the package name)

The fix would require either:
1. **AST-based approach**: Use tree-sitter queries to specifically check for import usage in type annotations
2. **Context-aware regex**: Build separate patterns for type contexts (struct fields, interface methods, generic params)
3. **Go-specific analyzer**: Implement a proper Go type checker that understands import aliases and qualified names

## RESEARCH REPORT: Issue #378 Analysis

### ISSUE SUMMARY
`show subjective` suggests dimensions that are invalid for the language, while `review --run-batches` accepts them, but `review --import-run` rejects them. This creates an inconsistency where users are guided to dimensions that won't work.

---

### 1. WHERE `show subjective` GENERATES DIMENSION SUGGESTIONS

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/show/scope.py`**

In the `_resolve_subjective_dimension()` function (lines 61-84), dimensions are looked up using:
- `DISPLAY_NAMES.get(lowered)` — This pulls from the **universal DISPLAY_NAMES catalog** (line 66)
- Falls back to `state.get("dimension_scores")` for dynamically computed dimensions

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/base/subjective_dimension_catalog.py`**

The `DISPLAY_NAMES` dict (lines 5-29) contains **all subjective dimensions** that exist across all languages:
```python
DISPLAY_NAMES: dict[str, str] = {
    "cross_module_architecture": "Cross-module arch",
    "initialization_coupling": "Init coupling",
    "convention_outlier": "Convention drift",
    # ... 26+ more dimensions ...
    "naming_quality": "Naming quality",
    "logic_clarity": "Logic clarity",
    # etc.
}
```

**PROBLEM:** This is a **global, language-agnostic** list. The `show subjective` special view command does NOT filter by language.

---

### 2. WHERE DIMENSIONS ARE VALIDATED DURING IMPORT (`review --import-run`)

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/parse.py`**

In the `_validate_holistic_issues_schema()` function (lines 180-214):
```python
def _validate_holistic_issues_schema(
    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
    *,
    lang_name: str | None = None,
) -> list[str]:
    """Validate strict holistic issue schema expected by issue import."""
    issues = issues_data["issues"]

    allowed_dimensions: set[str] = set()
    if isinstance(lang_name, str) and lang_name.strip():
        _, dimension_prompts, _ = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name)  # <-- LANGUAGE-SPECIFIC
        allowed_dimensions = set(dimension_prompts)

    errors: list[str] = []
    for idx, entry in enumerate(issues):
        _normalized, entry_errors = validate_review_issue_payload(
            entry,
            label=f"issues[{idx}]",
            allowed_dimensions=allowed_dimensions or None,  # <-- VALIDATES AGAINST LANGUAGE
```

**KEY:** The validation uses `load_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name)` to get the **per-language** allowed dimensions set (line 190), then passes it to `validate_review_issue_payload()` which rejects dimensions not in that set (lines 206-211 show the error message customization for language context).

---

### 3. WHERE VALID DIMENSIONS PER-LANGUAGE ARE DEFINED

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/dimensions/data.py`**

The `load_dimensions_for_lang()` function (lines 194-207):
```python
@lru_cache(maxsize=16)
def load_dimensions_for_lang(
    lang_name: str,
) -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict[str, object]], str]:
    """Load unified review dimensions for a language (with lang override applied)."""
    payload, context = _load_payload_for_lang(
        lang_name,
        _DIMENSIONS_FILE,
        [REDACTED],
    )
    dims, prompts, system_prompt = parse_dimensions_payload(
        payload, context_prefix=context
    )
    return dims, prompts, system_prompt
```

This loads dimensions from:
1. **Shared base:** `desloppify/languages/_framework/review_data/dimensions.json`
2. **Language override:** `desloppify/languages/{lang_name}/review_data/dimensions.json.override`

The actual dimensions **per language are stored in JSON files** in the language directories.

---

### 4. WHERE `review --run-batches` ACCEPTS DIMENSIONS

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/orchestrator.py`**

In `_load_or_prepare_packet()` (lines 197-300), dimensions are parsed via:
```python
dims = parse_dimensions(args)  # Line 225
```

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/helpers.py`**

The `parse_dimensions()` function (lines 6-11):
```python
def parse_dimensions(args: object) -> set[str] | None:
    """Parse ``--dimensions`` from *args* into a set, or ``None`` if absent."""
    raw = getattr(args, "dimensions", None)
    if not isinstance(raw, str) or not raw.strip():
        return None
    return {d.strip() for d in raw.split(",") if d.strip()}
```

**PROBLEM:** This performs **NO VALIDATION** of dimension names against the language. It just splits the comma-separated string.

The dimensions are then passed to `prepare_holistic_review()` which uses them to configure batches, but there's **no language-aware validation** at the command entry point.

---

### 5. WHERE VALIDATION HAPPENS IN `review --import-run`

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/orchestrator.py`**

In `do_import_run()` (lines 501-619):
```python
def do_import_run(
    run_dir_path: str,
    state: dict,
    lang,
    ...
) -> None:
    # Line 526:
    allowed_dims = {str(d) for d in packet.get("dimensions", []) if isinstance(d, str)}
    
    # Lines 540-551:
    batch_results, failures = collect_batch_results(
        selected_indexes=selected_indexes,
        failures=[],
        output_files=output_files,
        allowed_dims=allowed_dims,  # <-- Used for validation
```

Then in `_merge_and_write_results()` (lines 573-589), the code calls `_enforce_import_coverage()` (lines 590-596):
```python
_enforce_import_coverage(
    missing_after_import=missing_after_import,
    packet_dimensions=packet_dimensions,  # <-- These are validated
    allow_partial=allow_partial,
    scan_path=scan_path,
    colorize_fn=colorize,
)
```

Which uses `scored_dimensions_for_lang()` (in batch/scope.py, lines 137-143):
```python
def scored_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name: str) -> list[str]:
    """Return default scored subjective dimensions for one language."""
    try:
        default_dims, _, _ = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name)  # <-- LANGUAGE-SPECIFIC LOAD
    except (ValueError, RuntimeError):
        return []
    return normalize_dimension_list(default_dims)
```

This validates that dimensions in the packet match the **scored dimensions for the current language**.

---

### 6. THE ROOT CAUSE

There are **two independent paths** for dimension validation:

| Path | Validation | Language-Aware? |
|------|-----------|-----------------|
| **`show subjective` suggestions** | Uses global `DISPLAY_NAMES` | ❌ NO |
| **`review --import-run` import validation** | Uses `load_dimensions_for_lang()` via `_validate_holistic_issues_schema()` and `scored_dimensions_for_lang()` | ✅ YES |
| **`review --run-batches` entry** | `parse_dimensions()` accepts ANY string | ❌ NO (but doesn't validate until later) |

When `review --run-batches` runs and reaches `prepare_holistic_review()`, it may accept dimensions that aren't in the language's review_data. When the results are imported via `review --import-run`, the dimension validation code (`_enforce_import_coverage()`) rejects them because they're not in `scored_dimensions_for_lang()`.

---

```
    13→def _print_dimension_score(dim_data: dict, display_name: str) -> None:

    15→    score_val = dim_data.get("score") if isinstance(dim_data, dict) else None

    17→        dim_data.get("strict", score_val) if isinstance(dim_data, dict) else None

    24→            )
    25→        )
    26→
    27→
    28→def _render_judgment(state: dict, dimension_key: str) -> None:
    29→    """Print judgment narrative (strengths, issue_character, score_rationale) if available."""
    30→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments", {})
    31→    assessment = assessments.get(dimension_key, {})

    35→    if not isinstance(judgment, dict):
    36→        return
    37→

    41→

    45→        for s in strengths:

    47→

   106→def _load_dimension_issues(
   107→    state: dict,
   108→    entity,
   109→    status_filter: str,
   110→) -> list[dict]:
   111→    """Load issues for all detectors in a mechanical dimension."""
   112→    all_matches: list[dict] = []
   113→    for detector in entity.detectors:
   114→        matches = load_matches(
   115→            state, scope=detector, status_filter=status_filter, chronic=False
   116→        )
   117→        all_matches.extend(matches)
   118→    seen: set[str] = set()
   119→    unique: list[dict] = []
   120→    for item in all_matches:
   121→        issue_id = item.get("id", "")
   122→        if issue_id not in seen:
   123→            seen.add(issue_id)
   124→            unique.append(item)
   125→    return unique
   126→
```

```
    28→def write_show_output_file(output_file: str, payload: dict, surfaced_count: int) -> bool:
    29→    """Write serialized show payload to file."""
    30→    try:
    31→        safe_write_text(output_file, json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
    32→        print(colorize(f"Wrote {surfaced_count} issues to {output_file}", "green"))
    33→    except OSError as exc:
    34→        payload["output_error"] = str(exc)
    35→        print_write_error(output_file, exc, label="show output")
    36→        return False
    37→    return True

    40→def group_matches_by_file(matches: list[dict]) -> list[tuple[str, list]]:
    41→    """Group issues by file and sort by descending count."""
    42→    by_file: dict[str, list] = defaultdict(list)
    43→    for issue in matches:
    44→        by_file[issue["file"]].append(issue)
    45→    return sorted(by_file.items(), key=lambda item: -len(item[1]))

    53→        "fixed": "✓",
    54→        "wontfix": "—",

    60→    print(
    61→        f"    {status_icon} T{issue['tier']} [{issue['confidence']}] {issue['summary']}{zone_tag}"
    62→    )
    63→
    64→    detail_parts = format_detail(issue.get("detail", {}))
    65→    if detail_parts:
    66→        print(colorize(f"      {' · '.join(detail_parts)}", "dim"))
    67→    if show_code:
    68→        detail_raw = issue.get("detail", {})
    69→        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}
    70→        target_line = (

    73→        if target_line and issue["file"] not in (".", ""):
    74→            snippet = read_code_snippet(issue["file"], target_line)

    84→    if issue.get("note"):

   212→    narrative: dict, matches: list[dict], *, plan: dict | None = None
   213→) -> None:
   214→    """Render a compact plan from current issues and narrative actions.

   216→    When a living *plan* is active, renders plan focus/progress instead.
   217→    """
   218→    if plan and (plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("clusters")):
   219→        render_plan_with_living_plan(
   220→            [],
   221→            plan=plan,
   222→            header="  AGENT PLAN (use `desloppify next` to see your next task):",
   223→        )
   224→        return
   225→
   226→    actions = narrative.get("actions", [])
   227→    if not actions and not matches:
   228→        return
   229→
   230→    print(
   231→        colorize(
   232→            "  AGENT PLAN (use `desloppify next --count 20` to inspect more items):",
   233→            "yellow",
   234→        )
   235→    )
   236→    if actions:
   237→        top = actions[0]
   238→        print(
   239→            colorize(
   240→                f"  Agent focus: `{top['command']}` — {top['description']}",
   241→                "cyan",
   242→            )
   243→        )
   244→    elif matches:
   245→        first = matches[0]
   246→        print(
   247→            colorize(
   248→                "  Agent focus: `desloppify next --count 20` — "
   249→                f"inspect and resolve `{first.get('id', '')}`",
   250→                "cyan",
   251→            )
   252→        )
   253→
   254→    if print_ranked_actions(actions):
   255→        print()
   256→
   257→
   258→def show_subjective_followup(
   259→    state: dict, target_strict_score: float, *, objective_backlog: int = 0,
   260→) -> None:
   261→    """Show subjective follow-up guidance for the current state."""
   262→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {}) or {}
   263→    if not dim_scores:
   264→        return
   265→
   266→    subjective = reporting_dimensions_mod.scorecard_subjective_entries(
   267→        state,
   268→        dim_scores=dim_scores,
   269→    )
   270→    if not subjective:
   271→        return
   272→
   273→    followup = reporting_dimensions_mod.build_subjective_followup(
   274→        state,
   275→        subjective,
   276→        threshold=target_strict_score,
   277→        max_quality_items=3,
   278→        max_integrity_items=5,
   279→    )
   280→    if print_subjective_followup(followup, objective_backlog=objective_backlog):
   281→        print()
   282→
   283→

   287→    "show_agent_plan",
```

```
1→"""Shared subjective follow-up rendering helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Protocol
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     8→
     9→
    10→class SubjectiveFollowup(Protocol):
    11→    """Contract for objects passed to print_subjective_followup."""
    12→
    13→    low_assessed: object  # falsy when no dimensions are below threshold
    14→    threshold_label: str
    15→    rendered: str
    16→    command: str
    17→    integrity_lines: list[tuple[str, str]]
    18→
    19→
    20→def print_subjective_followup(
    21→    followup: SubjectiveFollowup,
    22→    *,
    23→    leading_newline: bool = False,
    24→    objective_backlog: int = 0,
    25→) -> bool:
    26→    """Render common subjective quality/integrity guidance lines.
    27→
    28→    When *objective_backlog* > 0, quality nudges are suppressed entirely — the
    29→    queue already surfaces subjective work when it becomes actionable.
    30→    Integrity warnings are always shown (anti-gaming safeguard).
    31→
    32→    Returns True when any line is rendered.
    33→    """
    34→    printed = False
    35→    prefix = "\n" if leading_newline else ""
    36→    if followup.low_assessed and objective_backlog <= 0:
    37→        print(
    38→            colorize(
    39→                f"{prefix}  Subjective quality (<{followup.threshold_label}%): "
    40→                f"{followup.rendered}",
    41→                "cyan",
    42→            )
    43→        )
    44→        print(
    45→            colorize(
    46→                f"  Next command to improve subjective scores: {followup.command}",
    47→                "dim",
    48→            )
    49→        )
    50→        printed = True
    51→
    52→    if followup.integrity_lines:
    53→        if printed:
    54→            print()
    55→        for style, message in followup.integrity_lines:
    56→            print(colorize(f"  {message}", style))
    57→        printed = True
    58→    return printed
    59→
    60→
    61→__all__ = ["print_subjective_followup"]
    62→
```

```
1→"""Subjective scan reporting: common helpers, integrity checks, and output."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.config import DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE, coerce_target_score
     8→from desloppify.engine._scoring.subjective.core import DISPLAY_NAMES
     9→from desloppify.intelligence import integrity as subjective_integrity_mod
    10→
    11→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12→# Common helpers (formerly scan_reporting_subjective_common)
    13→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    14→
    15→
    16→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    17→class SubjectiveFollowup:
    18→    threshold: float
    19→    threshold_label: str
    20→    low_assessed: list[dict]
    21→    rendered: str
    22→    command: str
    23→    integrity_notice: dict[str, object] | None
    24→    integrity_lines: list[tuple[str, str]]
    25→
    26→
    27→def flatten_cli_keys(items: list[dict], *, max_items: int = 3) -> str:
    28→    """Flatten CLI keys across up to max_items subjective entries, preserving order."""
    29→    seen: set[str] = set()
    30→    ordered: list[str] = []
    31→    for item in items[:max_items]:
    32→        for key in item.get("cli_keys", []):
    33→            if key in seen:
    34→                continue
    35→            ordered.append(key)
    36→            seen.add(key)
    37→    return ",".join(ordered)
    38→
    39→
    40→def render_subjective_scores(entries: list[dict], *, max_items: int = 3) -> str:
    41→    return ", ".join(
    42→        f"{entry.get('name', 'Subjective')} {float(entry.get('strict', entry.get('score', 100.0))):.1f}%"
    43→        for entry in entries[:max_items]
    44→    )
    45→
    46→
    47→def render_subjective_names(entries: list[dict], *, max_names: int = 3) -> str:
    48→    count = len(entries)
    49→    names = ", ".join(
    50→        str(entry.get("name", "Subjective")) for entry in entries[:max_names]
    51→    )
    52→    if count > max_names:
    53→        names = f"{names}, +{count - max_names} more"
    54→    return names
    55→
    56→
    57→def coerce_notice_count(value: object) -> int:
    58→    if isinstance(value, bool):
    59→        return 0
    60→    if isinstance(value, int):
    61→        return value
    62→    if isinstance(value, float):
    63→        return int(value)
    64→    if isinstance(value, str):
    65→        try:
    66→            return int(value.strip())
    67→        except ValueError:
    68→            return 0
    69→    return 0
    70→
    71→
    72→def coerce_str_keys(value: object) -> list[str]:
    73→    if not isinstance(value, list | tuple | set):
    74→        return []
    75→    return [key for key in value if isinstance(key, str) and key]
    76→
    77→
    78→def subjective_rerun_command(
    79→    items: list[dict],
    80→    *,
    81→    max_items: int = 5,
    82→    refresh: bool = True,
    83→    has_prior_review: bool | None = None,
    84→) -> str:
    85→    _ = refresh
    86→    # If dimensions already have open review issues, route to the review queue
    87→    # instead of prompting a blocked rerun.
    88→    if any(
    89→        int(entry.get("failing", 0) or 0) > 0
    90→        for entry in items[:max_items]
    91→        if isinstance(entry, dict)
    92→    ):
    93→        return "`desloppify show review --status open`"
    94→
    95→    dim_keys = flatten_cli_keys(items, max_items=max_items)
    96→
    97→    # If no evidence of prior runner usage, suggest --prepare first
    98→    if has_prior_review is False:
    99→        command_parts = ["desloppify", "review", "--prepare"]
   100→        if dim_keys:
   101→            command_parts.extend(["--dimensions", dim_keys])
   102→        return f"`{' '.join(command_parts)}`"
   103→
   104→    command_parts = [
   105→        "desloppify",
   106→        "review",
   107→        "--prepare",
   108→        "--force-review-rerun",
   109→    ]
   110→    if dim_keys:
   111→        command_parts.extend(["--dimensions", dim_keys])
   112→    return f"`{' '.join(command_parts)}`"
   113→
   114→
   115→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   116→# Integrity and dimension-mapping helpers (formerly scan_reporting_subjective_integrity)
   117→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   118→
   119→
   120→def _subjective_display_name_from_key(dimension_key: str) -> str:
   121→    return DISPLAY_NAMES.get(
   122→        dimension_key, dimension_key.replace("_", " ").title()
   123→    )
   124→
   125→
   126→def subjective_entries_for_dimension_keys(
   127→    dimension_keys: list[str], entries: list[dict]
   128→) -> list[dict]:
   129→    by_key: dict[str, dict] = {}
   130→    for entry in entries:
   131→        for key in entry.get("cli_keys", []):
   132→            by_key.setdefault(str(key), entry)
   133→
   134→    mapped: list[dict] = []
   135→    for key in dimension_keys:
   136→        if key in by_key:
   137→            mapped.append(by_key[key])
   138→            continue
   139→        mapped.append(
   140→            {
   141→                "name": _subjective_display_name_from_key(key),
   142→                "score": 0.0,
   143→                "strict": 0.0,
   144→                "failing": 0,
   145→                "placeholder": False,
   146→                "cli_keys": [key],
   147→            }
   148→        )
   149→    return mapped
   150→
   151→
   152→def _integrity_notice_for_explicit_status(
   153→    *,
   154→    status: str,
   155→    matched_keys: list[str],
   156→    reset_keys: list[str],
   157→    target_display: float,
   158→    subjective_entries: list[dict],
   159→    max_items: int,
   160→) -> dict[str, object] | None:
   161→    if status == "penalized" and reset_keys:
   162→        reset_entries = subjective_entries_for_dimension_keys(
   163→            reset_keys,
   164→            subjective_entries,
   165→        )
   166→        return {
   167→            "status": "penalized",
   168→            "count": len(reset_keys),
   169→            "target": target_display,
   170→            "entries": reset_entries,
   171→            "rendered": render_subjective_names(reset_entries),
   172→            "command": subjective_rerun_command(reset_entries, max_items=max_items),
   173→        }
   174→    if status == "warn" and matched_keys:
   175→        matched_entries = subjective_entries_for_dimension_keys(
   176→            matched_keys,
   177→            subjective_entries,
   178→        )
   179→        return {
   180→            "status": "warn",
   181→            "count": len(matched_keys),
   182→            "target": target_display,
   183→            "entries": matched_entries,
   184→            "rendered": render_subjective_names(matched_entries),
   185→            "command": subjective_rerun_command(matched_entries, max_items=max_items),
   186→        }
   187→    return None
   188→
   189→
   190→def _at_target_entries(
   191→    subjective_entries: list[dict],
   192→    *,
   193→    threshold_value: float,
   194→) -> list[dict]:
   195→    return sorted(
   196→        [
   197→            entry
   198→            for entry in subjective_entries
   199→            if not entry.get("placeholder")
   200→            and subjective_integrity_mod.matches_target_score(
   201→                float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0))),
   202→                threshold_value,
   203→            )
   204→        ],
   205→        key=lambda entry: str(entry.get("name", "")).lower(),
   206→    )
   207→
   208→
   209→def subjective_integrity_followup(
   210→    state: dict,
   211→    subjective_entries: list[dict],
   212→    *,
   213→    threshold: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
   214→    max_items: int = 5,
   215→) -> dict[str, object] | None:
   216→    threshold_value = coerce_target_score(threshold)
   217→    raw_integrity_state = state.get("subjective_integrity")
   218→    integrity_state: dict[str, object] = (
   219→        raw_integrity_state if isinstance(raw_integrity_state, dict) else {}
   220→    )
   221→    status = str(integrity_state.get("status", "")).strip().lower()
   222→    raw_target = integrity_state.get("target_score")
   223→    target_display = coerce_target_score(raw_target, fallback=threshold_value)
   224→    matched_keys = coerce_str_keys(integrity_state.get("matched_dimensions", []))
   225→    reset_keys = coerce_str_keys(integrity_state.get("reset_dimensions", []))
   226→
   227→    explicit_notice = _integrity_notice_for_explicit_status(
   228→        status=status,
   229→        matched_keys=matched_keys,
   230→        reset_keys=reset_keys,
   231→        target_display=target_display,
   232→        subjective_entries=subjective_entries,
   233→        max_items=max_items,
   234→    )
   235→    if explicit_notice is not None:
   236→        return explicit_notice
   237→
   238→    at_target = _at_target_entries(
   239→        subjective_entries,
   240→        threshold_value=threshold_value,
   241→    )
   242→    if not at_target:
   243→        return None
   244→
   245→    return {
   246→        "status": "at_target",
   247→        "count": len(at_target),
   248→        "target": threshold_value,
   249→        "entries": at_target,
   250→        "rendered": render_subjective_names(at_target),
   251→        "command": subjective_rerun_command(at_target, max_items=max_items),
   252→    }
   253→
   254→
   255→def subjective_integrity_notice_lines(
   256→    integrity_notice: dict[str, object] | None,
   257→    *,
   258→    fallback_target: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
   259→) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
   260→    if not integrity_notice:
   261→        return []
   262→
   263→    status = str(integrity_notice.get("status", "")).strip().lower()
   264→    count = coerce_notice_count(integrity_notice.get("count", 0))
   265→    target_display = coerce_target_score(
   266→        integrity_notice.get("target"),
   267→        fallback=fallback_target,
   268→    )
   269→    rendered = str(integrity_notice.get("rendered", "subjective dimensions"))
   270→    command = str(integrity_notice.get("command", ""))
   271→
   272→    if status == "penalized":
   273→        return [
   274→            (
   275→                "red",
   276→                "WARNING: "
   277→                f"{count} subjective dimensions matched target {target_display:.1f} "
   278→                f"and were reset to 0.0 this scan: {rendered}.",
   279→            ),
   280→            (
   281→                "yellow",
   282→                "Anti-gaming safeguard applied. Re-review objectively and import fresh assessments.",
   283→            ),
   284→            ("dim", f"Rerun now: {command}"),
   285→        ]
   286→
   287→    if status == "warn":
   288→        dimension_label = "dimension is" if count == 1 else "dimensions are"
   289→        return [
   290→            (
   291→                "yellow",
   292→                "WARNING: "
   293→                f"{count} subjective {dimension_label} parked on target {target_display:.1f}. "
   294→                "Re-run that review with evidence-first scoring before treating this score as final.",
   295→            ),
   296→            ("dim", f"Next step: {command}"),
   297→        ]
   298→
   299→    if status == "at_target":
   300→        return [
   301→            (
   302→                "yellow",
   303→                "WARNING: "
   304→                f"{count} of your subjective scores matches the target score, indicating a high risk of gaming. "
   305→                f"Can you rerun them by running {command} taking extra care to be objective.",
   306→            ),
   307→        ]
   308→
   309→    return []
   310→
   311→
   312→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   313→# Output-oriented helpers (formerly scan_reporting_subjective_output)
   314→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   315→
   316→
   317→def build_subjective_followup(
   318→    state: dict,
   319→    subjective_entries: list[dict],
   320→    *,
   321→    threshold: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
   322→    max_quality_items: int = 3,
   323→    max_integrity_items: int = 5,
   324→) -> SubjectiveFollowup:
   325→    threshold_value = coerce_target_score(threshold)
   326→    threshold_label = f"{threshold_value:.1f}".rstrip("0").rstrip(".")
   327→    low_assessed = sorted(
   328→        [
   329→            entry
   330→            for entry in subjective_entries
   331→            if not entry.get("placeholder")
   332→            and float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0))) < threshold_value
   333→        ],
   334→        key=lambda entry: float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0))),
   335→    )
   336→    rendered = render_subjective_scores(low_assessed, max_items=max_quality_items)
   337→    command = subjective_rerun_command(
   338→        low_assessed,
   339→        max_items=max_quality_items,
   340→    )
   341→    integrity_notice = subjective_integrity_followup(
   342→        state,
   343→        subjective_entries,
   344→        threshold=threshold_value,
   345→        max_items=max_integrity_items,
   346→    )
   347→    integrity_lines = subjective_integrity_notice_lines(
   348→        integrity_notice,
   349→        fallback_target=threshold_value,
   350→    )
   351→    return SubjectiveFollowup(
   352→        threshold=threshold_value,
   353→        threshold_label=threshold_label,
   354→        low_assessed=low_assessed,
   355→        rendered=rendered,
   356→        command=command,
   357→        integrity_notice=integrity_notice,
   358→        integrity_lines=integrity_lines,
   359→    )
   360→
   361→
   362→def show_subjective_paths(
   363→    state: dict,
   364→    dim_scores: dict,
   365→    *,
   366→    colorize_fn,
   367→    scorecard_subjective_entries_fn,
   368→    threshold: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
   369→) -> None:
   370→    threshold_value = coerce_target_score(threshold)
   371→    subjective_entries = scorecard_subjective_entries_fn(state, dim_scores=dim_scores)
   372→    if not subjective_entries:
   373→        return
   374→
   375→    followup = build_subjective_followup(
   376→        state,
   377→        subjective_entries,
   378→        threshold=threshold_value,
   379→        max_quality_items=3,
   380→        max_integrity_items=5,
   381→    )
   382→    unassessed = sorted(
   383→        [entry for entry in subjective_entries if entry["placeholder"]],
   384→        key=lambda item: item["name"].lower(),
   385→    )
   386→    low_assessed = followup.low_assessed
   387→
   388→    all_issues = state.get("issues", {})
   389→    if not isinstance(all_issues, dict):
   390→        all_issues = {}
   391→    coverage_global, _reason_counts, _holistic_reasons = (
   392→        subjective_integrity_mod.subjective_review_open_breakdown(all_issues)
   393→    )
   394→
   395→    stale_count = sum(1 for e in subjective_entries if e.get("stale"))
   396→    if (
   397→        not unassessed
   398→        and not low_assessed
   399→        and stale_count == 0
   400→        and coverage_global <= 0
   401→        and not followup.integrity_notice
   402→    ):
   403→        return
   404→
   405→    # Integrity lines are always preserved (anti-gaming safeguard).
   406→    for style, message in followup.integrity_lines:
   407→        print(colorize_fn(f"    {message}", style))
   408→
   409→    # Collapsed summary replacing verbose Subjective path section.
   410→    parts: list[str] = []
   411→    if low_assessed:
   412→        parts.append(f"{len(low_assessed)} below target ({followup.threshold_label}%)")
   413→    if unassessed:
   414→        parts.append(f"{len(unassessed)} unassessed")
   415→    if stale_count:
   416→        parts.append(f"{stale_count} stale")
   417→    if coverage_global > 0:
   418→        parts.append(f"{coverage_global} files need review")
   419→
   420→    if parts:
   421→        print(colorize_fn(f"  Subjective: {', '.join(parts)}.", "cyan"))
   422→        print(colorize_fn("  Run `desloppify show subjective` for details.", "dim"))
   423→
   424→    print()
   425→
   426→
   427→__all__ = [
   428→    "SubjectiveFollowup",
   429→    "build_subjective_followup",
   430→    "coerce_notice_count",
   431→    "coerce_str_keys",
   432→    "flatten_cli_keys",
   433→    "render_subjective_names",
   434→    "render_subjective_scores",
   435→    "show_subjective_paths",
   436→    "subjective_entries_for_dimension_keys",
   437→    "subjective_integrity_followup",
   438→    "subjective_integrity_notice_lines",
   439→    "subjective_rerun_command",
   440→]
   441→
```

## Summary

I've found where the `show subjective` special view generates the suggested `desloppify review --prepare --force-review-rerun --dimensions ...` command.

### Key Location: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/reporting/subjective.py`

**The exact function that builds the command string is `subjective_rerun_command()` at lines 78-112:**

```python
def subjective_rerun_command(
    items: list[dict],
    *,
    max_items: int = 5,
    refresh: bool = True,
    has_prior_review: bool | None = None,
) -> str:
    ...
    dim_keys = flatten_cli_keys(items, max_items=max_items)

    # If no evidence of prior runner usage, suggest --prepare first
    if has_prior_review is False:
        command_parts = ["desloppify", "review", "--prepare"]
        if dim_keys:
            command_parts.extend(["--dimensions", dim_keys])
        return f"`{' '.join(command_parts)}`"

    command_parts = [
        "desloppify",
        "review",
        "--prepare",
        "--force-review-rerun",
    ]
    if dim_keys:
        command_parts.extend(["--dimensions", dim_keys])
    return f"`{' '.join(command_parts)}`"
```

### Call Chain to `show subjective`:

1. **`desloppify/app/commands/show/dimension_views.py:87-90`** — When rendering a subjective dimension, calls:
   ```python

       state,
   ```

4. **`desloppify/app/commands/scan/reporting/subjective.py:27-37`** — The `flatten_cli_keys()` function assembles dimension names into the CSV string by extracting `cli_keys` from dimension entries.

5. **`desloppify/app/commands/helpers/subjective.py:20-58`** — Finally, `print_subjective_followup()` renders the command string to the user (line 46):
   ```python

       colorize(
           f"  Next command to improve subjective scores: {followup.command}",
           "dim",
       )
   )
   ```

**The dimension names are assembled via `flatten_cli_keys()` which extracts the `cli_keys` list from each dimension entry and joins them with commas.**

> DEVELOPER

Fix the scoring floor gaming vulnerability in desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/scoring.py.

**The bug**: The floor is calculated as `min(score_raw_by_dim.get(key, [weighted_mean]))` which takes the absolute minimum file score. This can be gamed by merging a bad file into a good file, eliminating the floor penalty.

**The fix**: Replace the `min()` floor with a weighted percentile floor that uses the bottom 10% of total weight. This way bad code always penalizes proportionally regardless of file boundaries.

Current code in scoring.py, `merge_scores` method (line 160):
```python
floor = min(score_raw_by_dim.get(key, [weighted_mean]))
```

The `score_buckets` dict contains `list[tuple[float, float]]` where each tuple is `(score, weight)`. The `score_raw_by_dim` only has raw scores without weights.

**Changes needed**:

1. In `scoring.py`, add a module-level function `_percentile_floor(weighted_scores, fallback)` that:
   - Takes the list of (score, weight) tuples from score_buckets
   - Sorts by score ascending
   - Walks through accumulating weight until 10% of total weight is reached
   - Returns the weighted mean of those bottom entries
   - Falls back to min() if only 1 entry

2. In `merge_scores`, replace line 160 with:
```python
floor = _percentile_floor(weighted_scores, fallback=weighted_mean)
```

Add constant: `_FLOOR_PERCENTILE = 0.1`

Also update the existing test file. Read `desloppify/tests/commands/review/test_review_batch_core_direct.py` to find existing floor/scoring tests and update them to expect the new percentile behavior.

IMPORTANT: Write the code changes. Do NOT just research.

```
1→"""Scoring primitives for holistic review batch merges."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.scoring_constants import CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS
     9→
    10→_IMPACT_SCOPE_WEIGHTS = {
    11→    "local": 1.0,
    12→    "module": 1.3,
    13→    "subsystem": 1.6,
    14→    "codebase": 2.0,
    15→}
    16→_FIX_SCOPE_WEIGHTS = {
    17→    "single_edit": 1.0,
    18→    "multi_file_refactor": 1.3,
    19→    "architectural_change": 1.7,
    20→}
    21→
    22→# Blending ratio between weighted mean and per-batch floor score.
    23→_WEIGHTED_MEAN_BLEND = 0.7
    24→_FLOOR_BLEND_WEIGHT = 0.3
    25→
    26→# Maximum total penalty that issues can impose on a dimension score.
    27→_MAX_ISSUE_PENALTY = 24.0
    28→
    29→# Per-unit penalty from cumulative issue severity.
    30→_PRESSURE_PENALTY_MULTIPLIER = 2.2
    31→
    32→# Extra penalty per additional issue beyond the first.
    33→_EXTRA_ISSUE_PENALTY = 0.8
    34→
    35→# Issues-based score cap parameters.
    36→_CAP_FLOOR = 60.0
    37→_CAP_CEILING = 90.0
    38→_CAP_PRESSURE_MULTIPLIER = 3.5
    39→
    40→
    41→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    42→class ScoreInputs:
    43→    """Normalized inputs for a single dimension merge computation."""
    44→
    45→    weighted_mean: float
    46→    floor: float
    47→    issue_pressure: float
    48→    issue_count: int
    49→
    50→
    51→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    52→class ScoreBreakdown:
    53→    """Named intermediate values for one merged dimension score."""
    54→
    55→    weighted_mean: float
    56→    floor: float
    57→    floor_aware: float
    58→    issue_penalty: float
    59→    issue_cap: float | None
    60→    final_score: float
    61→
    62→
    63→class DimensionMergeScorer:
    64→    """Compute pressure-adjusted merged scores for holistic review dimensions."""
    65→
    66→    def issue_severity(
    67→        self,
    68→        issue: dict[str, Any],
    69→        *,
    70→        note: dict[str, Any] | None,
    71→    ) -> float:
    72→        """Compute per-issue severity used for score-pressure adjustments."""
    73→        note_ref = note if isinstance(note, dict) else {}
    74→        confidence = str(
    75→            issue.get("confidence", note_ref.get("confidence", "medium"))
    76→        ).strip().lower()
    77→        impact_scope = str(
    78→            issue.get("impact_scope", note_ref.get("impact_scope", "local"))
    79→        ).strip().lower()
    80→        fix_scope = str(
    81→            issue.get("fix_scope", note_ref.get("fix_scope", "single_edit"))
    82→        ).strip().lower()
    83→
    84→        confidence_weight = CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(confidence, 1.0)
    85→        impact_weight = _IMPACT_SCOPE_WEIGHTS.get(impact_scope, 1.0)
    86→        fix_weight = _FIX_SCOPE_WEIGHTS.get(fix_scope, 1.0)
    87→        return confidence_weight * impact_weight * fix_weight
    88→
    89→    def issue_pressure_by_dimension(
    90→        self,
    91→        issues: list[dict[str, Any]],
    92→        *,
    93→        dimension_notes: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
    94→    ) -> tuple[dict[str, float], dict[str, int]]:
    95→        """Summarize how strongly issues should pull dimension scores down."""
    96→        pressure_by_dim: dict[str, float] = {}
    97→        count_by_dim: dict[str, int] = {}
    98→        for issue in issues:
    99→            dim = str(issue.get("dimension", "")).strip()
   100→            if not dim:
   101→                continue
   102→            note = dimension_notes.get(dim)
   103→            pressure_by_dim[dim] = pressure_by_dim.get(dim, 0.0) + self.issue_severity(
   104→                issue,
   105→                note=note if isinstance(note, dict) else None,
   106→            )
   107→            count_by_dim[dim] = count_by_dim.get(dim, 0) + 1
   108→        return pressure_by_dim, count_by_dim
   109→
   110→    def score_dimension(self, inputs: ScoreInputs) -> ScoreBreakdown:
   111→        """Compute one merged score with explicit intermediate values."""
   112→        floor_aware = (
   113→            _WEIGHTED_MEAN_BLEND * inputs.weighted_mean
   114→            + _FLOOR_BLEND_WEIGHT * inputs.floor
   115→        )
   116→        issue_penalty = min(
   117→            _MAX_ISSUE_PENALTY,
   118→            (inputs.issue_pressure * _PRESSURE_PENALTY_MULTIPLIER)
   119→            + (max(inputs.issue_count - 1, 0) * _EXTRA_ISSUE_PENALTY),
   120→        )
   121→        issue_adjusted = floor_aware - issue_penalty
   122→
   123→        issue_cap: float | None = None
   124→        if inputs.issue_count > 0:
   125→            cap_penalty = (
   126→                (inputs.issue_pressure * _CAP_PRESSURE_MULTIPLIER)
   127→                + (max(inputs.issue_count - 1, 0) * _EXTRA_ISSUE_PENALTY)
   128→            )
   129→            issue_cap = max(
   130→                _CAP_FLOOR,
   131→                _CAP_CEILING - cap_penalty,
   132→            )
   133→            issue_adjusted = min(issue_adjusted, issue_cap)
   134→
   135→        final_score = round(max(0.0, min(100.0, issue_adjusted)), 1)
   136→        return ScoreBreakdown(
   137→            weighted_mean=inputs.weighted_mean,
   138→            floor=inputs.floor,
   139→            floor_aware=floor_aware,
   140→            issue_penalty=issue_penalty,
   141→            issue_cap=issue_cap,
   142→            final_score=final_score,
   143→        )
   144→
   145→    def merge_scores(
   146→        self,
   147→        score_buckets: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]],
   148→        score_raw_by_dim: dict[str, list[float]],
   149→        issue_pressure_by_dim: dict[str, float],
   150→        issue_count_by_dim: dict[str, int],
   151→    ) -> dict[str, float]:
   152→        """Compute pressure-adjusted weighted mean for each dimension."""
   153→        merged: dict[str, float] = {}
   154→        for key, weighted_scores in sorted(score_buckets.items()):
   155→            if not weighted_scores:
   156→                continue
   157→            numerator = sum(score * weight for score, weight in weighted_scores)
   158→            denominator = sum(weight for _, weight in weighted_scores)
   159→            weighted_mean = numerator / max(denominator, 1.0)
   160→            floor = min(score_raw_by_dim.get(key, [weighted_mean]))
   161→            breakdown = self.score_dimension(
   162→                ScoreInputs(
   163→                    weighted_mean=weighted_mean,
   164→                    floor=floor,
   165→                    issue_pressure=issue_pressure_by_dim.get(key, 0.0),
   166→                    issue_count=issue_count_by_dim.get(key, 0),
   167→                )
   168→            )
   169→            merged[key] = breakdown.final_score
   170→        return merged
   171→
   172→
   173→__all__ = ["DimensionMergeScorer", "ScoreBreakdown", "ScoreInputs"]
   174→
```

Fix the Go unused-import false positives in the tree-sitter unused import detector.

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/treesitter/analysis/unused_imports.py`

**The bug**: The `_extract_alias` function only handles `as`-style aliases (Python, PHP). Go uses a different syntax where the alias is a `package_identifier` before the path with no `as` keyword:
```go
import (
    daysailv1 "github.com/anshu/daysail/backend/gen/daysail/v1"
)
```

In tree-sitter's Go grammar, the `import_spec` node has:
- `name: (package_identifier)` — optional alias
- `path: (interpreted_string_literal)` — the import path

The current `_extract_alias` walks children looking for an `as` keyword which Go doesn't use. So for aliased Go imports, the detector falls back to `_extract_import_name` which extracts the last path segment (e.g., `v1`), but the code uses the alias name (e.g., `daysailv1`). The regex `\bv1\b` doesn't match inside `daysailv1.Something` because there's no word boundary between `l` and `v`.

**The fix**: Modify `_extract_alias` to also check for Go-style aliases by looking for `package_identifier` type children directly, before the `as`-keyword check:

```python
def _extract_alias(import_node) -> str | None:
    # Handle Go-style named imports: alias is a package_identifier child
    # before the path, with no "as" keyword.
    for i in range(import_node.child_count):
        child = import_node.children[i]
        if child.type == "package_identifier":
            return _node_text(child)

    # Handle "as"-style aliases (Python, PHP, etc.)
    found_as = False
    for child in _iter_children(import_node):
        text = _node_text(child)
        if text == "as":
            found_as = True
            continue
        if found_as and child.type in ("name", "identifier", "namespace_name"):
            return _node_text(child)
    return None
```

Also add a test case for Go-style aliases in the test file. Read `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_treesitter_complexity_and_integration.py` to find the existing unused import tests (around lines 615-679) and add a new test for Go-style aliased imports.

```
     2→
     3→Cross-references parsed import statements against file body to find
     4→imports whose names don't appear elsewhere in the file.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import logging
    10→import re
    11→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
    12→
    13→from .. import PARSE_INIT_ERRORS
    14→from ..imports.cache import _PARSE_CACHE
    15→from .extractors import _get_parser, _make_query, _node_text, _run_query, _unwrap_node
    16→
    17→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    18→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import TreeSitterLangSpec
    19→
    20→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→
    23→def detect_unused_imports(
    24→    file_list: list[str],
    25→    spec: TreeSitterLangSpec,
    26→) -> list[dict]:
    27→    """Find imports whose names are not referenced elsewhere in the file.
    28→
    29→    Returns list of {file, line, name} entries.
    30→    """
    31→    if not spec.import_query:
    32→        return []
    33→
    34→    try:
    35→        parser, language = _get_parser(spec.grammar)
    36→    except PARSE_INIT_ERRORS as exc:
    37→        logger.debug("tree-sitter init failed: %s", exc)
    38→        return []
    39→
    40→    query = _make_query(language, spec.import_query)
    41→    entries: list[dict] = []
    42→
    43→    for filepath in file_list:
    44→        cached = _PARSE_CACHE.get_or_parse(filepath, parser, spec.grammar)
    45→        if cached is None:
    46→            continue
    47→        source, tree = cached
    48→        source_text = source.decode("utf-8", errors="replace")
    49→
    50→        matches = _run_query(query, tree.root_node)
    51→        if not matches:
    52→            continue
    53→
    54→        for _pattern_idx, captures in matches:
    55→            import_node = _unwrap_node(captures.get("import"))
    56→            path_node = _unwrap_node(captures.get("path"))
    57→            if not import_node or not path_node:
    58→                continue
    59→
    60→            raw_path = _node_text(path_node).strip("\"'`")
    61→            if not raw_path:
    62→                continue
    63→
    64→            # Check for alias (e.g. PHP ``use Foo as Bar``, Python ``import X as Y``).
    65→            # When an alias is present, search for the alias name instead.
    66→            alias_name = _extract_alias(import_node)
    67→
    68→            # Extract the imported name from the path.
    69→            name = alias_name or _extract_import_name(raw_path)
    70→            if not name:
    71→                continue
    72→
    73→            # Get the import statement's line range so we can exclude it
    74→            # from the search.
    75→            import_start = import_node.start_byte
    76→            import_end = import_node.end_byte
    77→
    78→            # Build text without the import statement itself.
    79→            rest = source_text[:import_start] + source_text[import_end:]
    80→
    81→            # Check if the name appears in the rest of the file.
    82→            if not re.search(r'\b' + re.escape(name) + r'\b', rest):
    83→                entries.append({
    84→                    "file": filepath,
    85→                    "line": import_node.start_point[0] + 1,
    86→                    "name": name,
    87→                })
    88→
    89→    return entries
    90→
    91→
    92→def _extract_alias(import_node) -> str | None:
    93→    """Extract alias name from ``use Foo as Bar`` or ``import X as Y``.
    94→
    95→    Walks children of the import node looking for an alias/name node
    96→    after an ``as`` keyword. Returns the alias text or None.
    97→    """
    98→    found_as = False
    99→    for child in _iter_children(import_node):
   100→        text = _node_text(child)
   101→        if text == "as":
   102→            found_as = True
   103→            continue
   104→        # The node immediately after "as" is the alias name.
   105→        if found_as and child.type in ("name", "identifier", "namespace_name"):
   106→            return _node_text(child)
   107→    return None
   108→
   109→
   110→def _iter_children(node):
   111→    """Recursively yield terminal-ish children relevant to alias extraction.
   112→
   113→    Only descends into namespace_use_clause / import_clause nodes (the
   114→    immediate import container) — avoids descending into unrelated subtrees.
   115→    """
   116→    for i in range(node.child_count):
   117→        child = node.children[i]
   118→        # Yield leaf-like nodes (keywords, identifiers).
   119→        if child.child_count == 0:
   120→            yield child
   121→        elif child.type in (
   122→            "namespace_use_clause", "import_clause",
   123→            "namespace_alias", "as_pattern",
   124→        ):
   125→            yield from _iter_children(child)
   126→
   127→
   128→def _extract_import_name(import_path: str) -> str:
   129→    """Extract the usable name from an import path.
   130→
   131→    Examples:
   132→        "fmt" -> "fmt"
   133→        "./utils" -> "utils"
   134→        "crate::module::Foo" -> "Foo"
   135→        "com.example.MyClass" -> "MyClass"
   136→        "MyApp::Model::User" -> "User"
   137→        "Data.List" -> "List"
   138→    """
   139→    # Strip common path separators and take the last segment.
   140→    for sep in ("::", ".", "/", "\\"):
   141→        if sep in import_path:
   142→            parts = import_path.split(sep)
   143→            # Filter out empty segments and take the last.
   144→            parts = [p for p in parts if p]
   145→            if parts:
   146→                name = parts[-1]
   147→                # Strip file extensions.
   148→                for ext in (".go", ".rs", ".rb", ".py", ".js", ".jsx", ".ts",
   149→                            ".tsx", ".java", ".kt", ".cs", ".fs", ".ml",
   150→                            ".ex", ".erl", ".hs", ".lua", ".zig", ".pm",
   151→                            ".sh", ".pl", ".scala", ".swift", ".php",
   152→                            ".dart", ".mjs", ".cjs"):
   153→                    if name.endswith(ext):
   154→                        name = name[:-len(ext)]
   155→                        break
   156→                return name
   157→
   158→    # No separator — the path itself is the name.
   159→    return import_path
   160→
   161→
   162→__all__ = ["detect_unused_imports"]
   163→
```

Fix the dimension validation bug where `show subjective` suggests dimensions that are invalid for the current language, and `review --run-batches` accepts them without upfront validation.

**The fix**: Add upfront dimension validation in the batch orchestrator so invalid dimensions are rejected early with a clear error.

**File to edit**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/orchestrator.py`

In the `_load_or_prepare_packet` function, after line 225 where `dims = parse_dimensions(args)`, add validation when explicit dimensions are provided:

```python
dims = parse_dimensions(args)

# Validate explicit dimensions against the language's scored dimensions.
if dims:
    lang_obj = lang  # lang is already passed to this function
    lang_name = getattr(lang_obj, "name", None) or str(getattr(lang_obj, "lang", ""))
    if lang_name:
        valid_dims = set(scored_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name))
        if valid_dims:
            invalid = sorted(dims - valid_dims)
            if invalid:
                valid_list = ", ".join(sorted(valid_dims))
                raise CommandError(
                    f"Invalid dimensions for language '{lang_name}': {', '.join(invalid)}. "
                    f"Valid dimensions: {valid_list}",
                    exit_code=1,
                )
```

The result is the\n    weighted mean of those bottom entries, so bad code penalises\n    proportionally regardless of file boundaries.\n\n    Falls back to ``min()`` when only one entry exists.\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(weighted_scores) <= 1:\n        return min((s for s, _ in weighted_scores), default=fallback)\n\n    total_weight = sum(w for _, w in weighted_scores)\n    if total_weight <= 0:\n        return fallback\n\n    threshold = total_weight * _FLOOR_PERCENTILE\n    sorted_scores = sorted(weighted_scores, key=lambda t: t[0])\n\n    accumulated_weight = 0.0\n    numerator = 0.0\n    for score, weight in sorted_scores:\n        accumulated_weight += weight\n        numerator += score * weight\n        if accumulated_weight >= threshold:\n            break\n\n    if accumulated_weight <= 0:\n        return fallback\n    return numerator / accumulated_weight\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ScoreInputs:"
```json
}
```

> DEVELOPER

Add file-level locking to the state persistence layer to prevent lost updates from concurrent commands.

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py`

**The bug**: State mutations follow a load → modify → save pattern with no locking. Concurrent commands can overwrite each other's changes.

**The fix**: Add a context manager that acquires an exclusive file lock around the read-modify-write cycle.

1. Add a `state_lock` context manager to `persistence.py` that:
   - Creates a lock file at `{state_path}.lock`
   - Uses `fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX)` for exclusive locking
   - Reloads state inside the lock (to get the latest version)
   - Yields the state for mutation
   - Saves the state on clean exit
   - Releases the lock
   - Has a timeout (default 30 seconds) to avoid indefinite blocking

Here's the implementation to add to the end of persistence.py (before the existing code ends):

```python
import contextlib
import fcntl
import time

@contextlib.contextmanager
def state_lock(
    path: Path | None = None,
    *,
    timeout: float = 30.0,
    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None,
):
    """Context manager that locks the state file for exclusive read-modify-write.
    
    Usage:
        with state_lock(state_file) as state:
            # modify state...
            state["issues"]["foo"] = "fixed"
        # state is saved automatically on clean exit
    """
    state_path = path or _default_state_file()
    lock_path = state_path.with_suffix(".json.lock")
    lock_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    
    lock_fd = open(lock_path, "w")
    try:
        deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
        while True:
            try:
                fcntl.flock(lock_fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX | fcntl.LOCK_NB)
                break
            except OSError:
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    lock_fd.close()
                    raise TimeoutError(
                        f"Could not acquire state lock within {timeout}s. "
                        "Another desloppify command may be running."
                    )
                time.sleep(0.1)
        
        # Reload state inside the lock to get the latest version.
        state = load_state(state_path)
        yield state
        save_state(
            state,
            state_path,
            subjective_integrity_target=subjective_integrity_target,
        )
    finally:
        try:
            fcntl.flock(lock_fd, fcntl.LOCK_UN)
        except OSError:
            pass
        lock_fd.close()
```

2. Update `__all__` to include `state_lock`.

3. Also check if there's a Windows-compatible approach needed. Since the project seems macOS/Linux focused (uses os.replace atomically), `fcntl` is fine for now.

```
    65→    """Load state from disk, or return empty state on missing/corruption."""
    66→    state_path = path or _default_state_file()
    67→    if not state_path.exists():
    68→        return empty_state()
    69→
    70→    try:
    71→        data = _load_json(state_path)
    72→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError, ValueError) as ex:
    73→        backup = state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    74→        if backup.exists():
    75→            logger.warning(
    76→                "Primary state load failed for %s; attempting backup %s: %s",
    77→                state_path,
    78→                backup,
    79→                ex,
    80→            )
    81→            try:
    82→                backup_data = _load_json(backup)
    83→                logger.warning(
    84→                    "Recovered state from backup %s after primary load failure at %s",
    85→                    backup,
    86→                    state_path,
    87→                )
    88→                print(
    89→                    f"  ⚠ State file corrupted ({ex}), loaded from backup.",
    90→                    file=sys.stderr,
    91→                )
    92→                return _normalize_loaded_state(backup_data)
    93→            except (
    94→                json.JSONDecodeError,
    95→                UnicodeDecodeError,
    96→                OSError,
    97→                ValueError,
    98→                TypeError,
    99→                AttributeError,
   100→            ) as backup_ex:
   101→                logger.warning(
   102→                    "Backup state load failed from %s after corruption in %s: %s",
   103→                    backup,
   104→                    state_path,
   105→                    backup_ex,
   106→                )
   107→                logger.debug("Backup state load failed from %s: %s", backup, backup_ex)
   108→
   109→        logger.warning(
   110→            "State file load failed for %s and backup recovery was unavailable. "
   111→            "Falling back to empty state: %s",
   112→            state_path,
   113→            ex,
   114→        )
   115→        print(f"  ⚠ State file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
   116→        rename_failed = False
   117→        try:
   118→            state_path.rename(state_path.with_suffix(".json.corrupted"))
   119→        except OSError as rename_ex:
   120→            rename_failed = True
   121→            logger.debug(
   122→                "Failed to rename corrupted state file %s: %s", state_path, rename_ex
   123→            )
   124→        if rename_failed:
   125→            logger.debug(
   126→                "Corrupted state file retained at original path: %s", state_path
   127→            )
   128→        return empty_state()
   129→
   130→    version = data.get("version", 1)
   131→    if version > CURRENT_VERSION:
   132→        print(
   133→            "  ⚠ State file version "
   134→            f"{version} is newer than supported ({CURRENT_VERSION}). "
   135→            "Some features may not work correctly.",
   136→            file=sys.stderr,
   137→        )
   138→
   139→    try:
   140→        return _normalize_loaded_state(data)
   141→    except (ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as normalize_ex:
   142→        logger.warning(
   143→            "State invariants invalid for %s; falling back to empty state: %s",
   144→            state_path,
   145→            normalize_ex,
   146→        )
   147→        print(
   148→            f"  ⚠ State invariants invalid ({normalize_ex}). Starting fresh.",
   149→            file=sys.stderr,
   150→        )
   151→        return empty_state()
   152→
   153→
   154→def _coerce_integrity_target(value: object) -> float | None:
   155→    if not is_numeric(value):
   156→        return None
   157→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value)))
   158→
   159→
   160→def _resolve_integrity_target(
   161→    state: StateModel,
   162→    explicit_target: float | None,
   163→) -> float | None:
   164→    target = _coerce_integrity_target(explicit_target)
   165→    if target is not None:
   166→        return target
   167→
   168→    integrity = state.get("subjective_integrity")
   169→    if not isinstance(integrity, dict):
   170→        return None
   171→    return _coerce_integrity_target(integrity.get("target_score"))
   172→
   173→
   174→def save_state(
   175→    state: StateModel,
   176→    path: Path | None = None,
   177→    *,
   178→    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None,
   179→) -> None:
   180→    """Recompute stats/score and save to disk atomically."""
   181→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   182→    _recompute_stats(
   183→        state,
   184→        scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
   185→        subjective_integrity_target=_resolve_integrity_target(
   186→            state,
   187→            subjective_integrity_target,
   188→        ),
   189→    )
   190→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   191→
   192→    state_path = path or _default_state_file()
   193→    state_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   194→
   195→    content = json.dumps(state, indent=2, default=json_default) + "\n"
   196→
   197→    if state_path.exists():
   198→        backup = state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
   199→        try:
   200→            shutil.copy2(str(state_path), str(backup))
   201→        except OSError as backup_ex:
   202→            logger.debug(
   203→                "Failed to create state backup %s: %s",
   204→                state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak"),
   205→                backup_ex,
   206→            )
   207→
   208→    try:
   209→        safe_write_text(state_path, content)
   210→    except OSError as ex:
   211→        print(f"  Warning: Could not save state: {ex}", file=sys.stderr)
   212→        raise
   213→
```

**Changes made:**

1. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/treesitter/analysis/unused_imports.py`** — Modified `_extract_alias` to first check for Go-style `package_identifier` children before falling back to the `as`-keyword approach. This ensures aliased Go imports like `daysailv1 "github.com/.../v1"` are detected by their alias name (`daysailv1`) rather than the last path segment (`v1`).

2. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_treesitter_complexity_and_integration.py`** — Added two test cases:
   - `test_go_aliased_import_not_false_positive` — verifies a used aliased import is NOT flagged
   - `test_go_aliased_import_unused` — verifies an unused aliased import IS correctly flagged using the alias name

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py",
  "old_string": "    try:\n        safe_write_text(state_path, content)\n    except OSError as ex:\n        print(f\"  Warning: Could not save state: {ex}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        raise",
  "new_string": "    try:\n        safe_write_text(state_path, content)\n    except OSError as ex:\n        print(f\"  Warning: Could not save state: {ex}\", file=sys.stderr)\n        raise\n\n\n@contextlib.contextmanager\ndef state_lock(\n    path: Path | None = None,\n    *,\n    timeout: float = 30.0,\n    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None,\n) -> Generator[StateModel, None, None]:\n    \"\"\"Context manager that locks the state file for exclusive read-modify-write.\n\n    Acquires an exclusive file lock, reloads state from disk (to pick up the\n    latest version), yields it for mutation, then saves on clean exit.\n\n    Usage::\n\n        with state_lock(state_file) as state:\n            state[\"issues\"][\"foo\"] = \"fixed\"\n        # state is saved automatically on clean exit\n    \"\"\"\n    state_path = path or _default_state_file()\n    lock_path = state_path.with_suffix(\".json.lock\")\n    lock_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    lock_fd = open(lock_path, \"w\")  # noqa: SIM115\n    try:\n        deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout\n        while True:\n            try:\n                fcntl.flock(lock_fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX | fcntl.LOCK_NB)\n                break\n            except OSError:\n                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:\n                    lock_fd.close()\n                    raise TimeoutError(\n                        f\"Could not acquire state lock within {timeout}s. \"\n                        \"Another desloppify command may be running.\"\n                    )\n                time.sleep(0.1)\n\n        # Reload state inside the lock to get the latest version.\n        state = load_state(state_path)\n        yield state\n        save_state(\n            state,\n            state_path,\n            subjective_integrity_target=subjective_integrity_target,\n        )\n    finally:\n        try:\n            fcntl.flock(lock_fd, fcntl.LOCK_UN)\n        except OSError:\n            pass\n        lock_fd.close()"
}
```
